Yes, if he could never become a Demon Lord, then killing them both and dying was the answer.

Since he had never possessed any intention of achieving something through his own effort, the end of this abandoned desire was always death. He simply lacked the strength to kill and thus could not act on it.

At the end of this despair, someone approached him.

It was precisely this person currently sending him a telepathic message.

—Have you met the Soul-Stealing Demon Lord?

Surprisingly, the sender of the message was the nursery's female owner. Saujong's reason for frequenting this place was not to soothe his anguish. He was not so weak as to drift through flower gardens to forget his pain—he was a man who would kill or be killed.

—I did as you instructed, but she did not try to kill me.

Saujong was exceedingly deferential. He was neither one to easily trust others nor one to lower his head. Yet this woman, who had approached him during his most agonizing hours, had recruited him in an instant.

She was a woman who dared target the Soul-Stealing Demon Lord of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, yet she had given him the conviction that she could see it through. Saujong had already experienced the fear hidden beneath her ordinary exterior.

Thus he was entangled with three women. He thought: it was his destiny to succeed only through women.

—Meet the Soul-Stealing Demon Lord again and provoke her. You must make her use the Soul Subjugation Technique to kill you.

—I will do so.

Saujong was no man to die meekly, so he had prepared a single contingency against the woman's words.

—The moment the Soul-Stealing Demon Lord uses the Soul Subjugation Technique, you must activate the Yin-Yang Reverse Soul Technique.

Yin-Yang Reverse Soul Technique.

A secret technique from the distant beyond, said to have been lost to practical application long ago, it was a secret art capable of blocking the opponent's Soul Subjugation Technique and reversing it onto them. However, it only worked on a partner with whom one had shared a bed.

That was why he had been chosen for this task. He had no interest in the woman's true intentions. Whether he turned the Soul-Stealing Demon Lord struck by the Yin-Yang Reverse Soul Technique into a puppet to kill the Cult Leader or the Young Cult Lord, it mattered not. Saujong's heart held only one thought.

—You must keep your promise.

At Saujong's repeated confirmation, the woman paused while moving a sapling as large as her torso and straightened her back. Wiping the sweat from her brow, she sent a telepathic message. It was a reward Saujong could never refuse.

—If you carry out this task, you will become the Sword Lord, and the Ilhwa Sword Lord will become yours.

Chapter 306: What kind of Demon Venerable believes in superstition?
-------------------------------------------------------------------
After leaving the Thousand Poison Forest, the place I headed for was the Great Drunken Forest.

As always, Yeobin of the Three Great Drunkards guided me to the Drunken Demon.

As we crossed the lake by boat, I asked her.

“Does the Drunken Demon still drink a lot these days?”

“He cut down quite a bit for a while, but lately he seems to be drinking heavily again.”

The look in her eyes was hoping I would try to stop him. Yeobin was the only woman who liked the Drunken Demon and sincerely cared for him.

We arrived at the island and went up to the Drunken Dream Pavilion.

The Drunken Demon was already drunk.

“Still, how could you really come to me last? I’m last among the Demon Supremes, aren’t I? Otherwise, this makes no sense.”

The Drunken Demon made no attempt to hide his hurt feelings.

“I have to meet you last, brother, so I can drink comfortably.”

“Nice excuse.”

“Come on, brother, be understanding. It’s been a tiring day.”

I sat down comfortably in front of the Drunken Demon with my legs stretched out.

“You know what? I’m most at ease when I’m with you.”

“You mean I’m easy to deal with!”

“Is that it?”

Shaking his head as if I was hopeless, the Drunken Demon poured me a drink. Before I knew it, his expression had softened. Rather than a long-winded excuse, sometimes a single “Is that it?” could prove far more powerful.

We drank together.

As the strong liquor slid down my throat, my insides burned. It was stronger than the alcohol the Drunken Demon used to drink before.

“They said you’d cut down, but you’ve been drinking even stronger liquor.”

“If it’s too strong, drink this.”

The Drunken Demon brought over another bottle from the ones piled up in the pavilion and poured it for me. Then he drank the strong liquor he had been drinking before.

What on earth was troubling him?

I was curious, but I did not ask first. There are only two ways to hear the truth from a drunkard. Make him sober up, or make him drink more.

“Here, another cup!”

Whatever his worry was, drinking with the Drunken Demon after so long put me in a good mood.

“The atmosphere is incredible.”

Tonight, the thick water mist and the moon glowing hazily among the clouds heightened the mood even more.

“Even when I was away, whenever I thought of drinking, this place always came to mind. Is brother drinking? Is he floating around in the lake?”

“It’s cold now, so I don’t go into the water much.”

“Time really flies.”

It had been spring just the other day, and now it was already cold. For a while, we each fell into our own thoughts and drank. The Drunken Demon was definitely drinking more than usual.

“Lady Yeo was very worried that you’ve been drinking too much.”

“Yeobin worries every day.”

“Be good to her while she worries about you. Don’t regret it later.”

“Regret?”

The Drunken Demon gave me a look asking what I meant, but I did not answer. Why ask when he already knew? It might seem as though he did nothing but drink all day, but the Drunken Demon was a delicate and sensitive person. How could he not know that Yeobin liked him as a man? He was probably just pretending not to know.

Come to think of it, not a single one of the Demon Supremes had formed a family. At least the Fist Demon had recently gained a daughter. The rest were all living alone like this.

Was it because, as the Blood Heaven Blade Demon always said, family was an enemy? Or because family was a weakness?

“Come with me for a moment. There’s somewhere we need to go.”

The Drunken Demon put me on a boat.

He rowed to the lakeside opposite the entrance and moored the boat. After getting off, the Drunken Demon walked into the forest. I always came only to the Drunken Dream Pavilion, so I had forgotten that the Great Drunken Forest was also an extremely vast place.

After walking for quite a while, the surroundings became shrouded in mist. Something felt strange, so I examined it and realized it was not mist.

It was alcohol qi. It was the first time I had ever seen alcohol qi spread out like fog.

“If someone is exposed to this place, they become drunk and collapse. It isn’t alcohol qi that can be drawn out with inner force either, so ordinary people can never pass through. Stay close behind me and follow.”

As the Drunken Demon walked, the alcohol qi parted and formed a path.

As though it were alive, once we passed, the alcohol qi that had parted joined back together and the path disappeared.

We could not run through with lightness skill either. The alcohol qi blanketed the area like fog, making it impossible to see ahead, and the path was as complicated as a maze. In the end, this road was a forbidden ground of the Great Drunken Forest that only the Drunken Demon could traverse.

After we made it through the maze, the alcohol qi finally cleared.

A single building stood before us.

“This is where generations of Great Drunken Demons brewed and drank their alcohol.”

I followed him inside. The place was arranged like a brewery. The facilities looked old and worn, but every single one of them seemed extraordinary. In display cabinets along the walls were stored the liquors favored by the previous Drunken Demons.

“As for me, I like drinking all kinds of alcohol, so I don’t drink much of the liquor here. Besides, though I have talent for drinking, I don’t have much talent for brewing. So I come here sometimes to rest.”

In the center of the brewery was a strangely shaped wine cup.

Looking down at it, the Drunken Demon said with a grave expression.

“Not long ago, the wine essence spoiled.”

Indeed, a rotten smell was coming from it.

“Since I became a Demon Supreme, this has never happened before.”

The Drunken Demon added with a dark expression.

“It’s ominous.”

Only now did I understand why the Drunken Demon had seemed so troubled.

“Do you think I’m going to die?”

“I guess you don’t want to die.”

“There’s still so much alcohol I haven’t drunk yet.”

“Don’t worry. If you die, I’ll buy every kind of liquor in the Central Plains and pour it over your grave.”

The Drunken Demon let out a sigh. In his heart, which had sensed ill omen, flocks of birds must have taken flight and herds of animals begun to run before an earthquake. Truly, I had never imagined he would be so serious over something like this.

“What kind of Demon Supreme believes in superstition?”

“My master once said something like that. If the wine essence spoils, something terrible will happen. Either something will happen to the Great Drunken Demon, or something will happen to our cult. Of course, I didn’t believe it.”

But now that the wine essence had actually spoiled, he seemed unsettled.

“If it bothers you that much, tell Father.”

“No! I’ve only just cleared up the misunderstanding with the Cult Leader.”

Originally, the Drunken Demon had misunderstood that Father disliked him. But after learning that it was not him Father disliked, but his master, the misunderstanding had been resolved.

“I’ve even been giving the Cult Leader alcohol as gifts. We’ve been getting along well all this time. But if he hears that the wine essence has spoiled, won’t it leave a bad taste in his mouth for no reason? ‘Of course. Like master, like disciple. Unlucky drunkards, the lot of them.’”

It made me wonder why he was worrying over even something like that, but that was how delicate the Drunken Demon was. At least when it came to his relationship with Father, even more so.

“Father will dismiss it as superstition and drink. That’s the kind of man he is.”

I examined the cup containing the wine essence and asked.

“How do we clean this up? Don’t try to scare me by saying I broke it by accident and it was a treasure of the Great Drunken Forest passed down for a thousand years.”

“Why? Are you going to clean it now? Forget it. I’ll do it later.”

“Why later? You’ll clean it while thinking every gloomy and terrible thought by yourself. Clean it up quickly while I’m here.”

Together with the Drunken Demon, I emptied the spoiled wine essence from the cup.

While I drew water and washed the cup, the Drunken Demon prepared to brew new liquor.

As I washed the cup, I said to him.

“It didn’t spoil because of you, brother. There’s someone trying to disturb our cult these days. It must have spoiled because of that unlucky bastard.”

“You don’t have to make things up just to comfort me.”

“I’m not making it up. I’ve even mobilized the Yellow Springs Pavilion and the Heaven-Piercing Pavilion to investigate him.”

“There really is a bastard like that?”

“There is. I guess I can’t look down on my brother. You even guessed something like this.”

Even so, the Drunken Demon’s expression did not relax. I could not just stand by and watch a Demon Supreme get devoured by superstition.

“Brother, do you think a person’s future is set in stone?”

“To some degree.”

“I don’t. Depending on what I say to you right now, my relationship with you changes immediately. So what exactly is set? I think the wine essence spoiled because fate was trying to make you bring me here. If that hadn’t happened, I would never have known that a place like this existed. How can anyone know the affairs of the world? Someday, I might fall into danger and end up fleeing here. This isn’t an ominous warning, but a great message of destiny meant to save your younger brother’s life.”

“You really do have a talent for forcing things to fit.”

He laughed in disbelief, but thanks to that, much of his ominous mood seemed to ease. The Drunken Demon’s expression brightened.

“You waited for me so eagerly because you wanted to hear something like this, didn’t you?”

The Drunken Demon readily admitted it.

“It was more than I expected.”

“Please continue to make frequent use of me in the future, customer!”

As I placed the clean wine cup back where it had been, I said.

“The price for using my services is one cup of alcohol and one superstition-loving big brother.”

* * *

When I returned to my quarters, the night-shift guards greeted me warmly.

“You look tired.”

After returning from the Gangseo Branch, I had met people almost without rest until now. And it was not as if they were ordinary people.

“I’ll be resting from now on, so don’t disturb me.”

I entered my room as though I would throw myself onto the bed at once, but instead of sleeping, I opened the Space-Time Transposition Art.

My way of dealing with ominous feelings? Training.

If something ominous happens, I simply erase it. I endure by becoming someone even misfortune cannot do anything about. I’m working this hard to endure. Misfortune, what can the likes of you do?

Within the Space-Time Transposition Art, I once again activated the Secret Art of Time and made time flow differently.

Through painstaking effort, I had managed to obtain twice as much time as the outside world. I did not hope for infinite time. The final goal of the Secret Art of Time was exactly a tenfold difference in time. Just enough time to reach the twelfth-star great completion of the Nine Calamities Demonic Art, please.

Today’s training was the Nine Calamities Demonic Art. Until now, I had repeatedly trained only the first form, but I instinctively felt that the time had come to learn the second form. Such was the level of martial attainment I had reached.

Nine Calamities Demonic Art, Second Form.

Great Annihilation Form.

If the first form, the Human Annihilation Form, was an attack aimed at a single person, the second form, the Great Annihilation Form, was a technique for facing many opponents.

When I executed the second form, one evil spirit silently appeared before me with its back turned.

It was the most terrifying-looking of the four evil spirits that appeared in the first form.

For an instant, its body seemed to tremble slightly.

Srrrk.

The evil spirit split apart. The number it split into was three in total.

Kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa!

The three evil spirits thrust out their swords and swept forward.

Nothing remained in the place they passed through. It was an attack that swept away whatever stood there.

The higher my mastery of the second form rose, the greater the number of splits would become.

Later, it would become a technique that split into dozens and swept away everything visible before my eyes.

Of course, the amount of inner force consumed would also increase according to the number of splits.

How many can Father split into?

I will surpass the number Father can create.

Just what is my limit?

When I imagined the evil spirits sweeping through everything before my eyes, my chest surged with excitement.

Once that image came to mind, I could not sleep.

I devoted myself to training the second form until morning, and by the time the sun rose, I was nearly exhausted.

Now it truly was time to rest.

I released the Secret Art of Time and activated the Space-Time Transposition Art again. My most cherished space unfolded.

A hot sun, a blue sea, a pure white sandy beach, and a comfortable chair beneath a broad-leafed tree.

It had truly been a long time. I had deliberately saved it. For a day like this.

I sank heavily into the chair as though lying down.

Looking at the blue sea and sky, the same color as my sword aura, put my heart at ease.

Whether the wine essence had spoiled, whether Sa Ujong was acting up, whether there was someone behind him. At least for this moment, I forgot it all and rested.

I placed the secret coffer from my robes beside me. I had drawn eyes and a mouth on it like a person’s face. It was a brightly smiling expression.

Though I had said I would tell it “open, open” every day until I forced it open, ever since obtaining the secret coffer, I had never actually said the word open to it.

“You rest too. For all those long years, people must have urged you to open and open. How exhausting must that have been? You rest, and I’ll rest too.”

Sleep washed over me. The demon of sleep guided me to a being I had not seen in a long time.

In my dream, I was standing in the sky.

Above a sea that resembled the heavens, within my own aura.

Then a shadow fell, and the world darkened. When I turned around, something enormous was blocking the sun. At first, I thought it was a tidal wave as high as a fortress wall, but it was not.

The Heavenly Demon Soul.

The thing that had shown itself to me several times had appeared in my dream. The Heavenly Demon Soul was looking down at me with its arms crossed. Because the sun was behind it, I could not see its face clearly.

But I knew instinctively. This was my Heavenly Demon Soul.

I stared piercingly at the Heavenly Demon Soul, wanting to see its face. But at this moment, even the Divine Eye Art did not work.

“Do you also want me to reach you more quickly?”

The Heavenly Demon Soul merely looked down at me in silence.

* * *

Three days later, I received the first report in Seo Daeryong’s office.

“Even after thoroughly examining Sa Ujong’s financial situation, we found nothing particularly suspicious.”

“No trace of money having changed hands?”

“No. Judging by his habits of saving and spending, he is not someone with much interest in money.”

Seo Daeryong asked cautiously.

“Could he really have been trying to die at the hands of the Soul-Capturing Demon Supreme?”

I shook my head.

“That would actually be an even worse case. What you’re saying means he was moved by something other than money, doesn’t it? If he were a chivalrous hero, he might have acted for a great cause, but he isn’t that kind of person.”

“Young Cult Leader, you believe there is someone behind him.”

“I’m keeping several possibilities in mind.”

Considering Baek Cheongyeong of the Mok Cheon Family, who had infiltrated the Martial Alliance, I could not guarantee that our cult had no such person. From now on, in any incident that occurred, I had to always keep in mind the possibility of someone behind it.

“This is the record of Sa Ujong’s daily routine.”

I calmly examined the day written on the paper. In a daily life no different from that of ordinary martial artists, one thing caught my eye.

“What’s this flower garden?”

“They say he’s been going to that flower garden often lately. He goes there and sits blankly for a while before returning. Since he was someone who helped the One Flower Sword Supreme tend flower gardens, it probably isn’t strange for him to visit one.”

“No, it is strange, isn’t it? If it were me, I wouldn’t even want to look at flowers. If he had instead gone off out of nowhere to learn the geomungo, I wouldn’t have suspected him. But he regularly visited a place everyone naturally accepted without suspicion?”

“Now that you put it that way, it does seem suspicious. Ah, I’m truly glad you aren’t my classmate, Young Cult Leader. I would never have entered at the top of the class.”

I laughed at his banter and rose from my seat.

Since it was obvious where I was going now, Seo Daeryong looked at the flowerpot by the window and said.

“Number Three, looks like you’re going to make a friend.”

Chapter 307: Why Are the Flowers Entrusted to Me.
-------------------------------------------------
For a natural visit to the flower garden, I needed one person’s help.

“Are you moving?”

Amid the clothes and luggage filling the room, Ian lifted her head.

“You’re here?”

Ian was in the midst of preparing to leave the cult. She was about to set out to meet Seojin, the younger sister of the Ghost Spirit.

“There’s so much to prepare for a long journey. Clothes to change into, the uniform I’ll wear when I meet Lady Seo, food in case I get hungry on the way, Golden Wound Medicine and antidotes are packed, I won’t need medicine for getting sick from unfamiliar water, right? I packed a map too. And…”

“What are these?”

“Ah, those are bowls in case I end up camping, and these are seasonings. And this is leather to spread on the ground when I sleep.”

I took all the bowls out of her leather pouch.

“Don’t cook anything that requires bowls. Take only salt for seasoning. What’s this?”

“A book to read when I can’t sleep.”

“When have you ever read books like that! Pick just one!”

I helped her pack. No, I helped her unpack.

“When are you leaving?”

“Tomorrow morning. I was going to come say goodbye to you anyway.”

“You’re going alone?”

“Yes. Woman to woman, I’m going to try persuading her.”

If Seojin, a descendant of the Ghost Gate, joined the Ghost Shadow Squad, its strength would improve greatly. She was skilled not only in formations, but also in ghost arts.

“No matter how much luggage you have, you can at least take this, right? Here, try wearing it.”

I held out what I had brought to her. Small daggers were densely fitted into a leather belt worn around the waist.

“These are daggers I specially asked Craftsman Gwak of the Iron Workshop to make. Use them when you’re in danger. It’s a gift for leaving the cult.”

“Ah! Thank you, Young Master!”

Ian was deeply moved by the unexpected gift.

After wrapping the leather belt around her waist, she swiftly drew daggers and threw them at the wall.

Four daggers stuck into the wall. If there had been a human-shaped drawing there, they would have been at the arms and legs.

I drew one dagger from her belt and threw it at the wall.

The dagger lodged in the neck.

“Always here.”

It was advice to harden her heart when facing an enemy.

“Remember this. The clumsy mercy I show may cause someone else to die.”

Ian nodded once, then threw another dagger.

It stuck right beside the one I had thrown.

I nodded to her with a look of approval.

Ian retrieved the daggers embedded in the wall and fastened them back at her waist.

“I really like them! Thank you, Young Master.”

“No need to treasure them too much. If you use them all, I’ll have more made for you.”

“I’ll go and come back safely. Don’t worry.”

“I’m not worried. Why would I worry about the direct subordinate captain of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s Young Cult Leader, and the daughter of the Fist Demon? In that sense, shall we take a short walk?”

“As you can see, I’m busy. Please pick that up over there. And bring me the clothes hanging there too.”

“A woman rejecting the Young Cult Leader of the Demon Cult!”

Of course, Ian’s pretending to be busy was just a joke.

“Even if you asked me to take a walk all the way beyond the borders, I’d have to go.”

She came to my side and was about to link arms with me, then flinched. In the past, she would get piggyback rides and link arms freely, but after I became Young Cult Leader, it seemed she found it difficult to treat me so casually.

When she and I left the residence, the guards waiting outside greeted her.

Ian greeted them as well. Since they were the two organizations closest to me, they were conscious of each other, whether they showed it or not. To be precise, Jeok Yeon, the head of the guards, was the one who paid a bit more attention.

When the guards followed behind us, Ian showed a faintly puzzled expression.

It had been a long time since the two of us had time alone, so she probably had not expected me to bring the guards along.

I felt sorry for not telling her in advance, but today’s outing was closer to official business than personal.

We left the main sect like that and entered Maga Village.

It felt good to walk with Ian after so long. We looked at goods being sold in the market and chatted about this and that until we arrived at the Pungryu Tavern. When I passed it by, Ian asked in surprise.

“Huh? Aren’t we going to Pungryu Tavern?”

“No.”

Since we had come out to Maga Village, she must have naturally assumed we were going to Pungryu Tavern.

We continued walking along that road and arrived at the flower garden on the outskirts that had been in Seo Daeryong’s report.

“Buy a flower. One that blooms well all through winter.”

“No way. Did you come here to buy me flowers?”

When I nodded, she looked even happier than when she had received the daggers.

“It’s my first time getting flowers as a gift!”

Before entering with her, the guards went in first and searched the interior.

A woman with a generous-looking face, apparently the owner, came out from the backyard of the flower garden holding a flowerpot.

The moment I saw her, startled and setting down the pot, I thought,

‘She looks familiar.’

She was definitely a woman I had seen somewhere. But I could not remember.

Of course, it would not be because I remembered buying flowers from her.

‘There’s something here.’

I examined her aura. If I had glanced at her absentmindedly, I would not have realized she was a martial artist. That was how well she concealed her aura.

Then I had hit the mark.

Sa Ujong must have visited this place every time because of that woman. If it had simply been a contact point, he would have come only once or twice, but the report said he had come often, almost every day. If so, he must have received something from that woman.

“Madam, please recommend a flower that would suit this beautiful young lady.”

At that, the middle-aged woman bowed politely and said,

“There are flowers befitting one so precious and beautiful. Now, please come this way.”

She led Ian over and introduced the flowers. Judging by her extensive knowledge of flowers, anyone would have taken her for the owner of the garden.

‘If she is a master capable of disguising herself to this degree, I should be able to remember her.’

Feeling puzzled, I looked around the flower garden. In Baek Cheongyeong’s workshop before, I had sensed mysterious energy from the dolls. But here, they were ordinary flowers and trees.

She was not one of the Twelve Earthly Branch Kings who had followed Hwa Mugi.

Then was she someone I had seen during my days as a wandering warrior? Or had I seen her while searching for other materials for the Grand Art?

I even examined her build. If not her face, perhaps her body or gait might remind me of someone, but still no one came to mind.

In the meantime, Ian chose a flower.

“She says this flowering tree lives the longest. A really pretty flower will bloom from this bud.”

Then she flinched in surprise.

“Ah! Come to think of it, I have to leave the cult tomorrow, don’t I?”

I answered with a smile.

“Do we not have a flower-growing expert?”

Guessing who I meant, Ian smiled in relief.

I paid the middle-aged woman for the flower.

She might find my sudden visit suspicious.

But she would also be confused. Because I had brought Ian here without saying anything to her, Ian was not acting; she was genuinely happy.

I had also deliberately brought the guards along. Since one would not come so grandly when investigating something, it would give her all sorts of things to think about.

Hey, who are you? If this is the first time I’ve seen you, why do you look so familiar?

* * *

The next day, when I stopped by Seo Daeryong’s office, he was watering a flowerpot. Now there were two flowerpots by the window.

“This is the flowerpot that Warrior Ian entrusted to me.”

Seo Daeryong put on an exaggerated air.

“Do you know what Warrior Ian said when she left this with me?”

“If you kill the flower, I won’t let you off?”

“It would have been better if she had. I could at least have protested, saying what kind of threat is that when she’s asking a favor.”

Seo Daeryong imitated Ian.

“Ah! Look at this flower bud. It’ll be so pretty when it blooms, won’t it? I’m really, really looking forward to it. It’s the first flower I’ve ever received from the Young Master. By the time I come back, it’ll have bloomed. Right?”

Seo Daeryong shook his head.

“You should have seen her eyes then. Those eyes sparkling with expectation, with tears glimmering in them. Ah! How am I supposed to let this flower die?”

Seo Daeryong had been thoroughly caught by Ian’s move, which was far scarier than a threat.

“Why are all the flowers entrusted to me ones that absolutely must not die?”

After sighing, Seo Daeryong looked at the flowerpot and said,

“Grow well here beside your elder brother.”

“Why elder brother?”

“Because they look alike. The flowering tree that the Sword Venerable gave me…”

The next moment, I burst out of Seo Daeryong’s office and ran.

I remembered who she was.

Hwan Yeo!

I had never seen her. And yet I had mistaken her for someone familiar because I knew someone who looked like her.

Among the Twelve Earthly Branch Kings who followed Hwa Mugi, the Fifth King.

The absolute master of illusions and Soul-Capturing Art, the Illusion King.

Hwan Yeo and the Illusion King were twins.

The fact that the Illusion King had an older twin sister was only revealed long after he had become active as one of the Twelve Earthly Branch Kings.

The reason the Illusion King had advanced so smoothly and risen to become one of the Twelve Earthly Branch Kings was because of Hwan Yeo, who operated in secret.

Moreover, the Illusion King and Hwan Yeo were famous for being terrifying and vicious. They mercilessly killed anyone who hindered their aims. They were cold-blooded people without blood or tears, using Soul-Capturing Art to make parents kill children, wives kill husbands, and friends kill friends. That was why everyone feared them.

‘The person Hwan Yeo is after is the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable!’

* * *

“The Sword Venerable has a secret message for you.”

Seeing the Demon Sword from the Northern Heaven Sword Clan, Cheongseon sighed. The person bowing his head in greeting was none other than Sa Ujong.

When she had refused to meet him, he had come openly under the pretext of running an errand for the Sword Venerable.

“Follow me.”

Cheongseon dismissed her subordinates and led Sa Ujong to the reception hall.

Once only the two of them remained, Cheongseon’s expression hardened coldly.

“Don’t you know that falsely invoking a Demon Venerable’s order is also a grave crime? If the Sword Venerable finds out, she won’t let you off.”

“Then go tell the Sword Venerable.”

Sa Ujong acted like an immature brat twisted by heartbreak.

“Are you really going to be like this? If you’re a man, you should know how to give up like one.”

“That’s rich coming from you, kicking a man aside just because you rose to the seat of Demon Venerable.”

“You think I abandoned you because I became a Demon Venerable?”

A sneer as cold as the look in her eyes crossed Cheongseon’s lips.

“You have someone else you like besides me, don’t you?”

She had belatedly realized that Sa Ujong was in love with the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

“You thought I wouldn’t know you were thinking of that woman when you slept with me?”

As a woman, it wounded her pride so much she had not even wanted to say it aloud.

In truth, at first, she had not known in her wildest dreams. No matter how outstanding her appearance was, Cheongseon had never imagined he would love the Ilhwa Sword Venerable, who was old enough to be his parent, as a woman.

Sometimes he would gaze at the painting on the wall with heated eyes, but she had thought it was respect for the Sword Venerable.

But once the veil over her eyes was removed, she realized those were the eyes of a man looking at a woman. He had even looked at the Sword Venerable’s portrait while sleeping with her. Thinking of that made her want to kill him right here and now.

But since she had entrusted this matter to Geom Mugeuk, she had to soothe him properly and send him away.

“You should be grateful I let you live. Go back now.”

Sa Ujong needled her nerves until the end.

“It can’t be helped, can it? The Sword Venerable is more attractive. Any man would feel the same, I bet.”

Cheongseon clenched her fists tightly. What made her even angrier was that he thought she would not be able to kill him even if he said such things so carelessly.

“Do you know? Surprisingly, the Sword Venerable’s skin was better too.”

Sa Ujong chose the method that would infuriate someone the most. He compared, and compared again.

“Stop. If you don’t want to die.”

Cheongseon endured to the end. But Sa Ujong even fabricated things that had never happened as he provoked her.

“The Sword Venerable smells good. Is it because she practices arcane arts? I didn’t say it, but you had a foul smell. You wouldn’t know, but there were times I had to hold my nose while…”

Cheongseon whirled around. Both her eyes had turned black, even the whites.

Kiiiiiiiiii.

With a bizarre sound, pitch-black energy poured from Cheongseon’s eyes.

Haaaaaaa.

At the same time, red energy poured from Sa Ujong’s eyes. If ghostly wails sounded from Cheongseon’s Soul-Capturing Art, then from the Yin-Yang Reverse Soul Art rang a sound like the moans of a man and woman.

The red energy coiled around the incoming black energy like a snake and flew toward Cheongseon’s eyes. A single chance granted only to one who had shared a bed with the target.

‘I did it.’

When the red energy flew back along the black energy and burrowed into her eyes, Sa Ujong was so delighted he felt he might soar. He imagined the moment when he became a Demon Venerable and made the Ilhwa Sword Venerable his woman.

Right then!

‘!’

Sa Ujong’s eyes flew wide open.

Regardless of his will, his right hand had begun drawing his sword.

‘What? No!’

The woman at the flower garden had clearly said it. The moment the Yin-Yang Reverse Soul Art took hold, the Soul-Capturing Art Cheongseon had placed on him would be broken.

‘The Yin-Yang Reverse Soul Art definitely took hold properly, didn’t it?’

Even at this moment, the red energy was taking over both her eyes, pushing out her black energy.

But Cheongseon’s Soul-Capturing Art still controlled his body.

Srrrng.

With the sword he had drawn, he pointed it at his own neck.

Only then did Sa Ujong realize.

‘She deceived me.’

He realized too late that he could not break the opponent’s Soul-Capturing Art. He had been so careful and suspicious. Yet as if bewitched by something, he had been deceived by her.

‘Could it be… me too?’

The very moment suspicion arose that the woman from the flower garden might have planted some suggestion in him as well—

Slash!

He cut his own throat with the sword.

At that very moment, Geom Mugeuk rushed into the reception hall.

Sa Ujong was collapsing, a fountain of blood spraying from his neck.

Beyond him, Cheongseon came into view.

The red energy of the Yin-Yang Reverse Soul Art had now completely driven the black energy out of her pupils. The red energy that had filled her eyes gradually shrank until it became as small as a dot, and soon her eyes began returning to their usual state.

Geom Mugeuk could tell.

‘Cheongseon has fallen to Soul-Capturing Art.’

Originally, their target must have been the previous Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.

But because I killed the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable in this life, their target must naturally have changed to Cheongseon. Now I knew for certain.

‘Before my regression, the traitor was the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.’

Chapter 308: Without Even Knowing the Heart of the Person Beside You.
---------------------------------------------------------------------
I thought it was fortunate that the traitor was the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.

What if one of the other Demon Venerables had been the traitor? Even if he were living a different life in this one, I would not have been able to look at him with an easy heart.

Now I could infer one fact.

The Murim Alliance had Baek Cheongyeong, and our cult had the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, so there must be someone plotting in the Unorthodox Alliance as well.

If anything, I felt hope. Until now, Hwa Mugi had felt like a natural disaster. An absolute existence that no human could stop. But now I knew those events were the result of meticulous preparations made over long years.

Cheongseon’s eyes returned completely to normal.

Having come back to her senses, she found Sa Ujong’s corpse before her and let out a sigh.

Then, seeing that I was there, she flinched in surprise.

“Young Cult Leader?”

Soon she began to reproach herself. She merely did not know she had fallen under the soul-capturing art; in every other respect, she was normal. She clearly remembered being unable to restrain herself and using the soul-capturing art on Sa Ujong.

“I fell for his provocation.”

As if she had not meant to kill him like this, Cheongseon looked down at the corpse with complicated eyes. Though brief, they had once shared a bed, and now it had ended in such ruin.

“I have no face to show you, Young Cult Leader.”

Her voice trembled slightly. She seemed terribly sorry and ashamed that, after saying she would leave it to me, she had ended up killing Sa Ujong.

“I truly can’t understand it. That man acted as if he wanted to die by my hand. He was like that last time too, but today he seemed completely determined.”

Since what was done was done, I consoled her.

“It seems he wanted to die at the hands of a Demon Venerable. If he was determined to die and provoked you, there was nothing to be done.”

“No. No matter what he said, I should have endured it better.”

She was genuinely regretting it. Yes, if she was to live as a Demon Venerable from now on, she needed to be able to control her emotions in the face of any provocation. This incident would be a great lesson for her.

“He was someone who deserved to die. However, Sa Ujong was a martial artist belonging to the Northern Heaven Sword Clan, so this incident cannot simply be buried as it is.”

Worry flashed across Cheongseon’s face. If this matter came to light, she would become the subject of gossip.

“Demon Venerable.”

“Yes, Young Cult Leader.”

“Can you trust me to the very end?”

Cheongseon’s eyes trembled faintly. There is nothing that gives a person greater strength than having someone to rely on when they are driven into a corner. She nodded.

“Leave what comes next to me, and return for now. I will have the Yellow Springs Pavilion handle the corpse.”

Cheongseon, who had been about to turn away, asked me,

“But how did you know to come here?”

I could not tell her about Hwanyeo.

“I was secretly inspecting Sa Ujong. When I received a report that he had gone to meet the Demon Venerable, I came to see what on earth he intended by meeting you.”

“I see. I am ashamed to say this, but I will entrust the aftermath to you, Young Cult Leader.”

Cheongseon cupped her fist respectfully, then left.

Left alone, I looked down at Sa Ujong’s corpse. He had said he knew the weakness of the One Flower Sword Venerable, but that secret had been buried forever with his death.

Hwanyeo.

As expected, her nature was no different from before my regression. She had used him thoroughly, then disposed of him.

If Hwanyeo is in the flower garden, where is Hwanwang? Is he somewhere nearby? Or elsewhere?

* * *

After leaving the reception hall, I returned straight to the Yellow Springs Pavilion.

Because I had suddenly rushed out in the middle of our conversation, Seo Daeryong was wondering what had happened. If Seo Daeryong had not brought up the story of the flowerpot, I would not have thought of Hwanyeo.

“Sa Ujong is dead.”

Seo Daeryong was startled. He had just reported on the contents of his surveillance of him, so hearing that the man had died in that short span would naturally shock him.

“As expected! Whenever you rush out, Young Cult Leader, wicked men inevitably die.”

At his half-joking, half-serious words, I shook my head.

“It wasn’t me this time.”

“Then who killed him?”

“The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.”

Seo Daeryong asked in surprise,

“What on earth happened?”

I told Seo Daeryong only that the one behind this incident was the owner of the flower garden.

“That woman used Sa Ujong to cast the soul-capturing art on the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.”

“Is that possible? Ah, of course the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable is still young.”

“Even so, it is no easy feat. That is how formidable our opponents are.”

Seo Daeryong’s expression grew grave. This was a conspiracy aimed at none other than a Demon Venerable.

“Do you know who the woman in the flower garden is?”

Even to Seo Daeryong, I could not reveal that her identity was Hwanyeo, so I shook my head.

“Stop the special inspection and put the flower garden under surveillance. Quietly, using skilled people. They are people who plot against Demon Venerables. Have them watch only the larger movements from afar. And send the enforcement martial artists to retrieve Sa Ujong’s corpse.”

“Understood.”

I could not predict how Hwanyeo would act. Since she had cast the soul-capturing art, I did not know whether she would leave this place for now, or remain nearby continuously to manage the soul-capturing art placed on the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.

“What do you intend to do now?”

This incident could not be resolved by me and the Yellow Springs Pavilion alone. The soul-capturing art merely did not work on me; I could not undo the soul-capturing art placed on someone else.

“I must send a message telling him to return at once.”

“To whom?”

“To the only person who can resolve this matter.”

He was the only person, but when he came, two people would come.

* * *

“I have lost.”

The old man admitted defeat. He was someone reputed to be skilled at go in the vicinity. About a month ago, a young man had come seeking instruction, so he had accepted payment for each game and taught him.

At first there had been such a gap in skill that they played handicap games, but each time they played, the young man improved, until before long he had defeated him. His skill had now risen to an unbelievable degree.

“How did your ability improve so quickly? Did you perhaps deceive me from the beginning?”

The person who had learned go from the old man was Gowol. He smiled, placed the lesson fee beside the go board, and rose from his seat.

“I have learned much from you.”

It was a final farewell, implying he would not come to learn anymore.

“Let us play just one more game!”

But the old man had already lost three games in a row.

“Please don’t be too upset. It is thanks to how well you taught me, elder.”

After bowing politely, Gowol left.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader, who had been watching from the side, said a word to the old man.

“That’s right. That man was a go master from the start. He’s a sinister fellow beyond compare, so he deceived you from the very beginning. He wanted to trick you, elder, and show you him turning the tables.”

But the old man did not agree with him at all. The sparks of defeat flew toward the Pungcheon Cult Leader instead.

“You look more sinister to me.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader squatted down beside the old man.

“Now, now. Perhaps because you only play go, you lack an eye for people. I am a man who has never once in my life been called sinister.”

“You look so sinister to begin with that everyone must have been cautious around you. You likely never had the chance to be sinister.”

Having tried to tease Gowol for no reason and instead become the sinister one, the Pungcheon Cult Leader bristled.

“I am a martial arts master. You should not speak to me so carelessly.”

“I will be eighty-seven the day after tomorrow. What will you do about it?”

The two engaged in a fierce battle of glares. I am an old man who would not mind dying tomorrow—with that invincible momentum, the Pungcheon Cult Leader was pushed back.

Just then, from far away, Gowol called to the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“What are you doing? Aren’t you coming?”

Like a sulky child, the Pungcheon Cult Leader swept the stones on the go board into disarray.

“Must be nice to be old!”

He sprang lightly into the air and landed before Gowol, who stood by the distant front gate. From behind him came the old man’s shout: Tsk, you sinister bastard!

The two left the house.

“When one grows old, one fears nothing.”

“Do you think he acted that way because he wasn’t afraid? He realized that our cult leader is a kindhearted man. He read every move.”

“Why do people look down on you so much when you treat them well?”

“To be honest, you didn’t treat him well, did you? You just grumbled beside us every day, saying you were bored, hungry, asking when it would end.”

“What? Did you become close with the go teacher already? Did the two of you plot this?”

Gowol laughed and quickened his steps.

“Now let’s go to the next branch.”

“We’re going again? You’ve become a master at go now, so let’s return.”

Gowol had said he would not return until he became a go master, but in truth, that had been an excuse. He was pouring all his strength into making sure Silver Moon, the intelligence organization, functioned properly. He was traveling to each branch, inspecting and examining them. He was striving to ensure that, even without its leader, everything would operate systematically and smoothly on its own.

“Even if you do this, the Young Cult Leader won’t acknowledge it. He’ll only appear when he needs you and say, ‘Help me with this. Tell me about that! As expected of our Strategist Go!’ He’ll praise you only with words.”

As he said that, he looked back. Whenever he cursed him, Geom Mugeuk had appeared from behind, so he was looking back just in case.

“You aren’t hoping the Young Cult Leader comes, are you?”

“What are you talking about!”

At times, he missed Geom Mugeuk. So sometimes he cursed Geom Mugeuk even more, hoping Geom Mugeuk would appear from behind, saying, “You’re speaking ill of me again.”

“That heartless bastard has forgotten us. Do you know how much money we earn for him?”

Apart from the highest-grade information sent to Geom Mugeuk, they sold information deemed suitable for sale to martial artists. The profits gained from that were used to operate the organization.

The Heaven-Piercing Pavilion was directly operated by the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, but Silver Moon was different. It was impossible to run this vast organization, spread across the Central Plains like a spiderweb, on Geom Mugeuk’s money alone.

“The Second Young Master is greedy.”

“He is not greedy. When he came last time, he gave me a large sum of money. He even ordered me to use it personally.”

“If he put you through that much hardship, of course he should give you money. But what about me? Why did he give only you money?”

“You’re rich, Cult Leader.”

“What kind of logic is that? Is a rich man’s hardship not hardship?”

At that moment, Gowol said something unexpected.

“That hardship is something I should take care of.”

“Why you?”

“Because the hardship you voluntarily went through was for me, Cult Leader. Not for the Young Cult Leader.”

As he said that, Gowol took something from his robe and handed it to the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

When the Pungcheon Cult Leader confirmed what it was, he was astonished. It was a house deed. Even more surprising was that the owner listed on it was the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“You wanted to have a house in the Central Plains, didn’t you? It’s your house, Cult Leader.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s hand holding the deed trembled.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader had wanted to own a house in the Central Plains. While drinking with Gowol, he would occasionally say that he wanted to buy a house. When he was the Pungcheon Cult Leader, his dream had grandly been to advance into the Central Plains, but even while freely roaming the Central Plains, securing a single small house was so difficult.

The moment he bought a house in the Central Plains, he felt as though he would truly settle there, leaving the lands beyond the border behind.

“I bought it with the money the Young Cult Leader gave me this time, plus the salary I have received until now.”

“Why are you buying this!”

Gowol answered calmly.

“I thought it would be good if you had a house, Cult Leader. It is my gift.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader was inwardly moved. He had received countless gifts more precious than this until now, but never had one made him this happy.

“I have a lot of money.”

“I know. Enough to buy hundreds of houses like that.”

“Thousands, you mean!”

“But you didn’t buy even one, did you? Why not? When we traveled around the Central Plains, you could have bought one in each region.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader said nothing. Gowol was looking straight into his heart.

“Can I really buy this? That was what you thought. You were still the former Pungcheon Cult Leader, after all. You somehow felt sorry toward your juniors beyond the border.”

How had he known? He had certainly felt that way too.

“I’m not sorry! Why would I be sorry? I let them become cult leader and made sure everyone had their positions before I left.”

Then Gowol gave another reason.

“You must also have been conscious of the Young Cult Leader. ‘He said he was out helping the strategist, and now he is buying houses!’ You didn’t want to show him that side of you, did you?”

Of course, he had felt that way too. How could this man know his heart so well?

“Why would I care what the Second Young Master thinks! I’m buying it with my own money.”

“Then why didn’t you buy one?”

But more than those reasons, there was one decisive reason.

With an expression that seemed to say, Do you not understand my heart? the Pungcheon Cult Leader revealed the reason.

“It’s not as if I can live only for my own happiness, can I? If I buy a house, I should buy two close by so we can live as neighbors, right? I can’t just buy one because I like it, can I? I should discuss it with you first. You’ve been so busy working—how was I supposed to ask you to go house hunting? Why? You don’t like it? You don’t want to live next door to me?”

Their gazes tangled in midair.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader turned his eyes away and spat out,

“What kind of strategist are you, when you don’t even know the heart of the person beside you? Second Young Master, you chose the wrong man! You’d be better off bringing that Go teacher from earlier and making him your strategist! That old man who saw right through my sly thoughts would be a hundred times better.”

At that, Gowol took something else out from his robes.

“So I bought another house nearby. This one is in my name.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader stood there for a moment as if his soul had left his body. After staring blankly at Gowol for a long while, he suddenly asked,

“It’s smaller than my house, isn’t it?”

Gowol burst into loud laughter.

“I know our Cult Leader’s personality too well to do otherwise. I bought a smaller one for myself.”

“Then that’s fine.”

“Your house won’t even compare to the one you used to live in. Don’t be disappointed later when you see it and think the house is small.”

“I should be disappointed. I am the Pungcheon Cult Leader! I was the previous Pungcheon Cult Leader!”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader gazed toward the distant mountains. Looking at Gowol, he felt as though tears might fall. He was happy to receive the house, but the fact that Gowol had even bought his own house beside it made him ten times happier. That was the true gift.

‘Someday, I’ll build you the largest house in the Central Plains. I’ll build you one even bigger than the Second Young Master’s.’

That was what lay in the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s heart.

It was then. A subordinate came to them, delivered a letter, and left.

“Who is it from?”

“The Young Cult Leader.”

“This is why I can’t help cursing him. On such a good day, what work is he trying to make us do now, sending a letter and all? Tell him we can’t! Tell him we won’t!”

Gowol held the letter out to the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“It says he needs your help, Cult Leader, not mine.”

In that instant, the Pungcheon Cult Leader flinched and opened his eyes wide.

“Me? Not you, but me? Are you sure? He really needs my help?”

“It says your help is urgently needed, and asks that you return quickly.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s shoulders soared toward the sky as he put on a pointless fit of sulking.

“What’s this? Now that there’s no sacred relic left for him to take, he’s going to work me instead?”

“Then should I send a message saying you can’t go?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader spoke as if reluctantly giving in.

“Let’s return, and take a look at the newly bought houses while we’re at it!”

Following behind him as he strode off, Gowol said,

“The houses we bought are in the opposite direction from the road to the main headquarters.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader pretended not to hear and quickened his steps.

Gowol followed with a smile. At last, they were returning to the main headquarters. Having completed the mission of creating an intelligence organization without fail, it was a pleasant journey home.

In contrast to what had happened earlier at the Go teacher’s house, this time the Pungcheon Cult Leader was the one ahead, waving his hand and urging him on.

“What are you doing? Hurry up and come!”

Chapter 309: There Are No Villains Who Like Flowers.
----------------------------------------------------
When I visited, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable was absorbed in sword training again today, her face bare of makeup.

I knew full well why she had become so immersed in martial arts. For masters of Demon Venerable caliber, progress in martial arts was like an extraordinary opportunity that did not come easily. And when such a moment came, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable was someone who would unfailingly make that achievement her own.

After soaring into the air and swinging her sword in a flurry, she turned toward me.

The provocative look she gave me said exactly this:

Come on in!

If she was asking for a bout, then I had to oblige. Without hesitation, I kicked off the ground, flew up, and charged toward her.

The Black Demon Sword and the Ilhwa Sword clashed one after another in midair. Though I attacked faster than I had in our last spar, she blocked my strikes.

At the clear improvement in her skill, I raised my inner power and speed even further.

The swords collided so quickly that the sharp clangs that should have rung out separately—clang, clang, clang—merged into one long sound: Claaaaaang.

Dozens of strands of sword-lines bloomed splendidly and vanished again and again. It was an exchange so swift that, no matter who watched, they would not be able to tell where or how the attacks and defenses were being carried out.

The speed of our sword swings rose higher and higher.

It was not the Ilhwa Sword Venerable and I who were fighting. It was a battle where instinct thrust out the sword, and instinct blocked the sword. A spar in which a single mistake in an instant could send an arm flying or sever a neck. It was a spar more like real combat than real combat itself.

When the speed reached its peak—

Ta-dang! An alien sound burst out, and we descended to the ground together.

Thunk.

The Ilhwa Sword stabbed into the floor.

She had dropped her sword again.

From the Ilhwa Sword Venerable, I could sense neither the regret of defeat nor shame. She had lost, but because she had fought so well, her expression was instead one of satisfaction. In that exchange just now, she had likely displayed swordsmanship superior to any she had wielded in her entire life.

She asked me,

“Just how far are you trying to take me?”

She knew exactly why I had pushed her to her limit. She knew my heart, telling her not to be satisfied, not to stop yet.

“I intend to go all the way.”

All the way.

Yes, I intend to go all the way with these Demon Venerables.

“Why?”

“I told you before. You are the only one I can be stimulated by while exchanging swordsmanship like this. The only people I can freely cross swords with like this are my father and you, Senior. But I cannot spar with my father this often, and even when we do fight, it becomes a different kind of battle. Therefore, my spars with you are incredibly precious to me.”

After a brief pause, I conveyed my sincerity.

“I want to go with you until the very moment we lay down the swords in our hands.”

Emotion flashed across the Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s face.

She looked down at her empty hand and asked,

“Do you remember our first spar?”

“I do.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable also revealed her honest feelings.

“Around that time, I was thinking this: I’ll remain a Demon Venerable for another ten years or so, hand the position over to my successor, and enjoy a comfortable old age.”

Still looking down at her empty palm, she raised her head and looked at me.

“Back then, I could see my destination. As a martial artist, and as a Demon Venerable. The finish line of my life, where people were lined up to cheer and congratulate me. I can still see it now. A path I can simply walk down with no worries, no thoughts. And yet you are standing me at the starting line. Right now, I feel as though I am back at the beginning.”

I gazed into her eyes and said calmly,

“It may seem like it would be comfortable and enjoyable once you cross that finish line, but it will only feel good for about a month. From the next day on, you will be bored and restless. I should have tried something more while I was even a year younger. Isn’t it too empty to end things like this? All sorts of idle thoughts and regrets will torment you, Senior.”

A faint smile formed at the corners of the Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s mouth.

“You say that without ever having gone there?”

“I have a vivid imagination.”

To have Sa Ujong seize some weakness of hers, to be caught up in an ill-fitting greed for power—she was far too precious a person to live that sort of life.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable extended her hand. Then the Ilhwa Sword embedded in the floor pulled free and flew into her grasp. Holding the sword in her hand, she declared,

“I am standing at the starting line again. Until the moment I die, I will never set down the sword in this hand.”

I could feel it. The woman before my eyes was a different person from the Ilhwa Sword Venerable before my regression. Depending on whom one meets, and who stands by one’s side, a person’s life can change like this.

“Why did you come today?”

“Sa Ujong died at the hands of the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable.”

At the unexpected answer, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable flinched in surprise.

“What happened?”

I told her the truth as it was, leaving out everything related to Hwanyeo. As a result, it became a death born of a romantic entanglement.

“Sa Ujong threatened and insulted a Demon Venerable, so he committed a grave crime worthy of death. However, since a Demon Sword died at the hands of another Demon Venerable, it may damage your reputation or that of the Northern Heaven Sword Clan, Senior.”

After being lost in thought for a moment, she asked me,

“How would you like this matter handled?”

“For the sake of the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable, I would like you to yield this once. She is in the midst of establishing her position within Seohwanjin. If this matter is brought to the forefront, her standing will narrow.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable accepted my request.

“Handle it quietly.”

“Thank you.”

“In return, tell me where that man was buried. He did serve me once, after all. I should at least pour a cup of wine on his road to the next world.”

After finishing her words, she began swinging her sword again.

I bowed to her with due courtesy, then left the place.

Just before leaving the Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s residence, I looked back.

She was looking up at the sky, her sword hanging loosely at her side. Somehow, I could sense a complicated state of mind from her. I did not want to know what kind of relationship she had had with Sa Ujong, or what weakness he had held over her.

I only hoped this incident would become a turning point in her life. And that it would allow even her swordsmanship to change.

As I left the Northern Heaven Sword Clan, Seo Daeryong was waiting.

“We received word from those watching the flower garden. The seedlings that came in every day did not arrive yesterday or today.”

That meant one thing.

“She’s trying to leave.”

We could not let her go like this. We had to keep her here somehow. There was a chance, however slight, that Cult Leader Pungcheon might not be able to break the Soul-Subduing Art, and it was also possible that she would be needed in order to break it. We needed her to find out where the Phantom King was as well.

“What are you going to do?”

As I walked toward the main gate of headquarters, I answered,

“What else? I have to stop her from leaving. Send me whatever Enforcer martial artists you can.”

* * *

Hwanyeo was preparing to leave the flower garden.

She had received the report that Sa Ujong was dead. The fact that he had died meant the Yin-Yang Soul Reversal Art had succeeded. For now, she could withdraw, then return a year later to check on her.

If it were an ordinary martial artist, she could succeed with the Soul-Subduing Art in a single stroke and turn them into a puppet, but the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable, who had learned the Soul-Subduing Art herself, could not be controlled all at once. The Red Soul planted in her mind had to take root and grow. That process was like sowing a seed and nurturing a flower. When the blossom opened fully from its bud, she would be able to control the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable completely.

Just then, martial artists flooded into the place, and one person entered among them.

When Hwanyeo confirmed who it was, she was startled. Geom Mugeuk had come in with not only his guards, but even the Enforcer martial artists of the Hwangcheon Pavilion.

“What an honored guest has come again.”

“It seemed like you recognized me right away that day, too. This is our first meeting, so how did you know?”

“Is there anyone doing business here in Maga Village who does not know the Young Cult Leader? It is an honor to see you again.”

While plotting things here, the news she had heard most often of late had been rumors about Geom Mugeuk. Most were rumors difficult to believe, but she knew. She knew those rumors were all true. And she also knew that dealing with Geom Mugeuk was something she had to be extremely cautious about.

That was why Hwanyeo was suspicious of Geom Mugeuk’s visit. Once could be called coincidence, but twice?

‘Did he notice something?’

If not, there was no reason for the Young Cult Leader himself to come forward like this.

Of course, that did not mean she was anxious. There were many ways to escape. She could immediately unleash the Soul-Subduing Art and make the guards draw their swords to kill the Young Cult Leader. Then she could slip away amid the chaos.

“My subordinate was truly pleased with the flower she bought from you that day.”

“There is no gift better for a beautiful woman than flowers.”

If he had come only for such a reason, she would certainly have suspected the Young Cult Leader. But Geom Mugeuk asked bluntly,

“Let me ask you one thing.”

“Please do.”

“Do you know the martial artist who came here often of late?”

When he mentioned Sa Ujong, Hwanyeo was inwardly startled, but she did not reveal that emotion at all.

“Of course. He would always come, look at the flowers and trees, and leave.”

“His name was Sa Ujong.”

“He never told me his name.”

“He died recently.”

“Oh my! How did that happen?”

Hwanyeo sounded deeply regretful. Her acting was excellent, so to anyone watching, it would seem as if she had grown unknowingly fond of the demon martial artist who visited every day.

“Our cult is investigating that man’s death. That is also why I came here.”

“Ah, I see.”

Hwanyeo bowed her head and showed fear.

“But seeing as the Young Cult Leader is investigating personally, was he someone of very high status?”

“That man’s death is connected to one of our cult’s Demon Venerables.”

By revealing it like this instead, Geom Mugeuk made Hwanyeo inwardly puzzled.

‘Has he realized the matter with the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable?’

No. If he had, he would not have come like this. He would have brought the Demon Venerables with him to capture her.

‘He does not know about me yet.’

Geom Mugeuk’s attitude toward her was also only at the level of suspicion.

“So do not leave the flower garden for the time being.”

“I am someone with nowhere to go.”

“Then why have the seedlings that came every day stopped arriving?”

Geom Mugeuk had deliberately revealed even the fact that they were watching the flower garden.

“I have been feeling unwell, so I stopped them to rest for a few days.”

Geom Mugeuk sent her a suspicious look.

This was how he would avoid suspicion.

If he came for some other reason and acted no matter how naturally, she would find it strange. Better to say, We are watching you, so do not leave! That had a much greater chance of keeping her here. And he added one decisive thing on top of that.

“If you become frightened and leave, we will list you as a public enemy of our cult and send a pursuit party. Your portrait and description will be spread throughout the entire Central Plains, so there will be nowhere for you to hide.”

Geom Mugeuk knew her weakness precisely. They were moving in secret. Therefore, the thing they were most careful about was having their identities exposed to the outside. Her portrait and description posted all over the Central Plains? That was absolutely not something she would want.

Sure enough, Hwanyeo’s heart grew heavy.

‘Damn it.’

Being exposed was something she absolutely could not allow.

Geom Mugeuk went over to the flower Ian had bought and smelled its fragrance.

“Do you know what I said when my subordinates reported that this flower garden was suspicious?”

“What did you say?”

“There are no villains among those who love flowers.”

Geom Mugeuk left the flower garden with those he had brought.

Hwanyeo watched his back in silence.

* * *

“Ah! How long has it been since I came to headquarters?”

Entering headquarters, Cult Leader Pungcheon drew in a deep breath. It was not the Pungcheon Cult but the main headquarters of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, and yet there was the comfort of returning home.

After entering the inner court, the two headed straight for Geom Mugeuk’s residence. It was not his old residence, but the large and fine one he had received after becoming Young Cult Leader.

“Now that he’s become Young Cult Leader, his house has turned magnificent, hasn’t it?”

Cult Leader Pungcheon looked around Geom Mugeuk’s residence, something he had never cared about before.

“Power really is nice.”

“If you’re jealous, go back beyond the frontier. The cult leader’s residence was ten times bigger than this, wasn’t it?”

“Who says I’m jealous? I’m just saying.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon examined the interior from the window.

“Where did the Second Young Master go? We came, so he should have been waiting here first. Don’t tell me he even forgot he called us. Ah, right. I was the one who summoned you, wasn’t I? That matter has all been resolved. Since you’ve come all this way, rest for a few days, then please go handle something else. I’m busy, so I’ll be going now.”

Having imitated him so often, he had become better than anyone at mimicking Geom Mugeuk.

“Who knows, I might have gone from a somewhat tolerable Second Young Master to a nefarious Young Cult Leader.”

Just then, a welcome voice came from behind.

“You never fail to curse me in my absence.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader chuckled to himself without turning around. Yes, this was the voice he had so longed to hear.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader turned around.

Geom Mugeuk stood there, unchanged as ever.

“For now, he is still a somewhat tolerable Young Cult Leader. He probably still has a ways to go before he becomes truly nefarious.”

“That remains to be seen.”

Though he had missed him, the Pungcheon Cult Leader masked his feelings with petty spite.

“You have worked hard all this time, Cult Leader.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader looked at Gowol, who stood beside him.

“If it is that kind of greeting, it should be directed here.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Gowol and spoke.

“You have worked hard.”

Gowol smiled.

“It is only right that I do what I must.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader cut in, praising Gowol.

“Even when it is one’s own duty, no one works that hard. Young Cult Leader, buy her some restorative tonics. She has worked hard.”

Gowol shot the Pungcheon Cult Leader a look, telling him not to speak nonsense.

“I will buy her only the finest tonics.”

“You’ll buy some for me too, won’t you?”

Geom Mugeuk laughed heartily.

“Don’t just laugh—make sure you buy them. Don’t slight people with just food. Now, what help do you need that you called me?”

“It concerns the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable.”

After the previous Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable died, Cheongseon had received the Soul-Seizing Technique directly from the Pungcheon Cult Leader. At the mention of a matter concerning her, the playfulness vanished from the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s face.

“What about that child?”

Geom Mugeuk got straight to the point.

“Unknown assailants have cast the Soul-Seizing Technique on the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable.”

The moment he finished speaking, the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s aura shifted.

Whooooosh!

His aura awakened a primal terror.

Dark clouds gathered from every direction, and in an instant the surroundings dimmed into dreary weather that seemed to herald an imminent typhoon. The sound of bells drifted in on the bleak wind blowing from afar.

Each time the bells tolled, doors opened in the ground, and all manner of dark, grotesque monsters began to burst forth. It was as if the gates of hell were opening one after another.

The creatures that leaped out at tremendous speed rushed at their target without hesitation. Of course, they were not real monsters, but illusions embodying his aura.

Hundreds of monsters charged toward Geom Mugeuk.

The horrifying sensation that washed over him each time they passed through his body chilled his will to fight and shattered his mental strength. Involuntarily, his Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art activated.

Had it been someone with weak internal energy or willpower, they would already be swinging a sword wildly like a madman under the influence of this aura alone.

It was an enraged aura of the Pungcheon Cult Leader that none had ever witnessed.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader now was not his usual self, who joked around and blustered. He had returned to his true form—as the absolute sovereign of the transcendent Murim who had achieved mastery in the world’s supreme skill, the Angcheon Great Demonic Art.

With a cold, baleful gleam in his eyes, the Pungcheon Cult Leader spoke.

“They dared to lay a hand on my disciple?”

Chapter 310: Take Me Before That Damn Bitch
-------------------------------------------
Geommugeuk was completely intoxicated by the Pungcheon Cult Leader's aura.

He felt the Cult Leader's rage in the illusions of the flying beasts.

If the Cheonma Guardian Divine Art had not activated, his blood would have boiled in response to that aura, and he would have had to suppress the urge to draw his sword.

This was the true aura of a master. The true aura of a master who had reached the pinnacle of a martial art.

Now was the time to quell his rage.

Geommugeuk dodged the charging monstrous beasts this way and that, waving his hands and spouting nonsense.

"I didn't lay a hand on your disciple. Argh, I'm dying!"

At that, the Pungcheon Cult Leader withdrew his aura.

The rampaging monsters vanished in an instant. The dark clouds dispersed and the surroundings brightened. The bell tolls that had beat like a heart and opened the gates of hell faded into the distance. As the lingering echoes of the bells trailed off, one could feel the Pungcheon Cult Leader's emotions subsiding.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader's gaze returned to normal.

"Where is Cheongseon now?"

Beneath that calm question lay a heart worried for her.

This was why one had to look closely at people. No, even though he had looked closely, he had never known the Pungcheon Cult Leader had this side to him.

It was a moment that made him realize yet again how difficult it was for a person to know another—to understand and recognize all those layered, different facets.

"The Soul-Seizing Demon Lord is well."

Only then did the Pungcheon Cult Leader's face relax.

"If she knew you were this worried, she would be overjoyed. Though thanks to that, I nearly died."

"Nonsense! You stood your ground perfectly fine."

"I even had to use the Cheonma Guardian Divine Art!"

He had mentioned the Cheonma Guardian Divine Art on purpose to save the Pungcheon Cult Leader's face. And indeed, at those words, the Cult Leader's mood immediately brightened—after all, he acknowledged the latter's father as the strongest under heaven.

Gowol, who had been standing behind the Pungcheon Cult Leader, spoke up.

"Cult Leader, did you care this much about your disciple? You don't usually cherish her so much, do you?"

"I don't cherish her. What do you mean, cherish?"

As if somewhat flustered, the Pungcheon Cult Leader gave a different excuse.

"It's not because of Cheongseon, but my pride! How dare someone lay a hand on my disciple? This is a slight against me. Surely they couldn't have not known she was my disciple? Ah, perhaps they didn't? If they didn't know, that would be why they did it?"

Geommugeuk shook his head firmly.

"There's no way. Someone who orchestrated something like this couldn't have failed to investigate that much."

"Just who is this person?"

"A woman."

The Pungcheon Cult Leader tilted his head. Among the women he knew who had mastered the Soul-Seizing Art, no one came to mind who would do something like this. In fact, it was the same for men. To dare touch a Demon Lord of the Cheonma Divine Cult? If found out, their entire clan would be annihilated. Just who?

"Who is this woman?"

"The woman disguised as the owner of a flower garden in Maga Village."

"I must see her myself to know. Before that, let me see Cheongseon first."

"I will go and bring her. Oh, your disciple does not know she has fallen victim to the Soul-Seizing Art."

"I suppose not."

The Pungcheon Cult Leader's eyes grew deeper. He was one who never neglected matters related to the Soul-Seizing Art, whatever else he might disregard.

As Geommugeuk was leaving, Gowol spoke to him.

"Cult Leader."

Having called out to him, Gowol said nothing.

They were always together; how could he not know Gowol's heart? She was worried that because it concerned his disciple, he might get swept up in his emotions.

"What worry? You know full well what a terrible human being I am."

With a face that said 'I know very well,' Gowol nodded.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader shouted. Until his disciple arrived, he had temporarily hidden his dignity.

"You're supposed to say that's not true! You should say you're worried because the Cult Leader is secretly soft-hearted! At least say it's not true!"

* * *

Black energy rising from Cheongseon's fingertips coiled around her arm.

The black smoke transformed into an armor resembling fish scales.

It seemed the Phantom Armor Art would succeed, perfectly protecting her arm, but then:

Clang!

Just as a sound like splitting metal was heard, the scales burst in all directions with a dull pop, scattering as smoke.

Cheongseon clutched her arm, her face twisting in pain.

She had nearly injured her arm severely because she recited the mnemonic formula incorrectly.

After killing Saujong, she had been unable to focus on her martial arts training.

Because of guilt? Absolutely not. She was not such a weak-hearted person.

It was her cruel and stubborn nature that tormented her—regret that she had been unable to control her own emotions.

She kept recalling that day's events, imagining how she should have handled them calmly.

'I should have answered like this. I should have handled it like this. I should have endured.'

Perhaps it was because of the Young Cult Leader. She should have shown him a more impressive sight.

She was the only Demon Lord of the next generation.

Even if all the other Demon Lords died of old age, she would be the one to survive and serve Geommugeuk when he became the Cult Leader.

Because it felt as though she had ruined the beginning of such an important relationship, lingering regrets remained.

Then, a subordinate's voice came from outside.

"The Young Cult Leader has arrived."

"Bring him in."

She had just been thinking of Geommugeuk, so she was inwardly surprised. How timely his appearance was.

Shortly after, Geommugeuk entered.

"Young Cult Leader."

"Demon Lord."

The two cupped their fists and exchanged greetings. Cheongseon was inwardly worried that the Saujong matter might have grown more serious.

But contrary to her worries, Geommugeuk had come bearing extremely good news.

And two pieces at that.

"The Ilhwa Sword Lord has decided to overlook this matter quietly."

Cheongseon was greatly relieved. She was so delighted that she had to force herself to maintain her composure, unable to openly rejoice.

"The Sword Lord yielded for the Demon Lord's sake."

How could Cheongseon not know?

That this outcome was only possible because Geommugeuk had mediated well.

"I will not forget the grace of your help."

And then another piece of good news was delivered.

"The Pungcheon Cult Leader has returned."

"Ah! Master!"

"The moment he returned, he asked for the Demon Lord first."

He wanted to tell her. How furious her master had been upon hearing that someone had cast a Soul-Seizing Art on her.

He wanted to tell her that even if he didn't show it on the outside, he cherished her deeply.

He would tell her once everything was over. Hoping that the relationship between the two would deepen even further.

"Where is he?"

"He is waiting for the Demon Lord at my residence right now."

This time, Cheongseon did not hide her joy.

* * *

Geommugeuk and Cheongseon returned to the residence.

"Master."

Cheongseon always tried to show a dignified face to everyone, but she constantly suffered from this burden.

When would she become strong enough in the Soul-Seizing Art to be like the other Demon Lords?

No one dared say such a thing to her, but it was a question that haunted her like an obsession.

To her, the Pungcheon Cult Leader was like a sturdy fence—a reliable protector.

Perhaps because she had been depressed over Saujong lately, seeing her master after so long was even more welcome.

"Did you have a safe journey? Master."

Cheongseon knelt and paid her respects.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader could discern her martial accomplishments from her aura alone.

"You have trained diligently during my absence!"

Cheongseon was overjoyed that her master recognized her achievements.

She had trained harder than anyone to catch up to the other Demon Lords.

With her master acknowledging that effort, it felt as though all her past hardships had been rewarded.

"Thank you, Master."

"Rise."

The Pungcheon Cult Leader stared intently into her eyes.

It was the first time her master had looked at her like this, so Cheongseon was inwardly flustered.

As she tried to lower her head,

"Look straight into my eyes."

"Yes, Master."

Cheongseon raised her head and met her master's gaze.

Geommugeuk and Gowol watched the scene with tense hearts.

Please, let it be a Soul-Seizing Art that the Pungcheon Cult Leader could unravel.
