“Please help me, Master. If you help me, I can unify the outer regions much more easily. Please help me, if only for the main cult.”

In the end, the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s rage exploded.

“Shut it! You insolent wretch!”

From the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s body, a frigid energy erupted as if exploding.

Swoooosh!

So Baekta’s expression didn’t change in the slightest, as though he had expected this reaction from his master the moment he first broached the subject. He showed no fear.

“It is a futile dream!”

“Why is it futile? A martial artist dreams of unifying Murim—why is that futile?”

“Do you speak without knowing how many people war will kill? Tens of thousands will die.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s voice rose, but So Baekta’s tone dropped even lower.

“Since when did Master care for people’s lives?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s expression froze coldly. The disciple was crossing the line. No, the moment he spoke of taking the first step, that foot was already beyond the line.

“Why is it that only the Demonic Cult may dream that dream, only the Murim Alliance may dream it, only the Saedo Alliance may dream it? Is it a dream we are forbidden to dream? Since I was young, the words I heard most from Master and my seniors were about advancing into the Central Plains. If this is how it would be, why did you all say such things?”

Pressed and questioned like this, the Pungcheon Cult Leader was bewildered as to how he should reply.

At that moment, someone insolent and clever came to mind. If it were that person—the one who kept adults from sleeping at night—he would have given this stupid fool a sharp scolding to bring him to his senses. But all he could manage was this much:

“You are not in your right mind.”

So Baekta walked toward the thunder bell like a true madman and struck it.

Deng! Da-ding!

The sound of the thunder bell echoed through the cult leader’s hall. It was as if it were a proclamation. That a new era had begun. To think he would hear the thunder bell—so longed to hear—in this manner.

The situation felt too surreal to the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

He almost felt as though that bell signal would cause people to pour out from behind the curtains shouting, “Surprise! It’s a welcome-back party for the Cult Leader!”

Of course, no such thing happened. Because So Baekta had the most serious gaze he had ever shown since meeting him.

“Did Master leave the cult in his right mind, ever?”

Yes, as the brat said, he truly hadn’t been in his right mind back then. He did feel sorry for that. But if the object of that apology was the Pungcheon Cult, then so be it; it wasn’t So Baekta. These weren’t words one should hear from the one who had inherited everything from him.

“Even if everyone else says such things, shouldn’t you be the one who doesn’t?”

“No. Others may not be able to, but I can.”

Da-ding!

The thunder bell rang out again.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader clenched his fist, feeling as though the demon carved on the bell was staring at him and laughing. His rage had boiled enough; now it only needed to overflow.

Then, Geom Muguk’s words echoed in his heart.

—The answer is not to be found in you, Cult Leader.

Ah, I’ve gotten worked up again and can’t see clearly. I was fighting myself again.

Matching the sound of the bell fading, the Pungcheon Cult Leader forced his emotions to subside.

“Fine. Very well, let us say I acknowledge your dream. But you! You haven’t even achieved great mastery in the Angcheon Great Demonic Art, yet you dream of unifying Murim?”

Had he at least reached the pinnacle of illusion arts, that might be different. Such strength would have its own allure.

But it would take at least another ten years for So Baekta to achieve great mastery in the Angcheon Great Demonic Art.

So Baekta stroked the thunder bell and answered calmly.

“If a martial tournament were held, martial prowess would be most important. But in unifying Murim, something else will be more important.”

“What else?”

So Baekta didn’t answer and walked out toward the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“You have seen the thunder bell, so please return now. The elders will be waiting for you, Master.”

When internal energy was injected into the Taesaui mechanism, a wall descended before the sacred object.

A wall descended before the thunder bell as well. The demon of the thunder bell hiding its form beyond the wall seemed as if it were saying:

You are obsolete. Make way for the young. When the back waves of the Yangtze push, you must be swept away.

Fortunately, the Pungcheon Cult Leader pushed away those negative thoughts.

You vile demon, they are the back waves who say that. Please, stop these solitary battles within your heart. My back waves are the ones that tell me that until I’m sick of it. Do you understand, you wretch!

The Pungcheon Cult Leader calmed the storm raging in his heart and looked at So Baekta again. Yes, I will look only at you as you are now.

He read a powerful will in the gaze directed at him. This brat, he’s serious.

“You must have known I would try to stop you. Why did you tell me?”

He had expected something like, “You lost the right to stop me when you left the Pungcheon Cult.” But So Baekta revealed a reason he had never imagined.

“No, I didn’t know. How could I know Master’s heart? You might have offered to help. You might be tired of merely looking after the young cult leader. Achieving my dream would be several times easier if you helped. Of course I had to tell you. Now, please go.”

So Baekta took the first step.

Until just moments ago, he had been thinking:

Who was it that had made his calm disciple like this?

But watching that confident figure walking away, he was no longer sure. Perhaps So Baekta had been this kind of person all along, and he had simply failed to see it.

Perhaps he was now paying the price for never once asking his disciple what his dream was.

* * *

The first words the Pungcheon Cult Leader uttered upon returning to the guesthouse were:

“I truly don’t know.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader told Geom Muguk and Ma Bul everything about the conversation he’d had with his disciple in the cult leader’s hall.

If anything, his emotions were more violent than before. In front of his disciple, he had tried to control himself and held back a great deal. It all came bursting out.

Having heard the whole story, Geom Muguk and Ma Bul were shocked. They had never imagined the new Pungcheon Cult Leader was proceeding with such matters.

“I wanted to handle it like you, but I couldn’t do it well. I feel like I was beaten one-sidedly.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader didn’t bother putting on false pride in front of Geom Muguk. Seeing that he also had nothing to hide in front of Ma Bul, it seemed the journey through the sandstorm together had been worthwhile.

“From now on, don’t tell me to endure! Don’t tell me to look closer! I’ll shout as I please, rage as I please, and think as I please! Ugh, I should have smacked that lunatic on the back of the head while calling him a madman.”

Ma Bul, listening, nodded with an expression that said it suited him.

Geom Muguk smiled at the Pungcheon Cult Leader. He was grateful that the man tried to follow his advice. The Cult Leader had done well. Since the Illusion King was behind this, they had to be careful, ever so careful.

“The fact that he revealed his plan to you means his preparations are already more or less complete.”

A problem as serious as uncovering where the Illusion King was hiding had occurred. The Pungcheon Cult being swept up in war was something Geom Muguk certainly did not wish for.

In any case, one question naturally arose.

“If the one behind this influenced this decision, what benefit is there in driving the outer regions into war?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader had been so immersed in his disciple that he hadn’t thought that far.

“What could be the reason?”

“We must uncover it from now on.”

What did the Illusion King want?

If his goal was to seize control of the Pungcheon Cult, he wouldn’t have steered things toward starting a war like this.

What in the world had happened between the Illusion King and So Baekta?

In the previous regression, the Pungcheon Cult had not started a war in the outer regions. When people changed, events changed in this way.

Geom Muguk asked the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“Do you regret it? Putting him in the cult leader’s seat.”

The word “Of course” got stuck in his throat. Was he truly regretting it? If so, why didn’t the answer come out immediately?

Geom Muguk asked in a different way.

“If you could go back, would you seat someone else?”

After a brief moment of thought, the Pungcheon Cult Leader answered.

“Even if I went back, I would seat that brat.”

No matter how much he thought about it, So Baekta had been the best choice at the time. However, if he could go back, he would ask the brat about his dreams, and before that futile dream could grow, he would plant a different one.

Even if someone prodded the dark desire deep within his heart, he would raise a disciple capable of saying, “Why are you prodding it, you bastard! Why don’t you get lost?”

“Then that’s fine. You can still set your disciple straight, even now.”

“How?”

“That is an answer you must give, Cult Leader. This is the Pungcheon Cult. What shall we do?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader stood up and said.

“There is someone I must meet first.”

Since there was no need to draw attention, Ma Bul and the guards were left behind, and the Pungcheon Cult Leader secretly slipped out of the guesthouse.

* * *

The place Geom Muguk arrived at together with the Pungcheon Cult Leader was the house of Seongya, who had been the chief of the Six Great Elders.

He was precisely the person the Pungcheon Cult Leader had cherished most during his days as cult leader.

“He is not one to break a promise with me.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader trusted him.

His house sat alone in the wasteland where the sandstorm blew. It felt empty, yet it possessed an atmosphere all its own.

Moreover, being in a wide-open place meant they could see anyone approaching, which was an advantage.

The front gate was open.

“Elder Seong.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader called from the courtyard, but no one came out.

Geom Muguk and the Pungcheon Cult Leader exchanged glances and entered the house together.

The house was empty.

“Judging by the dust, it seems he has been away for quite some time.”

Worry flickered across the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s face at Geom Muguk’s words.

Given the circumstances, Seongya’s absence felt ominous.

The two examined the interior. Neat and tidy. Just seeing how well-kept the house was revealed his personality.

“Does he have no family?”

“He was always alone.”

“Who was closest to him?”

After brief consideration, the Pungcheon Cult Leader let out a sigh.

He hadn’t known who Seongya was close to. Yet he had said Seongya was the person he cherished most. It was a life lived caring only about how others treated him.

“I wonder just what I was thinking, living like that.”

“Don’t blame yourself too much. Most people are similar.”

“Aren’t you different?”

“I was the same. No, I was worse.”

“But I am a young cult leader, am I not?”

“I was the Cult Leader!”

“Cult Leader!”

“Yes, Cult Leader.”

“No, look at this.”

Geom Muguk had discovered something.

Charred paper remained beside the desk.

“The house was kept so tidy, yet this ash was not cleaned away. He must have rushed out after seeing the contents.”

Unfortunately, only a corner of the burnt paper remained, and its contents were unreadable.

Touching the paper and examining it closely, the Pungcheon Cult Leader said something astonishing.

“Can you restore these contents?”

“No.”

“I can.”

Geom Muguk was startled by those words.

“There is such an illusion art? Please teach me that as well!”

“There is no such illusion art.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader rose, left the burnt remains as they were, and went outside.

Geom Muguk followed and asked.

“You said you would restore it?”

“I am going to restore it.”

“Shouldn’t you at least take the burnt remains?”

“That paper is a secret paper treated with a special chemical. When heat is applied, the true contents are revealed.”

“How did you know that?”

Then, an astonishing answer came from the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“Because I chose it.”

“Is it the secret paper used in the Pungcheon Cult?”

“It is not.”

Then how did the Cult Leader know?

But he said he chose it? What did it mean that the Pungcheon Cult Leader had chosen that paper?

“Gowol nagged endlessly about choosing something cheap and of good quality. I was saving your money, you see.”

At those words, Geom Muguk understood.

In the statement that he had chosen it, and that he could restore the contents, the answer was already there.

“It is the secret paper used in Eunwol.”

Chapter 321: This Sect Does Not Permit It
-----------------------------------------
In a remote forest on the outskirts, we waited for the Silver Moon martial artist sent by Gowol.

Once Cult Leader Pungcheon and I made up our minds and moved, there was no one who could keep watch on us.

Unless the Phantom King himself took action, anyone capable of tracking and surveilling our movements with stealth and qinggong would have to be at the level of Uncle Hwi, who guarded my father. But how common could someone like that be?

“If Elder Seong is a man of deep loyalty, as you said, Cult Leader, then he must have had no choice but to step down from his position as elder.”

“You mean his disciple pressured him and drove him out?”

“There’s a high chance of it. He may have desperately opposed the idea of unifying the Outer Territories.”

And now he had disappeared.

“Do you think So Baekta killed Elder Seong?”

I answered honestly.

“It’s possible.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon’s gaze sank coldly.

“If he did…… I will not forgive him.”

I said nothing. This was a matter between Cult Leader Pungcheon and his disciple, so it depended on his will.

Just then, a man arrived there.

The man, who introduced himself as Hwang, said he was Silver Moon’s person in charge of the Outer Territories.

From his gait alone, I could tell that neither his martial arts nor his qinggong were ordinary.

Even so, he looked plain. That was also a trait of the martial artists active in Silver Moon. If you mixed them in among people, they were so ordinary that they would never stand out.

“I have found out what you asked. The information Elder Seong asked us to investigate was about an ironworks.”

“Where is this place?”

Then an unexpected name came up.

“It is the Sinung Ironworks.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon tilted his head.

“Sinung Ironworks? Was there such a place?”

An ironworks of the Outer Territories that even Cult Leader Pungcheon did not know.

“It has not been around for very long, and it is not large in scale, so you likely would not have known of it.”

At Hwang’s explanation, Cult Leader Pungcheon nodded. Since he did not know it, he naturally assumed I would not know it either, but I knew about the Sinung Ironworks.

In my life before regression, there was another name for the Sinung Ironworks.

The Number One Ironworks of the Central Plains.

In the days when Hwa Mugi and the Twelve Zodiac Kings seized control of the martial world, the Sinung Ironworks was the most famous ironworks in the Central Plains. It commanded hundreds of regional ironworks throughout the Central Plains and supplied weapons to most of the major sects. It was truly the greatest ironworks. They amassed tremendous wealth by selling arms.

‘So the Sinung Ironworks began from here!’

I had thought the Sinung Ironworks had nothing to do with Hwa Mugi or the Twelve Zodiac Kings.

But seeing them appear in a situation like this, they too were connected to them.

“Unexpected. I thought what Elder Seong was trying to investigate would be the Hwayang Sect or the Heuksa Gang.”

If measured purely by the size of their forces, Pungcheon Cult could be seen as the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, the Hwayang Sect as the Martial Alliance, and the Heuksa Gang as the Unorthodox Alliance. In other words, when So Baekta said he would unify the Outer Territories, it meant he would strike the Hwayang Sect and the Heuksa Gang and force them to their knees.

Naturally, we had expected an investigation related to them, so Cult Leader Pungcheon’s doubts were only natural when an ironworks he had never heard of appeared instead.

“Do you perhaps know why he asked for an investigation into the Sinung Ironworks?”

Cult Leader Pungcheon asked just in case, but the answer was as expected.

“I cannot know his reason. Here is the information he took.”

“Thank you.”

Hwang immediately left.

Standing there with Cult Leader Pungcheon, I shared and read through the materials on the Sinung Ironworks. The information was varied. There were personal details on the House Master and those who worked under him, and it detailed how many weapons they produced and how much they supplied to which sects. There was even a layout of the buildings there.

“The Sinung Ironworks is definitely connected to that disciple of mine trying to start a war. Elder Seong realized that and entrusted Silver Moon with the investigation.”

“I think so as well. Elder Seong ran out while looking through these materials. There must be a part that caught his attention.”

As he read the documents, Cult Leader Pungcheon shook his head.

“I absolutely detest numbers.”

A considerable portion of the materials had to do with numbers. How much was produced and how much was earned. How much was spent.

Just then, my eyes caught something strange.

“Look at this. This is the amount of iron purchased last year. Compared to the year before, it is ten times greater.”

“So it is.”

“And here is the amount of weapons they sold last year.”

“They did not sell nearly that many weapons compared to the iron they bought. The gap is far too large.”

“And yet this year, they purchased even more iron. They should have had a great deal of inventory left.”

“That is certainly suspicious.”

We looked at each other, nodded, and immediately launched ourselves toward the Sinung Ironworks.

* * *

Before visiting the Sinung Ironworks, we bought bamboo hats with veils attached and covered our faces. We had decided to visit as customers.

“It’s larger than I expected.”

“According to the information earlier, they bought the land near the ironworks last year and expanded.”

Countless carts laden with cargo were coming and going, and the sounds of hammering metal and working bellows rang out from every direction.

An attendant came to greet us.

“What brings you here?”

“We have come to buy swords.”

“There are many kinds of swords.”

“High-grade swords.”

“Please follow me.”

The man guided us somewhere. As I followed him, I looked around. The good thing about coming as customers was that we could openly inspect the place.

In every building, muscular men stripped to the waist were busily making weapons.

At a glance, there was nothing suspicious. It was truly an ordinary ironworks.

When we entered the place the man led us to, various swords were on display there.

“Please take your time looking around.”

After examining the swords there, I asked the man.

“Do you have anything better than what is here?”

“We have supreme-grade items, but they are very expensive.”

“Let me see them.”

“Please wait a moment.”

A short while later, not the man who had guided us earlier, but a muscular young man entered.

“I am Ho Gyeong.”

Ho Gyeong.

This person, too, had been among the individuals in the information we received from Silver Moon. Since the House Master did not often show himself outside, the one effectively running the ironworks was this Ho Gyeong. The reason we had come as customers was also because I wanted to meet this man.

“You wish to see supreme-grade swords?”

He opened the box he had brought and handed me a sword.

I drew the sword and examined it. It was not quite a famed sword or a treasured blade, but among ordinary swords, it was truly well made.

“I’ll take twelve of these.”

“Twelve?”

Ho Gyeong was startled. Cult Leader Pungcheon beside me also looked at me in surprise.

—You’re not really going to buy them, are you?

—I am. Since they’ve gone through the trouble of coming all this way, I thought I’d buy each of the guards a sword.

—Buy them with your own money!

—Of course.

Since I could become a major customer, Ho Gyeong asked carefully.

“May I ask where you are from?”

I tapped the veil hanging down in front of my bamboo hat with my hand. It meant that I did not wish to reveal it.

“My apologies. It seemed this was your first transaction with our house, so I committed a discourtesy.”

Perhaps because he dealt with many martial artists, Ho Gyeong’s words and actions were very polite.

“Someone introduced this place to me.”

“Who was it?”

“Elder Seong of Pungcheon Cult.”

When Elder Seong was mentioned, I carefully observed Ho Gyeong’s expression.

“Ah, I see. He is a truly admirable man.”

He did not waver at all. From my impression, at least Ho Gyeong had nothing to do with Elder Seong’s disappearance. Or perhaps he had not disappeared? If he had hidden himself voluntarily, that would be fortunate.

“Then you will be taking the swords with you personally.”

“That’s right.”

“Understood. Please wait a moment.”

Ho Gyeong went inside to prepare the swords.

Leaving Cult Leader Pungcheon there, I quickly slipped outside. Since Silver Moon’s information had even included the layout of the buildings here, I launched myself without hesitation.

I encountered people along the way, but they did not notice me.

My Shadow Steps, having reached great accomplishment, turned them into blind men with their eyes open and guided me deep into the ironworks.

I moved between the buildings here and there. I used Swift Steps, Shadow Steps, and Flash Steps.

At the most secluded corner, there was a large building. The sound of metal came from within.

I sensed martial artists hiding around it. Judging by the airtight security, it seemed to be the place where the House Master forged swords. This alone showed that it was by no means an ordinary ironworks.

Would they really be able to detect my Shadow Steps after I had achieved great accomplishment?

I did not put that to the test today. What I was looking for was not the House Master. I was searching for the iron they had purchased. That vast quantity of iron had to be stored somewhere, but no matter where I searched, there was no place where iron was being kept.

‘Did they secretly make everything and send it somewhere?’

But for that, the scale of the ironworks was far too small.

Then I saw an empty lot overgrown with weeds.

It was the land they had bought last year, the one mentioned in Silver Moon’s information. They had bought it while they were busily scheming something. If they were going to leave it abandoned like that, why had they bought it?

I released dozens of strands of qi at once and scanned the surroundings. I was checking whether anyone was hiding there.

Then I sensed something from the ground of the empty lot. At first, I thought it was a person in hiding, but it was not.

All of my qi focused on that place.

* * *

“The swords are ready.”

Ho Gyeong returned with twelve swords, each wrapped well in leather, tucked into a long leather bag.

Only Cult Leader Pungcheon was in the room.

“Where did the other gentleman go?”

“He said he felt a little stifled and went out for some air.”

“I will escort him back.”

Just as Ho Gyeong was about to go outside, Cult Leader Pungcheon said,

“First, let us settle the price of the swords.”

He had held him back to buy time, but inwardly, Cult Leader Pungcheon was suspicious.

‘Surely not? Did the Young Cult Leader calculate even this far? That I would buy time while paying? Yes, if it is that merciless and greedy Young Cult Leader, he would be more than capable of it. Gowol, oh Gowol. I have come all the way to the Outer Territories only to be fleeced.’

Cult Leader Pungcheon paid the price.

He took out the money as slowly as possible and counted it twice, then three times to confirm it.

Even when the payment was finished, Geom Mugeuk had not returned, so Ho Gyeong went outside.

Geom Mugeuk, who had returned at some point, was stretching in front of the building.

“The swords are all ready.”

* * *

Only after we landed far away from the Sinung Ironworks did I tell Cult Leader Pungcheon the results of my search.

“I found out where that iron is.”

“Where was it?”

“There was a secret workshop underground. They were making weapons there.”

Through the qi I had released, I had discovered that there was a vast underground space beneath the ground, and that constant faint vibrations and noise could be sensed from it.

“The scale was tremendous.”

“So they were preparing for war there.”

War required enormous supplies.

Supplies were one thing, but the most important thing in war was not letting the enemy discover your preparations. So Baekta had joined hands with the Sinung Ironworks and secretly prepared war materiel. No, to be precise, the Phantom King had likely connected the two of them.

“There must be a considerable amount of materiel piled up there.”

“What will you do now?”

“I should meet So Baekta once.”

At last, the moment had come to face him alone.

“What do you intend to do?”

“The fact that he revealed that plan to the Cult Leader means he must have expected it to reach my ears as well. If so, he must also have something to say to me. Since he has shown one card, I need to see what other card he is holding.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon smiled meaningfully. He must have been thinking that his disciple had finally met his match.

“Ah, and this is the money spent on the swords. Please take it.”

“No need. Consider it my gift to the young ones.”

“Are you planning to regret it again later?”

“If I want to blame you for this for a long time to come, I should be the one to buy them.”

I handed him the leather bag of swords I had been carrying.

“Then please give them to them yourself. If you give them the swords, Cult Leader, the guards will truly be pleased. I will go to the Cult Leader’s Hall.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon accepted the leather bag and said,

“While I take the credit, you show him the taste of a true wave from behind.”

* * *

When I entered the Cult Leader’s Hall to meet So Baekta, he was standing before a bronze mirror hung on the wall, drawing patterns on his face.

“Young Cult Leader, please wait a moment.”

So Baekta greeted me through the bronze mirror.

“Take your time.”

I walked behind him. For a moment, I watched as unique lines unlike anything in the world were made.

“Our cult has three patterns in all. The Gyohwa, which symbolizes our cult; the Cheonhwa, the pattern used when going to war; and the Punghwa, which is drawn when one dies. The pattern I am drawing now is the Cheonhwa, drawn when going to war.”

Indeed, its colors and shape were intense. What I had seen on the first day had been the Gyohwa, and now I was seeing the second, the Cheonhwa.

“What do you think? Would you like to try drawing it once?”

“I decline. It seems you haven’t heard the rumors about me. I am a pacifist, you see.”

In the bronze mirror, So Baekta looked at me with a faint smile.

“You must be very timid.”

“Isn’t it rather the timid who start wars?”

So Baekta’s hand flinched and stopped for a moment, then began drawing again.

“Beware cowards. You never know what they might do.”

I asked him in a calm tone,

“You must have known that if you told the Cult Leader, it would reach my ears as well. Why did you reveal it?”

“The master I remember was not someone who would speak of our cult’s important affairs to outsiders.”

“Perhaps Master is the same. The you that Master remembers was surely not a disciple who would commit such absurd acts.”

He laughed in the bronze mirror.

“What is your real reason for doing this?”

“I wanted to receive your permission. If we are going to wage war anyway, whether it is approval or tacit consent, your permission has to be given, doesn’t it? If we simply went ahead with it, you might use that as justification to strike us. Aren’t you more than capable of doing exactly that?”

At last, So Baekta finished the Cheonhwa on his face and turned around.

“Permit our war.”

I could tell from his confident eyes. Even if we did not permit it, he would start the war.

What has made you so excited? What has filled you with such confidence? What has deluded you into thinking that our cult’s blade cannot pierce the sandstorms of the Outer Territories?

“Very well. I will state our official position.”

If I wanted to see his card, I would have to push him hard.

In a cold and authoritative tone, I said to him,

“Our cult does not permit your war.”

An unknowable heat rose in So Baekta’s eyes.

I poured cold water over that heat.

“So go wash your face and wipe it all off.”

Chapter 322: You Have Not Failed.
---------------------------------
A savage aura erupted from So Baekta’s body.

The words that their cult would not permit his war did not anger him so much. He had expected them.

But being told to go wash his face filled him with an indescribable sense of humiliation. It had struck directly at his pride.

‘Are you really that strong?’

Ghostly wails rang out in every direction as he pressed down on Geom Mugeuk with sinister ghost qi.

It was a demonic art that had yet to reach full mastery, but it was the Angcheon Great Demonic Qi.

The Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art activated, shielding Geom Mugeuk’s body. When Geom Mugeuk stood there with a calm expression, So Baekta drew up even more internal energy.

‘Are you truly that strong?’

He truly was strong. Far from being suppressed by the ghost qi, he began striding toward So Baekta instead.

“What exactly do you have that makes you cause such a commotion? This isn’t some back-alley thug snatching a courtesan house. What do you possess that makes you speak of unifying the lands beyond the border and dream of unifying the murim?”

Geom Mugeuk came right up before So Baekta.

“I doubt that this distracting pattern on your face is going to lead a war to victory.”

The very moment a certain resolve flashed through So Baekta’s eyes, another of his cards was turned over.

A vivid crimson energy flowed from his two eyes and began to merge with the ghost qi from before.

In that instant, Geom Mugeuk understood.

‘That energy is not the Angcheon Great Demonic Qi!’

The next moment, the crimson energy engulfed Geom Mugeuk, and his surroundings changed.

The place where Geom Mugeuk stood was no longer the Cult Leader’s Hall.

All around him, the walls and floor writhed as if alive. This place, which felt as though he were inside the entrails of some beast, was somewhere he had been before.

Yes, the space created by Hwanyeo, the woman of the flower garden!

But it felt different from the space Hwanyeo had made. The color was different, and so was the form. He could sense both the aura of Cult Leader Pungcheon’s Angcheon Great Demonic Qi and the aura he had once felt from Hwanyeo.

‘The two martial arts have been combined!’

The combined energy was even more powerful. Of course, the one who had allowed the two martial arts to be combined was most likely the Phantom King.

The reason So Baekta had seemed to change was not because he had fallen victim to soul-bewitching arts. It was because the merging of two powerful martial arts had affected his character. On top of that, the Phantom King’s persuasion and brainwashing must have been added.

The Phantom King had provided his own martial art and made him stronger?

Absolutely not. If it produced such an effect, he would have stolen So Baekta’s martial art and absorbed it himself.

‘The end of this combined martial art is death.’

Geom Mugeuk was certain. The kind of man the Phantom King was had already been shown in the life before his regression. He was a man who lived solely for himself.

At the front of this space stood the Ten-Thousand-Li Battle Wall, from which a human face would protrude.

From the opposite side, someone’s face suddenly pushed out.

“Young Cult Leader, you asked me to show you, did you not?”

The face protruding from behind the thin membrane was So Baekta’s. His voice echoed eerily.

“I may not be able to kill you, since you have cultivated the Guhwa Demonic Art. But I can imprison you here forever.”

This was where his confidence in unifying the murim had come from. From this powerful martial art that made him believe he could even imprison the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.

Naturally, Geom Mugeuk could also guess his final card. If the Phantom King had passed on his martial art, then So Baekta would also believe that he would help him. And if the Phantom King himself aided this combined martial art?

Only now could Geom Mugeuk understand his excitement and confidence.

“What martial art is this?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, So Baekta answered with confidence.

“The Angcheon Blood Command Art!”

It radiated an energy powerful enough for him to be so confident. But this was no different from taking Rampage, the unstable internal energy amplifier the Evil Alliance had sold before.

“Do you not know that if you become stronger in this way, you must inevitably pay a price?”

So Baekta laughed. In the expression revealed on the undulating membrane, there was not the slightest trace of unease.

“Shouldn’t you be the ones paying the price? You, who have grown complacent by trusting only in the Guhwa Demonic Art! Well? Have you seen enough of my ability now?”

Geom Mugeuk gave no answer. As he sank into thought, So Baekta asked him,

“What are you thinking so hard about?”

How can I get you to tell me about the Phantom King?

“I was wondering how I might get out of here.”

When Geom Mugeuk drew the Black Demon Sword, So Baekta sneered.

“The membrane looks thin, so you think it will tear easily?”

So Baekta, shaking the membrane with a leisurely laugh, had no way of knowing.

To Geom Mugeuk’s eyes, which had invoked the Blood-Eye Essence and the Divine Eye Art, a bluish line that could break through the membrane was visible.

Of course, not every method of breaking through could be found so easily. As in his past battle with the Soul-Bewitching Demon Venerable, there were illusions and sorceries whose weaknesses could only be found after crossing dangers where life and death hung in the balance.

But at the very least, the method to cut through the membrane of the Ten-Thousand-Li Battle Wall was clearly visible to Geom Mugeuk’s eyes.

Swish!

The Black Demon Sword lightly sliced through the air.

Riiiiip.

The membrane tore as if cloth were being ripped. Because of that, So Baekta, who had thrust his face into the membrane, suddenly had his head plunged inside.

His eyes flew wide in shock. Geom Mugeuk had even cut the membrane without leaving so much as a scratch on his face.

“How?”

Geom Mugeuk thrust his hand in, seized So Baekta by the collar on the other side of the wall, and pulled him inside.

So Baekta was dragged into that place.

Looking down at him as he lay sprawled on the floor, Geom Mugeuk said coldly,

“You said I would never get out of here? Are you confident you can spend the rest of your life with me?”

So Baekta reflexively dispelled the space. The space vanished, and it seemed as though they were returning to the Cult Leader’s Hall.

Ssssst.

They returned to the space they had originally been in.

“!”

So Baekta was startled.

He had recited the formula to erase the space, and he had clearly returned to the Cult Leader’s Hall. And yet he had been pulled back into that space again, as if dragged there.

He recited the formula once more. But the walls and floor merely heaved and surged; the space he had created did not disappear.

‘Something has gone wrong!’

Inwardly, he panicked. Ever since he had learned how to create this space, he had never once failed to leave it.

His gaze turned toward the torn wall.

‘Is it because of that?’

Looking around, he saw that it was similar to the space he had made, but different. The color was different, and so was the feeling.

‘Because that was torn, something changed!’

An inexplicable ominousness made So Baekta’s heart sink.

“Calm yourself and try again.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, he tried again, but still the space did not disappear.

So Baekta ran to the Ten-Thousand-Li Battle Wall and tried to leave again through the place Geom Mugeuk had torn. But behind the torn membrane, a new membrane had formed.

He poured true qi into it and tried to rip it apart, but it only stretched. It was completely useless.

So Baekta turned to Geom Mugeuk and shouted,

“You tear that wall again.”

Geom Mugeuk drew his sword and struck down at the membrane. But the membrane did not tear. Whether he stabbed or slashed it, the membrane only stretched and undulated.

“How did you tear it before?”

“I don’t know either. Seeing your face mocking me made me angry, so I tore it. Does a face have to be thrust in from that side for it to tear?”

Geom Mugeuk asked in return, but So Baekta gave no answer. He did not know either.

“We’ll be able to get out somehow. Surely we won’t be trapped here?”

Unlike Geom Mugeuk, who was somewhat at ease, So Baekta’s fear was growing larger and larger.

That carefree man did not know how terrifying this place was. If they could not erase the space, they would starve to death here.

“Is your internal energy being consumed?”

So Baekta checked his own internal energy, then shook his head.

Geom Mugeuk sank down against the wall and said,

“That is fortunate, at least. Otherwise, your innate true qi would be exhausted and you would die. Rest, then try again.”

Geom Mugeuk was right. This was not a problem that could be solved by getting agitated.

So Baekta calmly examined the wall. The movements of the wall, writhing like entrails, were subsiding, and its color was slowly darkening. The wall was dying.

“You combined another demonic art with a formidable one like the Angcheon Great Demonic Qi. Did you think there would be no side effects?”

That was right. So Baekta had believed there would be no side effects. There had been none until now.

So Baekta sat down and began circulating his qi and regulating his breath. He intended to calm his mind, replenish his internal energy, and try again with a fresh state of mind.

When he completed one circuit of qi and opened his eyes, Geom Mugeuk was examining the wall.

So Baekta calmly recited the formula to erase the space again. But it was still a failure.

It was then.

Drip, drip, drip.

Blood began to flow down from the walls on all sides.

As if announcing that death was near, the blood running down the walls did not stop.

Startled, So Baekta looked around and fell into despair.

“There’s nowhere for the blood to drain.”

Blood began to pool on the floor. At this rate, the blood would rise, and they would eventually drown in it.

He was going to drown in blood inside an illusion he had created? And together with the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult, no less?

So Baekta was so dumbfounded and appalled that he stood blankly for a moment.

Then, all at once, fierce hostility surged up inside him. So Baekta drew up his internal force. Crimson energy began to gather in both his hands.

“This is all because of you!”

So Baekta stretched out his hands to release astral qi toward Geom Mugeuk, but Geom Mugeuk was a step faster. Rushing in with the Dark Shadow Step, he struck.

Thud!

So Baekta, hit in the chest, was sent flying into the wall before collapsing to the floor.

Geom Mugeuk grabbed him as he lay submerged in blood, hauled him up, and sealed his internal energy. Before he knew it, the blood had risen to their knees.

“Why is it because of me? It’s because of you, swaggering about wanting to unite the lands beyond the border when you can’t even erase something you made yourself.”

Geom Mugeuk mercilessly struck the back of So Baekta’s head.

“That was for your master! Your master said this after returning from meeting you. ‘Good grief, I should have smacked that madman in the back of the head.’”

So Baekta clenched his teeth. He was enraged to the tips of his hair, but now was not the time to fight Geom Mugeuk. The blood was rising quickly.

So Baekta felt fear as he watched the blood climb. It was the first time he had ever felt the terror of death.

In the midst of that, Geom Mugeuk provoked him.

“If your master handed over the position of cult leader to you, you should have thought about protecting it. You couldn’t even hold on to the blessing you were given, and yet you thought you’d do what?”

“Blessing? What blessing?”

Being told he had been blessed was like touching his reverse scale.

“I was always going to inherit the position of cult leader!”

“Isn’t it good that you inherited it earlier?”

“Good? I wasn’t ready to become cult leader back then. It was a position I should have inherited after training further and reaching full mastery of the Angcheon Great Demonic Qi. But my master threw the position of cult leader at me and left.”

The things he had buried deep in his heart came bursting out.

“He said that many people followed him, so I need not worry? Don’t make me laugh! Those people followed Master, not me. Why did he not know that? Why didn’t he know that what they said in front of him and what they said in front of me were different?”

The blood had already risen to their stomachs.

“Do you know what I was like after Master left? Every day was like sitting on a bed of thorns. Was someone aiming for my position? Were they looking down on me in their hearts? I couldn’t even sleep properly for fear of assassination. And then what? Everyone else can say that, but you, of all people, should not? You don’t even know how I lived.”

Geom Mugeuk understood. The flashes of childish recklessness he had sensed in him had begun with resentment toward his master.

He should have placed “thanks to” where he had put “because of,” endured, and overcome it. Unfortunately, he had failed to do so.

“He was the only one who understood me.”

The Phantom King must have wormed his way into the gaps left by his resentment, anger, and fear.

So Baekta wanted to say this: that the man was a hundred times better than his damn master.

“He said he was the last descendant of the Blood Cult.”

That was not wrong. After the Phantom King and Hwanyeo, no more martial arts of the Blood Cult appeared.

“He said he had intended to live quietly, but when he saw me abandoned by my master and ascending to the position of cult leader, his heart changed.”

At a person’s weakest moment, the Phantom King must have pretended to become their greatest source of strength. By teaching him the demonic arts of the Blood Cult, he would have melted his heart.

So Baekta looked at his own face reflected in the blood that had now risen to his chest. Then, suddenly using the blood as a mirror, he dipped his fingers into it and began to draw the final pattern on his face.

Of the three patterns, it was Punghwa, the one drawn on the face of the dead. Of course, it did not draw well with blood.

“I wanted to go to the battlefield wearing the Cheonhwa he drew for me.”

The moment Geom Mugeuk heard those words, he understood.

“Don’t tell me that man was the painter in the Cult Leader’s Hall?”

Perhaps thinking this was the end, So Baekta nodded.

“He was the one who taught me how to draw the patterns on my face.”

At last, the moment had come when the Phantom King’s hiding place was revealed.

“He was the one who told me how I should live, and how extraordinary a person I was.”

The blood had now risen to just beneath their chins.

‘Am I going to die now?’

Suddenly, So Baekta’s eyes met Geom Mugeuk’s. In Geom Mugeuk’s clear, deep gaze, there was an unknowable elation.

“What are you so happy about that you’re smiling? What’s so impressive that you’re acting superior to the very end?”

“If it were my usual self, I would indeed have acted superior quite a lot, but today I held back.”

What followed was even more incomprehensible.

“Your attempts to erase the space did not fail. I was the one who succeeded.”

“What?”

“Now that I think about it, in this place, I suppose I would be your junior disciple.”

“What nonsense are you spouting?”

“He taught it only to me, so there is no way you would know.”

The blood had risen to their faces, and they had to stand on tiptoe to converse.

“There is a motion your master always makes whenever he unfolds and folds this place.”

Geom Mugeuk thrust his hand up out of the blood.

Instinctively, So Baekta felt that something had gone wrong. But that thought lasted only a moment, for the blood rose above both their faces.

At that very moment, as they sank into the blood, Geom Mugeuk snapped the fingers of the hand he had held outside.

Chapter 323: If You Want to Compare, Do It Like This.
-----------------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk and So Baekta stood in the Cult Leader’s Hall.

The hellish world within the illusion, where blood had been rising all around them, vanished in an instant.

So Baekta stared at Geom Mugeuk with a vacant expression.

Until just moments ago, blood had been pouring into his nose and mouth. He had despaired, thinking that this was truly the end.

‘Did we die?’

But this moment was far too vivid for him to think he was dead.

So Baekta touched his own face, then looked around again. He was not dead. Geom Mugeuk, standing calmly before him, was unmistakably alive as well.

“……How did this happen?”

Earlier, when Geom Mugeuk erased the space So Baekta had created with the Angcheon Blood Spirit Art and returned to the Cult Leader’s Hall, he had cast the Space-Time Illusory Shift Art and created a space that closely resembled the place they had just been in.

Because So Baekta had no idea that Geom Mugeuk could use the Space-Time Illusory Shift Art, he naturally thought they had returned to the space he himself had made.

From the moment the Space-Time Illusory Shift Art was cast, every word and action of Geom Mugeuk’s had been an act.

It had been for the sole purpose of uncovering one fact, and now he had discovered who the Illusion King was.

Unaware of that inner truth, So Baekta could not understand this situation at all.

“I am asking what you did.”

The mission was not yet over. Resolving the conflict between So Baekta and Cult Leader Pungcheon was just as important as killing the Illusion King. No, to Geom Mugeuk, it was even more important.

“I am the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

He did not say that he had learned the Space-Time Illusory Shift Art from Cult Leader Pungcheon. So Baekta already harbored deep resentment toward his master, and saying such a thing would only wound his feelings further.

“You are saying you unraveled it?”

Because So Baekta did not know that Geom Mugeuk’s goal had been to reveal who the Illusion King was, he paid no mind to what he had said about the painter. Right now, what So Baekta wanted to know was how they had escaped from that space.

“You said we had grown complacent, relying only on the Nine Flames Demonic Art? Who in the world told you that? Whoever said such a thing must have never met me or my father—must never have even come to our cult—so why do you believe that person’s words?”

It struck the very heart of the matter, so So Baekta could not answer.

“For now, come with me.”

“Where are you saying we should go?”

“Is there not something you must resolve with your master?”

As he had been sinking beneath the blood, he had poured out the feelings he held toward his master.

But when he thought of actually meeting his master and saying those things, So Baekta’s chest grew tight. He had spoken of his master because he had been facing death; he did not have the confidence to meet him in person and reveal his heart.

“There is nothing to resolve with my master.”

Geom Mugeuk did not force him. Once the parties involved actually met, their feelings might be different from how they were now. What mattered was taking him there somehow.

“Very well. It is between the two of you, so I will not interfere. Still, let us go. There is something I must say while everyone is present.”

So Baekta still had questions about how they had escaped from the illusion, so he nodded.

“Let us do that.”

And so he left the Cult Leader’s Hall together with So Baekta.

* * *

“These are swords your Young Cult Leader bought for you.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon handed out the swords he had bought from Sinung Ironworks to the guards. He did not take credit for them. For Geom Mugeuk, who had come all the way here for his sake, he said they had been purchased by him.

“Where is the Young Cult Leader?”

“He stopped by the Cult Leader’s Hall. He told me to bring these to you first.”

After inspecting the swords, the guards were greatly delighted. For a martial artist, what gift could be better than a weapon? Moreover, the swords they had received were better than the ones they already owned.

“Famed swords and treasured swords are all well and good, but as a rule, the best weapon is the one that fits your own hand. The only problem is that, in life, there comes at least once a time when that best weapon breaks, and you must fight with another sword. So until you reach the point where you can pick up a branch fallen on the road and fight with it like your Young Cult Leader, train with all sorts of swords. A martial artist is one who prepares in advance. A martial artist with many worries is one who lives long.”

He spoke as if offering words of blessing, but they were words imbued with Cult Leader Pungcheon’s insight.

The guards all cupped their fists at once and expressed their gratitude.

Leaving them behind, Cult Leader Pungcheon entered his residence.

Mabul was drinking tea while looking over the drawings of poisonous herbs that the Poison King had given him.

“Do you truly intend to dig those up?”

“When we finish matters here and return, I plan to try digging them up. If the weather grows any colder, there are quite a few that cannot be harvested.”

Because Cult Leader Pungcheon knew very well what kind of person Mabul had originally been, he could not get used to seeing him like this.

“I did not know you had become this close with the Poison King.”

“That is not why.”

Mabul said something unexpected.

“Have you ever tried something new? I do not mean the things you repeated while living as Cult Leader Pungcheon. I mean something entirely new.”

Of course he had. He had personally selected people to hire and bought a secret map. Most of what he had done while traveling through the Central Plains to create Gowol and Eunwol had been new.

“For me, it was digging up medicinal herbs. The joy I felt when I found the herb I was searching for was something I had never experienced before in my life.”

And because of the poisonous herb he had dug up, the Poison King had truly rejoiced. Had there ever been a time when someone had been this happy because of his actions?

Amazingly, that had been the first time. He had never seen it from his family, nor had he experienced it while living as a martial artist. It had been the same even when he became a Demon Venerable. Would anyone ever have had cause to rejoice like that because of him?

“I lived my entire life making others fear me……”

That was why he had not known. He had not known what it felt like to make someone happy. To think that he could feel greater joy from another’s happiness than from the joy of gaining something himself.

For a while, the two of them were each lost in thought. Cult Leader Pungcheon thought that if anyone could understand Mabul’s feelings best, it would be himself.

“How did the matter you went out for turn out?”

Cult Leader Pungcheon told Mabul what had happened at Sinung Ironworks.

“Your disciple has been rather busy all this time.”

“Who is there to blame?”

“You were always the sort to blame others, were you not?”

“True. And you were always the sort to say hateful things so well.”

The two of them looked at each other and smiled. Now, even such barbed words felt unfamiliar. If only one of them had changed, they would not have been able to laugh like this. But both of them had changed, and both were satisfied with that change.

The person who had exerted the decisive influence on both of their changes was the same.

“The Young Cult Leader?”

Cult Leader Pungcheon looked far out the window toward the Cult Leader’s Hall and answered.

“He went to that disciple of mine. He went with his heart steeled, so by now he must be looking into the black hole in my disciple’s heart.”

* * *

Geom Mugeuk and So Baekta entered the residence.

Seeing that his disciple had come with him, Cult Leader Pungcheon instinctively realized that Geom Mugeuk had uncovered something.

“Master.”

“Welcome, Cult Leader.”

Unlike the last time they met, So Baekta treated him awkwardly. Certain that something had happened, Cult Leader Pungcheon sent a voice transmission to Geom Mugeuk.

—Did you discover the hidden card my disciple had kept?

—I discovered even who was behind him.

As expected of the Young Cult Leader, he could not help but admire him. Then again, even he himself had been helplessly taken in, so how could his disciple have handled it?

—Why did you bring my disciple here?

—Because it seemed there was something he needed to resolve with you, Cult Leader. However, he will likely not open his heart easily.

Mabul tried to give them privacy.

“I will step out for a while.”

Then Geom Mugeuk spoke to him.

“Lord Mabul, please stay as well.”

Right now, So Baekta wanted someone to acknowledge the pain he had suffered. He had already told Geom Mugeuk, so now Geom Mugeuk thought Mabul’s presence would help him open his mouth. Sometimes, more than the person directly involved, one needed a third party to watch and bear witness.

And so Geom Mugeuk, So Baekta, Cult Leader Pungcheon, and Mabul sat around the table.

“What exactly are you planning to say that you brought me here?”

“First, I believe you should say to your master what you said to me.”

So Baekta’s expression stiffened at once.

“I clearly said there is nothing I need to resolve with my master.”

“Then your master will live his whole life without knowing how you truly feel. Is that what you want?”

Of course that was not what So Baekta wanted. But even so, he did not feel inclined to tell his master what he had felt back then. He did not want to look like a child whining to his master, and he no longer had any desire to look good in his eyes.

At that moment, Cult Leader Pungcheon spoke first.

“Did you resent me?”

“No. I do not resent you. What is there to resent? Rather, I should be grateful that you chose me as the Cult Leader.”

Everyone could feel it. A deep resentment lay within So Baekta’s words. Right now, he was saying this:

I have resented you all along, Master.

Geom Mugeuk was curious. How would Cult Leader Pungcheon react?

Cult Leader Pungcheon had resolved that if his disciple had killed Elder Seong, he would never forgive him. In this situation, when Elder Seong’s life or death was still unknown, would he truly be able to treat him gently?

After looking at So Baekta in silence for a long while, Cult Leader Pungcheon said quietly,

“I am sorry, my disciple.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon apologized sincerely.

Instead, So Baekta was flustered. He had not expected his master to apologize like this.

“Why are you apologizing? I told you, I do not resent you.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon looked at him without a word.

The moment their gazes met, emotion surged up within So Baekta. That gaze, as if it knew everything in his heart! It was precisely a gaze like that that made a person furious.

“Does everyone not simply live their own life?”

If it had been the old Cult Leader Pungcheon, he would have roared. He would have shouted, asking what sort of pathetic whining this was, saying that what he had received was the position of supreme ruler beyond the borders. He would have said that such a position was not gained so easily without any pain or sacrifice.

But Cult Leader Pungcheon apologized again.

“I am sorry.”

Geom Mugeuk could feel that Cult Leader Pungcheon had grown. So Baekta did not know. He did not know how difficult it was to make such an apology at this moment. Separate from the outcome of what he had done to Elder Seong, what required an apology had to be apologized for.

“Why do you keep saying you are sorry? I said I feel nothing!”

So Baekta shot to his feet, about to storm out.

At that moment, Cult Leader Pungcheon said,

“I had no intention of advancing into the Central Plains. I liked the lands beyond the borders.”

“Then why did you speak so much about advancing into the Central Plains?”

“Yes, I sang endlessly of advancing into the Central Plains. That was because my true feelings and my mouth were moving separately.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon let out a sigh.

“Because I, too, lived my whole life hearing only those words, just like you. My master, and my master’s master, all lived with the words that advancing into the Central Plains was our cherished desire. And so, before I knew it, it became a desire that was not truly my desire.”

“Is that the end of it? It is always like that. Master, you were always someone who thought only of yourself. After leaving for the Central Plains, you say you liked the lands beyond the borders? Do you call that something worth saying now?”

Geom Mugeuk felt it. In this moment, So Baekta was thinking of the Illusion King. He was comparing his master to him.

Now was the time for him to step forward. Unless So Baekta learned the truth about the Illusion King, this reconciliation could never take place.

“That painter is someone who approached the Cult Leader deliberately.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, So Baekta flinched in surprise. But soon he responded coldly.

“Do not speak nonsense.”

Geom Mugeuk did not try to persuade him emotionally. He conveyed only the facts.

“Not only you, but our cult and the Murim Alliance as well were infiltrated by these people.”

“!”

Because the words were shocking, So Baekta was startled.

“These are events that have already occurred, so they can be verified.”

“I cannot believe it.”

“On our side, they targeted the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable. On the Murim Alliance’s side, they targeted the Alliance Leader’s grandson. I will have the facts officially delivered through our cult’s Tongcheongak.”

As even specific details were mentioned, So Baekta grew shaken.

“Why do you think I came here? As you said, the Cult Leader or Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult had never formally visited the Pungcheon Cult, so why would I personally come all this way?”

At the end of the continuing truth, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“Cult Leader, have you ever once doubted him?”

Of course he had not. In that instant, his heart sank. Come to think of it, he had never once doubted that man. He had come to know him naturally, then came to trust him, and that had led him to this point.

So Baekta tried hard to deny Geom Mugeuk’s words.

“What scheme are you trying to play now?”

He was the sort to suspect others first like this, so why had he not suspected that man?

“The one who played a scheme was him. He dug into your resentment toward your master and won your heart. If my words are false, then by the authority of the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, I will permit your unification of the lands beyond the borders. No, we will support your unification.”

So Baekta was astonished.

“Are you sincere?”

“I am.”

Geom Mugeuk revealed his honest feelings.

“Do you know why I am proceeding with this matter in such a troublesome way? I could simply press your sleeping acupoint, put you to sleep, and deal with him.”

It was true. His internal energy was still sealed. If Geom Mugeuk put him to sleep and the three people here went after him, he would not be able to handle them.

“Why are you doing this?”

“Your master said this. Even if he could return to the past, he would still make you his successor. He said there was no one but you.”

“!”

“I am not doing this for you. I want to prove that he chose his disciple well. I do not want this matter to leave him with a wound in his heart for the rest of his life. For his sake, I can endure even greater trouble.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Cult Leader Pungcheon was inwardly overcome with emotion. It moved him more deeply than anything Geom Mugeuk had ever said to him. It was a moment when the distance between the two of them narrowed by another step.

As for So Baekta, he looked at Cult Leader Pungcheon with subtle and complicated feelings.

“You said that person told you how you should live, and how extraordinary a person you were?”

Geom Mugeuk remembered exactly what he had said inside the Space-Time Illusory Shift Art.

“You should not compare that person and your master in that way. When someone needs someone to lean on, it is not difficult to become a hero to them. If you are going to compare them, compare what that person can give up for you. Your master gave up the position of Cult Leader. The moment he learned that you were in danger, he rushed all the way here. Now then, as for what that person can give up, and what he can do for you—test him yourself.”

Only now did So Baekta understand Geom Mugeuk’s intention. He meant for So Baekta to uncover that man’s true identity himself, out of the hope that he and his master would reconcile.

“What if I pretend to test that man while secretly informing him of all this by voice transmission?”

Then you would die by my hand together with that villain.

Instead of conveying that cold reality, Geom Mugeuk looked at Cult Leader Pungcheon and spoke calmly.

“What I trust is not you, but your master. At the very least, I do not believe he would have passed the position of Cult Leader to someone who was a fool to the very end.”

Chapter 324: Just for Today, Think of Me as the Enemy.
------------------------------------------------------
So Baekta could not make a decision right away.

Instead, he asked for time alone with his master. That was exactly what Geom Mugeuk had wanted.

When Geom Mugeuk and Mabul went outside, only the Pungcheon Cult Leader and So Baekta remained there.

The one who broke the long silence was So Baekta.

“Even if you could go back, would you truly make me the next Cult Leader?”

“Yes.”

“Even though I dream of unifying the martial world?”

“Yes. Even so, I would make you the next Cult Leader.”

Despite the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s firm answer, So Baekta still doubted him.

“Why would you do that?”

“Because I believe you would protect the Pungcheon Cult better than anyone.”

“Is starting a war what you call protecting it?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader conveyed to his disciple the remorse and regret he had felt.

“If I could go back, I would have asked you what your dream was. And if, by any chance, you answered that it was to advance into the Central Plains, I would have told you this: that dream is not our dream, but a dream someone among our predecessors once had. Then I would have walked the wasteland with you and shown you how wonderful the lands beyond the frontier are, and how strong the people who live amid these sandstorms truly are. Because I believe that you, more than anyone, could protect and nurture these lands beyond the frontier.”

So Baekta grew angry.

“You say that only now? If you had not returned like this, it is something you still would never have told me, isn’t it?”

“Because I would have believed you were doing well. Because I did not know this was how you felt. Had I known, I would have come running long ago.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader gazed at his disciple and said,

“That is why I said I was sorry. I will keep saying it until your heart is completely eased.”

Their gazes became entangled in midair. Beyond the window behind them, the sandstorm was blowing.

* * *

Geom Mugeuk and Mabul stood side by side in the courtyard, looking up at the sky.

It felt like only yesterday that he had lain on a rock with him and looked up at the sky, and now they had come all the way to these distant lands beyond the frontier and were looking at the sky together.

Mabul suddenly said,

“That fellow’s fate will change today as well.”

He must have meant So Baekta, but his fate would also have a great effect on the Pungcheon Cult Leader. That was why Geom Mugeuk hoped the relationship between those two would come to a good end, in whatever form it took.

“I am worried about the Cult Leader.”

At that, Mabul, still looking up at the sky, said,

“Young Cult Leader, don’t get cheeky! That sort of worrying is what an elder does for you.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled faintly.

“Like how you worry about me, Mabul?”

“I only worry about your older brother.”

Those cold words made him even more grateful. After all, Mabul was still taking good care of his brother, unchanged.

“You said my brother brought me up while drinking?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Mabul nodded.

“He did.”

“Not that he was thinking about how to take the Young Cult Leader position back from me?”

“I found myself wishing he had been.”

Mabul’s eyes, as he looked at Geom Mugeuk, held concern.

“When a person suddenly loses their goal, their life is bound to waver. Even if he acts calm on the outside, he must be having a hard time. So don’t be arrogant and worry about your elders. Take good care of your brother instead.”

“As expected, Mabul, you are a little giant to me.”

“More nonsense!”

Mabul turned his gaze away with an unnecessarily awkward expression, but Geom Mugeuk knew that the words “little giant” were what he liked most. In the past, that giant had stood tall through flattery, but now the words were spoken with sincerity. To Geom Mugeuk, Mabul was a little giant.

He felt that Mabul, having emerged from his cave, was now beginning to build his own house. By taking care of his brother, he laid the floor; by agreeing to go to the lands beyond the frontier with him, he raised the pillars; by promising to gather medicinal herbs, he set the roof in place. In that way, he was building his own house.

When that house was complete, Geom Mugeuk sincerely hoped he would invite his brother, invite the Pungcheon Cult Leader, invite the Poison King, and even invite him as well, and hold a small banquet. He sincerely hoped Mabul would live a golden life.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze followed Mabul’s back up toward the sky.

Just then, Jeokyeon and the other guards approached and expressed their gratitude.

“Thank you for the swords you gave us as gifts!”

“Why are you thanking me?”

“We heard the Young Cult Leader gave them to us as presents.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“The Pungcheon Cult Leader bought them for you.”

“Ah! The Cult Leader said the Young Cult Leader bought them.”

“He must not have wanted to take credit for it. When you see him later, be sure to thank him!”

After they withdrew, Mabul laughed.

“Why are you laughing?”

“The money may have come from that man Pungcheon, but the one who meant to buy them was you, wasn’t it?”

As expected, Mabul was frighteningly quick-witted.

“Why do you think so?”

“Because he is not the sort of person to look after even your guards. No matter how much he may have changed, people do not change that much all at once. He must have paid while grumbling.”

Just as Geom Mugeuk was smiling, the Pungcheon Cult Leader and So Baekta walked out.

So Baekta came up to Geom Mugeuk and said,

“It seems the time has come to confirm whether I am a fool to the very end or not.”

Geom Mugeuk released the internal energy he had suppressed and said,

“Until now, you must have seen him only as someone on your side, so at least for today, think of him as an enemy and take a look.”

* * *

The Illusion King was painting.

He looked exactly like Hwan Yeo, and was tall, manly, and broad in appearance.

But the painting he was working on did not suit his appearance at all.

It was a dark and grotesque painting, filled with the aura of death.

There was a gate reminiscent of the gates of hell, and around it were corpses and beasts that had died gruesome deaths.

But the grotesque part began now.

The painting moved. From a small path beside the gate, a man drawn in the painting walked out, looked this way, and spoke.

—All preparations are complete.

His voice could be heard only by the Illusion King.

—However, he says the Young Cult Leader must not be touched yet.

—Any other mention?

—None. He mentioned only the Young Cult Leader.

—Is it certain that the one who killed Hwan Yeo was Pungcheon?

—Judging by the circumstances, yes.

When the Illusion King nodded, the man who had finished his report disappeared back down the path he had come from.

Just then, someone’s presence was heard outside, and So Baekta entered.

“Master.”

He was calling the Illusion King his master. The Illusion King rose from his seat and bowed respectfully to So Baekta.

“Welcome, Cult Leader.”

“What sort of painting were you working on today?”

The Illusion King showed him the painting. When So Baekta saw it, he flinched in surprise.

“It is a different style from your usual paintings.”

Normally, he mostly painted the beautiful scenery of the lands beyond the frontier. He painted smiling people, dogs, and cats. But today’s painting was far too different.

“Sometimes I feel like painting things like this as well.”

Why, of all days, had he been painting something like this on the day So Baekta came to see him?

“Where does this gate lead?”

“To my hell.”

At that meaningful answer, So Baekta looked at the Illusion King.

But only the painting was different; the Illusion King, with a good-natured smile, served him tea as usual.

So Baekta toyed with the teacup and recalled what Geom Mugeuk had said.

—If there is tea or liquor he gives you, there is probably some kind of drug mixed into that as well.

It was one of the few things Geom Mugeuk had told him before he came here. Thinking back, whenever he came here, the Illusion King had always served him tea.

At first, his guards had tested it for poison, but after he learned martial arts from the Illusion King, that process had been omitted. In the end, he had been drinking it all this time without suspecting at all what might be inside.

If, as Geom Mugeuk said, he regarded the other party as an enemy? It was enough to send a chill down his spine.

“Please drink. A new tea came in, and it tastes very good.”

“The fragrance is excellent.”

So Baekta merely savored the aroma, then set the tea down and continued speaking.

“Earlier, the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult came to see me alone, so I told him to allow me to unite the lands beyond the frontier.”

“What did he say to that?”

“He told me to show him my ability. He said he would see whether I had the qualifications for it. So I used the Angcheon Blood Spirit Art and showed him my power.”

At that, the Illusion King’s expression hardened.

“Did I not tell you to avoid using the Angcheon Blood Spirit Art whenever possible?”

“I had no choice.”

So Baekta imitated what Geom Mugeuk had said.

‘Our cult does not permit it. So go wash your face and come back. What an insolent Young Cult Leader!’

Unable to hold back his anger, So Baekta struck the table where the tea sat. The table shattered, and the tea spilled.

“If I ever strike the Central Plains, I want to sweep away the Demonic Cult first.”

The Illusion King calmly wiped and cleaned the place, then brewed tea again. In the past, So Baekta would have felt sorry and grateful in such a situation, but now his feelings were different. Just as Geom Mugeuk had said, he was looking at him as an enemy.

‘Is he trying to make me drink the tea to the very end?’

It was the same action, but the feeling with which he received it was completely different.

Setting the tea down on a new tea table, the Illusion King asked. The fact that he was persistent yet did not force it made him even more frightening.

“So what happened?”

“The Young Cult Leader broke my Angcheon Blood Spirit Art.”

“That cannot be.”

“I experienced it myself.”

“Then you must have fallen for one of his tricks.”

At the Illusion King’s firm conclusion, So Baekta asked as if arguing,

“Why are you so certain? You have not personally experienced the Guhwa Demonic Art.”

For an instant, the Illusion King’s expression hardened. Even normally, whenever the Guhwa Demonic Art was mentioned, he showed discomfort, but today his face looked angrier than usual.

So Baekta quickly apologized to him.

“I am sorry, Master. Because the Angcheon Blood Spirit Art did not work, I behaved rudely and sensitively.”

Still with his expression stiff, the Illusion King asked,

“Why do you think the Young Cult Leader came to the lands beyond the frontier?”

“I do not know his true intentions.”

Of course, that was a lie. He had been told that Geom Mugeuk had come to capture the very person before his eyes.

Then the Illusion King struck at the heart of the matter.

“He came to capture me.”

So Baekta flinched in surprise. The Illusion King had revealed something he thought he would conceal.

“How would he know of your existence, Master?”

“I planted one of my people in the Demonic Cult. But recently, that person was captured, and my existence was exposed.”

“Why did you plant someone in the Demonic Cult?”

“Do you know why I am so sensitive about the Guhwa Demonic Art? It is because the Blood Cult fell to that Guhwa Demonic Art. Our cult and the Demonic Cult are irreconcilable enemies. No reconciliation can wipe away that blood. As the last descendant of the Blood Cult, how could I simply stand by and watch them?”

The perfect material for a lie is ninety percent truth.

Because he spoke while mixing in so many facts, So Baekta was inwardly confused. Could this side be right and the other side wrong? So Baekta tried in every way to discern the truth.

“I will absolutely not give up.”

“Of course you must not. You, Cult Leader, will surely accomplish your great undertaking.”

“But there is a problem. Since I rashly revealed that I learned another martial art, given my master’s personality, he will not simply let me be. Master, please help me.”

The Illusion King stared intently at So Baekta, then took out a medicine bottle smaller than a ring finger from his robe.

“Feed this medicine to your master! It is colorless and odorless, so if you mix it into liquor, he will never notice.”

So Baekta was startled. He did not have to ask to know. It was poison that would kill his master.

“It will not take effect immediately. The poison will activate after three days, so he will not suspect that it was a drug you added.”

He had never imagined the Illusion King would tell him to feed his master poison and kill him.

“They say a man who is not ruthless is no true man. If you do not kill him, Cult Leader, you will be the one who suffers.”

At that moment, So Baekta’s heart went cold. What could be more cowardly than mixing poison into liquor to kill an enemy?

Had he truly respected and liked such a person?

The Illusion King was reading his heart precisely.

“Are you disappointed in me?”

So Baekta answered honestly.

“I did not think you would tell me to mix poison into liquor.”

“To deal with him, you must fight a battle of life and death. If you add the drug, Cult Leader, it becomes a fight you will win unconditionally. Then should you not add it? Is there anything more precious than your life?”

After a brief pause, So Baekta asked in a cold gaze,

“Is this for my sake? Or is it to save yourself, Master?”

“We are already in the same boat. Is there a difference between the two?”

“That is true. Then the boat will not sink. Instead, I will become someone who poisoned his master to death over drinks.”

There was no need to compare any further. As if the curtain of the Ten-Thousand-Li Battle Wall had been torn apart, it was the moment the scales that had covered So Baekta’s eyes fell away.

Even so, the Illusion King was at ease.

“They have already planted seeds of doubt in our Cult Leader’s heart.”

“Seeds I should have planted and nurtured from the very beginning.”

The Illusion King’s gaze turned cold. With the courtesy and laughter gone, his face looked so unfamiliar it was like looking at another person.

“I knew you would be won over by them. But I never imagined it would happen this easily.”

He had already guessed why So Baekta had come to see him.

Telling him to slip poison in had been the Illusion King’s test. A test to see whether So Baekta had been won over by the Young Cult Leader.

So Baekta lamented his past foolishness.

“So you really did approach me on purpose.”

When he became Cult Leader, he had wanted to flee from the difficulties before him. The man in front of him had served as a refuge better than anyone else.

Together with him, he had cursed his master. All while being brainwashed with words that his master was a coward, but he would become a splendid Cult Leader. To a feeble heart, there was no greater comfort than someone who cursed the person you hated alongside you.

That place had not been a refuge. It had been a hell bearing the name of refuge.

‘Master!’

The person who now surfaced in So Baekta’s heart was Cult Leader Pungcheon.

‘I’m sorry.’

So Baekta secretly drew up his inner power. He knew the Illusion King was stronger than him, but he was not afraid at all. The excitement born of remorse was ruling him.

But the Illusion King’s illusion arts overwhelmed him.

Wooooooong!

With a deep reverberation, their surroundings changed.

The place where the two stood was no longer the Illusion King’s residence from moments ago.

Corpses lay scattered all around, and beasts that seemed as if they belonged in paintings growled from every direction.

And before them stood a great door.

‘That door?’

It was the very door the Illusion King had earlier said led to his hell.

Astonishingly, they were inside the painting the Illusion King had drawn.

“What are you planning to do?”

“What were you planning to do? Did you test me because you believed those people would protect you?”

At those words, So Baekta hurriedly turned his head.

Far away in the sky, Geom Mugeuk and Cult Leader Pungcheon, huge as giants, were looking at him, and beyond the horizon, Mabul, who had become a true giant, was also staring at him.

So Baekta understood. The three who had entered the residence were looking into the painting.

The Illusion King and he had not moved to this place; they had truly entered the painting.

Cult Leader Pungcheon was looking not at the Illusion King, but at him.

What filled his gaze was worry.

The moment he saw that gaze, So Baekta felt something knotted in his heart completely melt away.

‘……Master!’

He was so sorry to his master. He had failed to appreciate the gratitude of being handed the position of Cult Leader, and like an immature child, had only resented him until he ruined everything. Even so, his master had apologized to him again and again, saying he was sorry.

‘I ruined everything. Master, I’m sorry.’

As if this were the consequence of that foolishness, the door began to open.

Before the open door, the Illusion King said,

“Your master knows how dangerous this place is. It is a place where even Cult Leader Pungcheon would have to risk his life.”

When he stretched out his hand, So Baekta flew helplessly toward him as though being sucked in.

From beyond the door as it began to close, the Illusion King’s voice was heard.

“I wonder if your master will open this gate to hell to save you.”
