There was a time when I hated him greatly. Even so, I have no intention of changing this Cult Leader’s hall. Honestly, the previous one was quite outdated.”

He had properly taken his master’s plea to live for himself.

“I intend to live diligently here now.”

“I believe the Cult Leader will do well.”

“I am truly grateful for your help this time. If given the opportunity next time, I shall surely repay this kindness.”

“It was done for your master. There is no need to be grateful to me.”

“That is why I am even more grateful.”

Because it wasn’t only Geom Mugeuk who had grown closer to the Pungcheon Sect Leader through this matter.

“In that spirit, I wish to give the Young Cult Leader a gift.”

When Sobaekta manipulated the chair, the surrounding walls rose and divine artifacts were revealed.

“Choose one among our cult’s divine artifacts.”

“Are you serious?”

“I am.”

What was a divine artifact compared to that? He had saved his life and even restored his relationship with his master.

Even so, Geom Mugeuk found Sobaekta’s offer of a divine artifact unexpected.

“May I truly choose?”

“Of course. However, the Eumnoe Bell is not permitted.”

“I know that much. But does your master know?”

“Why? Must you receive permission from your master?”

“Not exactly. But do not regret this. I am not someone who refuses gifts.”

“Before I change my mind, hurry and choose what you want.”

Geom Mugeuk looked around at the divine artifacts. First, he went to the Eumnoe Bell and greeted the specters.

‘I will be leaving soon, so I suppose I won’t see you anymore.’

The specters of the Eumnoe Bell now felt like friends, just as the vengeful spirits of the Guhwamagong did.

‘Next time, I shall return with Gowol.’

Geom Mugeuk looked over the divine artifacts one by one. In truth, he had seen them all when he had received the Hyeolsindan and the Geukpum Cheonjamsa from the Pungcheon Sect Leader in the past.

If he were to choose another divine artifact, the first to catch his eye was the Hyeollyong Sinpo, a protective garment that, like the Pipung-ui, provided immunity to blades and swords when worn. If not that, there was also the Hwasodan, which could greatly increase internal energy, though its effect was less than the Hyeolsindan.

Geom Mugeuk slowly walked around and looked over the divine artifacts once more.

As he circled the hall along the wall, a faint vibration came from within his chest.

Hum!

Surprisingly, the secret coffer he kept in his bosom had vibrated slightly. This was the first time it had shown such a reaction since he obtained the secret coffer.

Geom Mugeuk could tell. That the secret coffer had reacted to the divine artifact before him now.

To confirm precisely, he stepped a short distance away and then returned. This time too, it vibrated faintly in front of that divine artifact.

The divine artifact that the secret coffer reacted to was a black iron bead slightly smaller than a fist.

“What kind of divine artifact is this?”

“It is called Heukjeong because it looks like a black eyeball.”

“What is it used for?”

“I do not know. It has been passed down in our cult since long ago.”

He had seen it before too, but it was something he had simply passed by.

Only because the secret coffer was close by could he know that these two items were related.

If this Heukjeong was the key to unlocking the secret of the secret coffer?

Then it made sense why the secret hadn’t been unlocked until now. Because among the past cult leaders of the Cheonma Singyo, none had come near the Heukjeong while possessing the secret coffer.

His heart pounded involuntarily with anticipation that he might be able to unravel the secret of the secret coffer.

“I will choose this.”

“Are you sure that will be alright? Past cult leaders conducted much research but concluded it was merely an iron bead.”

“And yet it remained in the seat of a divine artifact, did it not? That is what makes it seem all the more extraordinary.”

“I hadn’t expected you to interpret it that way.”

“I will go with this.”

Geom Mugeuk held the Heukjeong in his hand. Then, as if confirming this was correct, the iron coffer in his bosom vibrated once more.

“Thank you for this precious gift.”

Geom Mugeuk accepted this as fate. Obtaining the iron coffer, coming to the frontier, and receiving a divine artifact as a gift from him. If he had not saved him, he would not have obtained the Heukjeong.

Just then, the Pungcheon Sect Leader entered.

“What is that?”

Geom Mugeuk quickly pocketed the Heukjeong.

“Don’t tell me? You didn’t wring a divine artifact from my disciple too, did you?”

“I didn’t wring it; the Cult Leader gave it to me as a gift.”

“On whose authority!”

“Not the retired Cult Leader—the current Cult Leader here!”

Geom Mugeuk spoke hastily as he fled.

“From now on, when you curse me, this matter will be included!”

Sure enough, the Pungcheon Sect Leader shouted immediately.

“Stop, you shameless Young Cult Leader who plunders master and disciple alike!”

Of course, Geom Mugeuk left the Cult Leader’s hall without looking back.

The Pungcheon Sect Leader’s expression, which had been raising veins in anger at being left alone with just his disciple, relaxed gently.

“Thank you for listening to my request.”

Chapter 330: There’s Just One Thing I Can’t Do
----------------------------------------------
Amazingly, the reason So Baekta had given Geom Mugeuk a sacred relic as a gift was because the Pungcheon Cult Leader had asked him to.

“Do you know why I told you to give the Young Cult Leader a gift?”

“Was it not out of gratitude for what happened this time?”

“Of course, that is part of it, but there is a greater reason.”

“What is the reason?”

Then the Pungcheon Cult Leader said something unexpected.

“It is because of you, Cult Leader.”

“Because of me?”

It was to tie the thread of fate between Geom Mugeuk and So Baekta even more firmly. In truth, his own relationship with him had begun when he gave him a sacred relic as well.

Simply parting ways like this and expressing gratitude by gifting a sacred relic were entirely different things.

Even if the other party was not someone who wished for repayment, whether to display the virtue of refusal was, in the end, up to them. Even that became a gift in itself.

“Do not let go of your bond with the Young Cult Leader. For your own sake, and for the sake of the Pungcheon Cult.”

Only then did So Baekta understand his master’s profound intention. It was also a moment when he once again realized just how highly his master regarded Geom Mugeuk.

“If you wish to become a good Cult Leader, you must covet people more than objects. Even if you use up every sacred relic there, gather people around you who can protect you.”

“I understand.”

Then the Pungcheon Cult Leader stared intently at So Baekta and asked.

“Do you really? Do you truly believe people are more important than the sacred relics here?”

“Well…”

So Baekta could not give an answer filled with certainty. After all, until just yesterday, he had been toyed with by others.

“Yes, human relationships are wearisome. Forming ties, trying to maintain them, being disappointed, being betrayed. There is truly nothing that consumes so much mental strength. Even so, you must place seeing people and governing them above all else. Do you know why?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader was telling his disciple all the things he had never been able to say before.

“Because you are the Cult Leader. If you were to live an ordinary life, there would be no need to place such importance on human relationships. But a Cult Leader is ultimately someone who governs people. You must be able to deal not only with our cult, but also with organizations that become our allies, and even with our enemies. Do not forget that depending on what kind of people you keep by your side and what kind of people you use, the fate of the Pungcheon Cult and everyone living beyond the borders will change.”

Before So Baekta could answer, he quickly added.

“That is not something I, who spent my life staring only at sacred relics, should be saying, is it?”

“Is that not precisely why you are able to say it?”

The two looked at each other and smiled.

“By the way, what did the Young Cult Leader choose?”

“He chose the Heukjeong.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader looked as if he could not understand, but soon nodded. Choosing only the very best, and then choosing the most worthless thing of all. It truly felt like the Young Cult Leader.

“He is no one else but the Young Cult Leader. There is no need to feel it a waste. Just now, you were the one who received a gift instead. So if the chance comes later, give him another.”

He meant that forming a bond with Geom Mugeuk through a single sacred relic was actually a gain.

Though he knew he should not feel this way, So Baekta felt jealous of Geom Mugeuk. At the same time, he resolved that he too would become someone who received such trust from his master.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader was reading his disciple’s thoughts.

“Do not rush. It was only after I was more than twice your age that I finally began to think deeply about my own life. Before that, it felt as if I had simply been swept along in a daze.”

To think that at this age, he would feel as though his true life was only just beginning.

“So do not be impatient. Your true life may not have begun yet either.”

So Baekta did not want to part with his master.

“Will you be returning with the Young Cult Leader?”

He had been afraid his master would say he had to go. But the Pungcheon Cult Leader smiled and said,

“For the time being, I will stay here with our Cult Leader before I leave. We will train together, drink together, and talk about the old days.”

At least for now, his disciple took precedence over Gowol and Geom Mugeuk.

Compared to those two, his disciple still had much to learn, so he wanted to remain with him a little longer and teach him.

So Baekta smiled brightly.

“Thank you, Master.”

Being jealous, then delighted. Seeing how he was swayed between joy and sorrow even in this short span of time, he thought, Yes, Master is right. I still have a long way to go.

* * *

All the way from the Cult Leader’s Hall back to my quarters, my heart pounded as if it would burst.

At the thought that I might finally unravel the secret of the secret coffer that even the Heavenly Demons of old had failed to solve for so many long years, my steps quickened of their own accord.

After ordering the guards not to let anyone disturb me, I entered my room.

I took the secret coffer and the Heukjeong from my robes and placed them side by side on the table.

“Secret coffer, you’ve got a friend now.”

Because I had drawn a smiling face on the secret coffer, it truly looked as though it were happy to have made a friend.

What connection could there be between these two?

I picked up the Heukjeong and placed it on top of the secret coffer. But nothing happened. It had even vibrated and told me to choose the Heukjeong, and yet.

“If you’re going to act this distant, why did you ask for this friend?”

I waited a moment, but the secret coffer showed no reaction.

I turned the secret coffer and placed the Heukjeong on another side. Again, there was no reaction. I placed it on another side, then another.

“Bear with it, even if doing a handstand is uncomfortable.”

And at the very moment I flipped the smiling secret coffer upside down and placed the Heukjeong on its bottom—

Sssssk.

The Heukjeong began to be sucked into the secret coffer.

I had never imagined the secret coffer would absorb the Heukjeong like this, so I could only stare at it in surprise. Like a drop of water being absorbed, the Heukjeong slipped cleanly into the secret coffer.

I drew up my internal energy and prepared for any possibility, holding my breath as I stared at the secret coffer.

Even after waiting for quite some time, the secret coffer remained as it was.

“You! You ate your friend!”

The moment those words ended—

Wooong!

The secret coffer vibrated once.

“Do you understand what I’m saying?”

The secret coffer shook and vibrated again. It was a powerful vibration, enough to shake the table. The secret coffer, vibrating fiercely, rose into the air.

It began spinning rapidly in midair.

Whiriririririk!

It looked as though it might burst at any moment, or fly off somewhere.

What should I do?

There was no time to think at length. I followed my instincts. After drawing my protective aura to its limit, I seized the secret coffer floating in the air with my hand.

At that very moment—

Shoooooooo!

A sound I had never heard before rang out, and something pitch-black began emerging from the secret coffer.

That pitch-black thing was not smoke, nor was it liquid. It was not internal energy or aura, and it was not light either. It was something I had never seen or felt in my life, something impossible to describe in words.

The thing that came from the secret coffer passed through my hand and arm and spread through my body. It did not extend anywhere else. Like ink spreading across paper, it spread only through my body.

Paaaaaaa.

I simply watched as it spread. Instinctively, I could tell. At the very least, this was not an energy meant to harm me. If it had been, the Heavenly Demon Protective Art would have activated. If it had been, I would have instinctively thrown the secret coffer to the floor.

But I gripped the secret coffer tightly and watched as it transferred into me. It flickered like light, seemed like waves, and gave off some sensation like energy. Though it was pitch-black, the feeling was as refreshing as drinking clear water.

Hwaaaaaak!

The moment it spread from the crown of my head to the tips of my toes—

Flash!

Radiance burst from my entire body. It was so blinding that I squeezed my eyes shut.

When I opened them again, the radiance had vanished. The thing that had spread through my body had already disappeared into me.

I hurriedly examined my body first.

The energy that came from the secret coffer had certainly seeped deep into every part of me, but there was no special change. My internal energy had not increased, nor was there any clump of energy gathered somewhere in my dantian that I could not dissolve.

But I felt it. That it remained in my body. That it existed somewhere within me.

‘What on earth is it?’

It was right then.

Clack!

The secret coffer spat out the Heukjeong it had swallowed.

The Heukjeong that popped out rolled across the floor.

I picked up the Heukjeong and examined it, but it no longer had the glossy ink-black sheen it had at first. Now it was nothing more than an ordinary lump of metal.

I could tell instinctively. The energy hidden within the Heukjeong had been absorbed into me through the secret coffer. It was the moment the secret of the coffer was revealed.

The secret coffer was, in fact, a device that absorbed energy contained within something else.

Without something it could absorb, like the Heukjeong, the secret coffer was truly worthless. How could anyone have uncovered this secret?

The reason I had uncovered it was, if anything, because I had not tried too hard to solve it. Because I had simply carried it around in my robes like a friend, I had been able to solve the mystery.

And then another premonition came to me.

This did not seem to be the end. I had a feeling that if there were other objects besides the Heukjeong that contained energy it could absorb, I would be able to absorb them with this secret coffer.

‘What exactly are you? Who made you?’

As if nothing had happened, the secret coffer merely stared at me while flipped upside down, smiling brightly.

Now that I knew which side was up, I would have to draw its face anew.

* * *

The next day, we said our farewells.

When the guards, who had risen early in the morning, were busy preparing the carriage, the Pungcheon Cult Leader and So Baekta came out to see us off.

“Return safely.”

At So Baekta’s farewell, I told him what I had truly wanted to say.

“You experienced it yourself, so you must know, but there are those plotting schemes in the shadows. Please be careful from now on.”

“I will. Young Cult Leader, take care of yourself as well.”

Next, I exchanged farewells with the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

Though it was not a long parting, the fact that I felt this reluctant meant I must have grown quite attached to him.

“Do you know what I felt while dealing with the Sinung Ironworks this time?”

“What did you feel?”

“They did not know where they belonged or what goal they were moving toward. And yet they were working in perfect harmony. Do you know who they made me think of? Eunwol. An organization made up of thoroughly compartmentalized cells, yet operating under a perfect system.”

What he wanted to tell me was precisely this.

“There must be someone truly intelligent behind them, someone like you or Gowol.”

Someone at the level of the Pungcheon Cult Leader could tell just by touching the tail how large and ferocious the monster was.

“I will keep that in mind when dealing with them. Thank you for telling me.”

“I will send word separately to Gowol. I will not stay here all that long.”

“Please take your time and rest comfortably.”

“Farewell, our Young Cult Leader who cleaned out the Pungcheon Cult.”

He grinned broadly, delighted at the thought of attacking me with this for a while.

I did not let it pass either.

“Now that I have seen the sacred relics, there were still many things left that I coveted.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader shouted to So Baekta.

“Protect our sacred relics well from the Young Cult Leader!”

If my farewell with him ended in jokes and banter like that, my farewell with Mabul was exceedingly blunt.

“I will go on ahead.”

“See you at the cult.”

But the looks in their eyes toward each other were different from when they had first come.

And so I left the Pungcheon Cult with Mabul. When we came, there had been three of us, but when we returned, there were two. Yet in my heart, it felt as though four of us were returning.

Until our carriage disappeared from sight, the Pungcheon Cult Leader and So Baekta stood watching in front of the main gate.

* * *

The road back was leisurely.

When we saw a tall mountain, we stopped the carriage, climbed it, and gathered poisonous herbs. We camped beneath the stars in the wasteland.

For meals, the guards cooked the animals they hunted. I taught the guards everything from how to process animal hides to which cuts of meat were tasty and how to cook them so they would not be tough. Thanks to that, the guards’ cooking skills improved by the day.

“There is truly nothing you cannot do, Young Cult Leader.”

At the guards’ admiration, I shook my head.

“There is exactly one thing I cannot do.”

“What is it?”

Without telling them, I sighed and turned away.

And so today as well, after eating well, I lay down to sleep with the campfire between us.

Lying there and looking up at the night sky, I said to Mabul,

“Come to think of it, Lord Mabul, you are a star too.”

Beyond the campfire, Mabul turned to look at me.

“What do you mean?”

“Like the stars, you always shine on your own.”

Mabul’s face was visible beyond the wavering flames. Perhaps he had grown used to it now, for he stared at me and said,

“I do not have sacred relics like the Pungcheon Cult Leader.”

“But you have poisonous herbs instead, don’t you?”

Mabul had gathered seven of them, but I still had not managed to gather even one. The one thing I had not told the guards I could not do was gathering medicinal herbs.

“Please, just give me one!”

Mabul gave me a look that said not a chance, then turned over to face the other way.

I knew what was in his heart. He wanted to hear from the Poison King that our Lord Mabul was indeed the best. He wanted to become a special person capable of gathering poisonous herbs that no one else could.

The more that was the case, the more I wanted to tease him.

I rose silently and was just about to creep toward the box of herbs Mabul had gathered when—

Still lying down, Mabul stretched his hand toward it.

The golden radiance extending from his palm became a golden dragon and coiled around the box as if protecting it. It was Mabul’s signature demonic art, the Golden Daera Demonic Art.

“This is too much! I’m the Young Cult Leader! The person who will become the Heavenly Demon in the future!”

As if to say it would not work, the golden dragon writhed and roared fiercely.

“Yes, I should sleep. I’m sleeping.”

I lay down again. A short distance away, Jeok Yeon, the head of the guards, had been watching us and could not hold back his laughter, but when his eyes met mine, he turned his head the other way.

Mabul, who had been lying turned away, called to me quietly.

“Young Cult Leader.”

“Yes, Lord Mabul.”

After a brief pause, Mabul said,

“Thank you.”

I was looking at the stars in the night sky. Tonight, the starlight was especially bright.

“Me too.”

Neither of us asked the other what we were grateful for.

And so, cutting through the sandy winds beyond the borders, we returned to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Chapter 331: Have You Gathered the Poisonous Herbs?
---------------------------------------------------
The palm force that came hurtling in collided with the great saber held upright.

Despite the thunderous boom and the shock, the great saber did not break.

“Do not be afraid! The moment you fear, you die!”

The one who shouted was the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, and the one who had raised the great saber like a shield to block the palm force was Seo Daeryong. The great saber he used was the one the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had given him as a gift. The very saber he had used in his youth.

“Now, here it comes again!”

An even more powerful palm force burst from the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s hand.

Seo Daeryong infused internal energy into the great saber and raised it before him once more.

Kwa-kwaang!

It was stronger than before, but this time too, he held his ground.

Behind the raised blade, Seo Daeryong was feeling fear. Even this attack felt as if it might tear his arms off. If an even stronger strike came flying at him, he might die. What if his master was overestimating him too much? Should he say now that he could not block any more?

Then Seo Daeryong’s eyes caught sight of the characters engraved on the blade.

Peerless (無雙).

They were the characters the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had carved in his youth. He could feel what kind of heart his master must have trained with back then. The will to lose to no one was contained in those two characters.

‘That’s right. I’ll endure it! Even if my arms break, I’ll endure it!’

The moment he drew up his internal energy and clenched his teeth, an even stronger palm force came flying in.

Shaaaaaak!

The merciless palm force came with greater power and collided with Seo Daeryong’s will.

A short while later.

Tap, tap.

At the sound of someone tapping the blade, Seo Daeryong came to his senses and raised his head. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon was standing beside the blade, looking at him. Had he lost consciousness while still standing?

“Are you alive?”

“It seems so.”

Seo Daeryong’s face was pale, and his arms were trembling.

“Did I block it?”

“You did.”

He was so happy that he tried to throw both hands into the air, only to scream. Not only his arms, but his entire body hurt so badly he could not move. It hurt so much he thought he might die.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon pressed down on the hilt of the saber that had been blocking the front. The great saber sank straight into the ground.

Then he seated Seo Daeryong against the great saber and infused internal energy into his chest to treat his injuries. If he had blocked one more strike, he truly would have died.

“You were afraid the blade would break, weren’t you?”

“Yes.”

Seo Daeryong answered honestly.

“It is natural to be afraid. But you must believe. You must have faith that you can block any attack with your blade. The Heaven-Destroying Demon Blade Form is a martial art that begins with that faith and ends with that faith.”

“I will keep that in mind.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon took a pill from his robes and held it out to Seo Daeryong.

“Take it.”

“What is this?”

“It is a Demon Essence Pill.”

At the words Demon Essence Pill, Seo Daeryong was startled. The Demon Essence Pill was one of the spirit medicines that represented the Demon Cult. The spirit medicine Geom Mugeuk had obtained in Socheondong was precisely this.

“I asked the Cult Leader and specially obtained one.”

“Something this precious! I cannot accept it.”

At that, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon glared at Seo Daeryong and said coldly,

“Then is my chief disciple not precious?”

In that instant, Seo Daeryong flinched in surprise.

“That is not true!”

“Then take it.”

Though startled and flustered, Seo Daeryong could refuse no longer, so he took the Demon Essence Pill. He circulated his heart method and dissolved the medicinal effects of the pill.

The energy of the Demon Essence Pill coursed through his blood vessels, became vast internal power, and was stored in his dantian.

Seo Daeryong slowly opened his eyes, and their gaze had deepened. A majestic internal power incomparable to before stirred in his dantian. Seo Daeryong was so moved he could hardly tell whether this was a dream or reality. He had thought he would never be able to take a spirit medicine like the Demon Essence Pill until the day he died.

Seo Daeryong rose on the spot and gave the Blood Heaven Blade Demon a deep bow.

“I will never forget the grace you have bestowed upon me, even until the day I die.”

He had given him the blade he had used in his youth, and now he had even given him a Demon Essence Pill. He truly regarded him completely as his successor. This was the result of drunkenly clinging to his trouser leg at the Pungryu Tavern. He would never let go of that hand until the day he died.

“You need at least this level of internal power to handle the latter half of the Heaven-Destroying Demon Blade Form. And your training from now on will be far more difficult and arduous.”

“I will absolutely accomplish it.”

Knowing well that his disciple was working hard even while cutting down on his duties as Pavilion Lord of Hwangcheon Pavilion and on his sleep, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon nodded with a look of pride. Once he overcame this hurdle, Seo Daeryong would truly join the ranks of the masters.

The two left the training ground.

As the two men, both carrying great sabers on their backs, walked side by side, they exuded an utterly overwhelming atmosphere.

“Has there still been no news from the Young Cult Leader?”

“I received word that he is on his way back, but I do not know exactly when he will arrive.”

Seo Daeryong missed Geom Mugeuk terribly. He missed him so much that a few days earlier, he had even appeared in his dreams. He had to meet him quickly and boast that his master had bestowed a Demon Essence Pill on him.

Just then, Dogwi of the Southern Blade Sect came and reported to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“They say the Young Cult Leader has arrived.”

Seo Daeryong’s face lit up brightly.

“Go.”

“Yes.”

Wanting at least to see his face before he entered the Cult Leader’s Hall, Seo Daeryong ran off like the wind.

In his haste, he failed to notice that a joy no less than his own had risen on the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s face.

Right now, all he wanted was to see Geom Mugeuk.

* * *

“Why have you suddenly gone wild at your age?”

At the Drunken Demon’s question, the One Blossom Sword Venerable emptied her cup instead of answering.

“It’s nice to drink after so long.”

The two of them were drinking at the Drunken Dream Pavilion in the Great Drunken Forest.

“I asked what’s with the martial arts training. Is the Martial Alliance invading? Or is there some sword-wielding scoundrel you need to impress?”

Even if there had not been rumors that the One Blossom Sword Venerable had fallen into a trance of martial arts training, one could tell from her appearance, which would have been unimaginable in the past.

She had no makeup on in front of the Drunken Demon, and though she had always gone about wearing only pure white martial robes, she was now wearing gray robes stained with dirt. She had been training in martial arts right up until just before coming here.

“I am called a Sword Venerable, after all. I should see the end of the sword.”

“So why? Why all of a sudden?”

The One Blossom Sword Venerable poured wine into her own cup.

“What is so extraordinary about a martial artist training in martial arts that you are making such a fuss?”

The words “Because I was ashamed” rose to her throat, but she could not bring herself to say them. That Drunken Demon would pry into every little detail, and she could not answer him with that.

She was ashamed of the matter involving Sa Ujong, and ashamed of the way she had cared about her outward appearance. Looking back, the same was true of the days when she had been at odds with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Still, it’s good to see.”

At the Drunken Demon’s single remark, the One Blossom Sword Venerable felt better.

“I hear you’ve also been drinking less and training these days?”

The Drunken Demon, too, was not simply a drunkard who did nothing but drink. The gaze of the Drunken Demon, who had begun controlling his drinking, was different from before. He was not the sort of person who could watch everyone else changing while he alone sat around drinking.

“This is all because of the Young Cult Leader.”

The One Blossom Sword Venerable smiled faintly. He was right. The fact that she herself, as well as the Demon Venerables, had changed was because of the Young Cult Leader.

“It’s about time he arrived, isn’t it?”

“He’ll come when it’s time.”

The One Blossom Sword Venerable could guess why the Drunken Demon was feeling spiteful.

When Geom Mugeuk left the cult this time, he had taken the Demon Buddha with him. The Drunken Demon must have secretly hoped that he would ask him to come along instead. Moreover, wasn’t the Demon Buddha someone who had supported the Eldest Young Master? She could see his disappointment dripping into his wine cup. He often talked nonsense when drunk, but he had not been someone who revealed his feelings like this.

‘Young Cult Leader, it seems this really is because of you.’

The One Blossom Sword Venerable emptied her final cup and rose from her seat.

“Why? You’re leaving already?”

“I have to train.”

“No! You came after so long, so stay and have more fun.”

“Next time. Today’s training is important.”

At those words, the Drunken Demon emptied another cup.

“The Young Cult Leader abandoned me, and now my friend abandons me too. Fine, my only friend is wine.”

The One Blossom Sword Venerable looked at Yeobin of the Three Great Drunkards, who was walking toward them, and said,

“I wonder if that is really true.”

The Drunken Demon understood what she meant, but he did not take his eyes off his wine cup.

Yeobin came to the two of them and reported respectfully,

“They say the Young Cult Leader has returned.”

The Drunken Demon sprang up from his seat.

“Where are you going?”

“My little brother went all the way beyond the frontier and came back. I should go see him.”

To the One Blossom Sword Venerable, those words sounded like this: Let’s see just how well you did after leaving me behind!

“Don’t go and make a drunken scene!”

“Am I a child, that I would cause a drunken scene there?”

The Drunken Demon lightly launched himself into the air and crossed the lake with the Water-Treading Step.

True, everyone else had taken turns leaving the cult with Geom Mugeuk. Though they called each other brothers, he had never once gone with him, so it was understandable that he felt disappointed.

The One Blossom Sword Venerable boarded a ferry with Yeobin.

“He must be a handful, isn’t he?”

Knowing she was speaking of the Drunken Demon, Yeobin, who was rowing, smiled faintly.

The One Blossom Sword Venerable had never usually said anything to her, but today there was something she wanted to say. As a fellow woman, and out of affection for the Drunken Demon.

“I regret the person I was in the past. I hope you will not do the same.”

Many details were omitted from those words, but Yeobin understood what she meant.

The boat carrying the One Blossom Sword Venerable quietly crossed the misty lake.

* * *

Poison smoke rose from all over the Thousand Poison Forest.

Today was a day of special training for the poison practitioners of the Thousand Poison Forest.

Most of those participating in the training wore between one and three poison pouches at their waists.

Due to the nature of poison arts, training that involved releasing poison externally like this could not be done often. There was no being as dangerous as a poison practitioner with half-baked skill. Thus, when they trained, they did so under the strict control of their seniors.

When they released poison, seniors with at least five poison pouches immediately neutralized that poison. If the poison smoke were carried outside by the wind, something terrible could happen. Detoxifying was always harder than poisoning.

A little distance away, there was training in which practitioners entered a space where poison had been released, combined the various medicinal herbs prepared there, neutralized the poison, and came back out. It was perilous training in which a delay could cost them their lives. This area was personally instructed and managed by seniors with seven or more poison pouches.

Elsewhere, training in throwing hidden weapons coated with poison was in full swing. Before one could freely use poison, hidden weapon arts were essential to master. The senior instructing them was explaining the reason poison practitioners died most often. The number one cause of death among poison practitioners was being killed by their own poison!

After one round of training ended like that, all the poison practitioners gathered in one place.

The Poison King stepped forward before them.

“When martial artists face us in actual combat, their reactions are of two kinds. Either they run away without looking back, or they charge at us fiercely. They think only of killing us before we can release our poison. Because they attack while holding their breath, most are defeated before they can even use their poison properly. At best, it ends in mutual destruction.”

The Poison King standing before the poison practitioners was completely different from his usual self. At least in this moment, he had come out of his own world and was instructing his subordinates.

The rookies, if anything, did not truly know what the twelve poison pouches at his waist meant. As the number of pouches they carried increased, their respect for him would grow all the more.

“The most important martial art for a poison practitioner is neither poison-releasing techniques nor hidden weapon arts. It is footwork and lightness skill. If they chase you, you must release poison while fleeing, and if they flee, you must be able to pursue them faster than they can run. You must avoid opponents who swing their swords while holding their breath. Your lives depend on that battle of time. Do you understand?”

The poison practitioners’ thunderous answer rang out.

“Now then, begin training.”

At that, the senior poison practitioners stepped forward and began footwork and lightness skill training.

The Poison King stood silently and watched them train.

Just then, Sangseon came and reported.

“The Young Cult Leader has retur……”

Before the words were even finished, the Poison King was no longer there. It was the moment everyone saw for themselves what the Poison King’s lightness skill was like.

* * *

Standing in place, I took a deep breath.

The main cult has a unique scent. When I return after leaving the cult and smell this scent, I feel the relief of finally being back.

The first person to welcome us was Seo Daeryong.

“Young Cult Leader!”

If it had been just the two of us, he would have charged in and hugged me without restraint.

Seo Daeryong came over with a composed face and greeted me.

“Did you have a safe trip?”

“Have you been well too, Pavilion Lord?”

His true feelings came flying to me through sound transmission.

—I missed you! Next time, please take me with you too!

—Then who will guard Hwangcheon Pavilion?

—It runs perfectly well even without me! I’m going to follow you no matter what! I definitely told you!

—Fine, let’s go. Cross a room filled with the Blood-Poison Great Python and thousands of venomous snakes, pass through a place where a colossal evil spirit tries to stomp you until you burst. You should enjoy the thrill of your own shadow springing up and trying to kill you, too.

—…….

—…….

—Regrettably, I have to protect Hwangcheongak. What kind of organization is this, that it won’t function without me?

How could I not know? His desire to go out with me. How much he must miss that time we went to Soryongjeon together. So hurry up and grow stronger, my right-hand man!

If Seo Daeryong had been the first to welcome me back, then the first to welcome the Demon Buddha was the Poison King.

He came running like the wind and landed before us. He asked the Demon Buddha urgently.

“My poisonous herbs! Did you dig up the herbs?”

I stepped in front of the Poison King and said,

“I’m back. I made it safely back from the life-or-death lands beyond the border!”

The Poison King shoved me aside and looked only at the Demon Buddha.

The Demon Buddha’s eyes, as he faced him, were also full of life. Hadn’t the Demon Buddha gone through all that hardship for this very moment?

“Here they are.”

The Demon Buddha set down the box he had been carrying himself. The Poison King crouched before it, and Seo Daeryong and I crouched with him to watch.

When he slowly opened the box, the poisonous herbs were lined up inside.

“Oh! You dug up this many? Handokcho (寒毒草), and even Yeongbulcho (英佛草)! This Sodokcho (小毒草) is truly hard to find. Thank you. Truly, thank you.”

The Poison King was overjoyed, practically ready to leap into the air. It wasn’t some show of happiness just because the herbs had been gathered for him. He was genuinely delighted that the poisonous herbs had been obtained.

With that being the case, how could the Demon Buddha ever have been careless in digging up poisonous herbs?

“You truly have a gift from the heavens.”

“I was fortunate.”

Only then did the Poison King glance at me.

“And what did you dig up?”

With those eyes that clearly expected nothing at all, I would have liked to answer, I dug up even more, but—

I pointed to one of the poisonous herbs the Demon Buddha had dug up and said,

“When that one was being dug up, I’m the one who said we should dig on that mountain. If it weren’t for me……”

Without even listening to the rest of my sorry excuse, the Poison King gathered up the box and said to the Demon Buddha,

“Come eat with me later!”

“Let’s do that.”

The Poison King and the Demon Buddha’s gazes tangled in midair. From between them, I shouted,

“I’m going too!”

But the Poison King left without even answering me.

On the Demon Buddha’s face as he watched the Poison King’s retreating back, there was no trace of all the toil he had endured digging up those poisonous herbs.

“I knew this would happen. You should have given me just one root. How heartless you are, Lord Demon Buddha!”

Just then, someone spoke from behind us.

“Then next time, go out with someone affectionate.”

I turned around to find the Drunken Demon walking toward us.

At that, the Demon Buddha looked at me meaningfully and said,

“Now it’s your turn to make yourself look good.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 332

Chapter 332: I Try to Comfort Them in My Own Way.
-------------------------------------------------
There was a gift for Chwima.

On the way back, he had stopped by a famous brewery in Saewe to buy alcohol.

Jeokyeon unloaded boxes of liquor from the carriage. It was not just one or two bottles. He had bought several bottles each of various kinds, enough to last a long while.

“Don’t tell me you bought this for me?”

“I bought them to your taste, Hyung, but I’m not sure if you’ll like them. I deliberately left out the overly strong ones. For your health.”

Chwima was flustered by the unexpected gift. He had originally come to give Geom Mugeuk a piece of his mind, asking if he’d had a nice trip with Mabul while leaving him out. But a gift?

Mabul spoke up on Geom Mugeuk’s behalf.

“The Young Cult Leader stopped by many places in Saewe for you. He did not spare himself the trouble of traveling all the way to distant places.”

This was how Mabul highlighted the effort, making up for not having handed over the poisonous herb to Geom Mugeuk.

“Then I’ll leave you two to talk. I’ll go on ahead to the Cult Leader’s palace.”

Mabul was the first to step away.

After he left, Chwima looked over the alcohol. The emotion came not merely from receiving the gift. Every kind he had mentioned as being decent while drinking with Geom Mugeuk in Saewe was there. He had remembered them all.

Chwima picked up a bottle, uncorked it, and drank straight from it.

The taste of the liquor was incredibly sweet.

“Give me a sip too.”

Geom Mugeuk also drank from the bottle Chwima handed him.

“It definitely tastes different from the alcohol of the Central Plains. I’ll head there soon, so let’s have a drink together then.”

Then someone else appeared.

“Did you have a good trip?”

It was none other than Ilhwa Geomjun.

Chwima asked her,

“Aren’t you busy with training?”

She had come because she wanted to see Geom Mugeuk. She wanted to show him that she still trained without wearing makeup. And one more thing.

“I came to tell you to take our drunkard with you next time you go out. But it seems there’s no need for that.”

She looked at the boxes of alcohol and asked,

“You bought quite a lot. Is there no gift for me?”

“I’m sorry, but this time the gifts are only for our Chwima-nim.”

At that, Chwima asked Geom Mugeuk,

“You didn’t get one for the Cult Leader either?”

When Geom Mugeuk nodded, Chwima was inwardly flustered but pleased.

It was a moment that normally would have called for a round of banter, but he couldn’t bring himself to do so in front of Ilhwa Geomjun. How could she not know? That the reason she had come was to protest him taking only Mabul.

Ilhwa Geomjun asked Geom Mugeuk,

“How did the matter go?”

“It was handled well.”

“Yes, because you went.”

“There were dangerous moments as well.”

Then Ilhwa Geomjun repeated what she had said before.

“Yes, because you went.”

It meant that storms followed wherever Geom Mugeuk went.

“Since you’ve returned, stop by sometime.”

“Yes, I will pay you a visit soon.”

Ilhwa Geomjun looked at Chwima and asked,

“Will you help me carry these in?”

Chwima nodded. If he used his internal energy, what were ten boxes, or even a hundred, that he couldn’t carry? She wasn’t asking because she didn’t know that.

Chwima and Ilhwa Geomjun walked side by side from that spot, carrying the boxes of bottles.

After walking for some time, Ilhwa Geomjun abruptly spoke to Chwima.

“Lord Majon, it is time you came to your senses.”

Chwima had nothing to say in reply.

After Chwima and Ilhwa Geomjun left, another Majon arrived. This time, it was Seophon Majon.

“Did you return safely?”

Since this trip to Saewe had begun because of her, how could she not come out to see him?

“Thanks to your concern, I returned safely.”

After exchanging greetings, she looked for the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“Where is my master?”

“The Cult Leader remained in the Pungcheon Cult.”

He quickly spoke to her startled expression.

“He said he would come as soon as matters there are finished, so it shouldn’t take too long.”

Disappointment flashed across Seophon Majon’s face. It was clear she had waited for the Pungcheon Cult Leader more than anyone. The Pungcheon Cult Leader was currently explosively popular among his disciples.

After she left, Seo Daeryong spoke.

“Just how many Majon have come? Let me see. One, two… a total of four have come.”

Geom Mugeuk replied,

“No, it’s five.”

“Eh? Did I miscount?”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze was directed at a massive stone statue standing far away.

“There is one over there as well.”

“Who is it?”

Seo Daeryong couldn’t make out who it was; the figure looked that small.

But with his Divine Eye Technique, Geom Mugeuk saw clearly. It was the person he was happiest to see.

The one watching them with arms crossed, wearing a white mask, was none other than Geukak Soma. He stood atop the same statue where they had once stood together before.

‘Soma-nim!’

He had been watching from that distance for Geom Mugeuk’s return.

Last time, he had said that when he returned to the cult, perhaps he would be the first to come and greet him. He had not let those words and actions end there; this time too, he had come out first to welcome him.

‘Thank you, Soma-nim. I will visit you later.’

He didn’t know if Geukak Soma could see, but Geom Mugeuk bowed his head in that direction before turning toward the Cult Leader’s palace.

Because right now, he missed his father too much.

* * *

His father was at the Cheonma Palace with Chief Strategist Sima Myeong.

Mabul had already greeted them and returned.

“I have returned, Father.”

He received the greeting with a stony face, but I was so happy and glad to see Father. I hoped this emotion showed on my face. I hoped Father would recognize it.

“Strategist, I have returned.”

“You’ve worked hard, Young Cult Leader.”

He had already received full reports on what had happened in Saewe through the Silver Moon.

“Until now, our side failed to detect the Blood Cult’s revival movements. It is entirely my oversight.”

“That is not the case. Before he died, he told me that while his purpose had been the Blood Cult’s revival, he changed his mind. As if to prove it, in his final moments he tried to destroy the sacred site the Blood Cult had left behind. He was a man who acted on personal desire.”

Of course, Sima Myeong must have suspected as much. Baek Cheongyeong, who had infiltrated the Martial Arts Alliance, had no connection whatsoever to the Blood Cult.

“There may be remnants remaining, so please be careful.”

“They are the ones who should be careful. Whose son did they provoke?”

Sima Myeong smiled at my joke.

Then Father, who had merely been listening to our conversation, abruptly asked me,

“What sort of people are they?”

He cut straight to the chase, skipping all preamble. He was asking for my gut feeling.

“They are very dangerous people. More dangerous than any organization that has appeared in the martial world until now. Because no one has ever conceived of provoking both Majeongsa and Saewe at the same time.”

Father slowly nodded, as if agreeing with those words.

Father looked at Sima Myeong and said,

“You will become busy.”

It was a word imbued with Father’s intent to handle things carefully and prudently, and also a word filled with trust in the Chief Strategist.

“I will report as soon as new information comes in. Then I shall leave you two to speak.”

Sima Myeong courteously bowed and excused himself.

Father stepped down from the high seat.

“Let us walk a bit.”

“Yes.”

These days Father likes walking with me. Walking together in the inner courtyard was good for me in many ways. Whenever the passing demonic practitioners saw us like this, the authority and loyalty toward me would only grow.

“Ah, and Father. I unlocked the secret of the Secret Coffer.”

Father stopped in surprise.

I took the coffer from my bosom. And I told him that I had absorbed the black essence energy at the Pungcheon Cult.

“If you wish, I will return it to you.”

“It is an object whose fate is tied to you; it is yours now.”

Father’s bearing always moves me. At times like this, the words, “Father is truly Father,” come to me unbidden.

Father reached out and took my wrist instead of the coffer. A stream of energy flowed in through my wrist. He was examining whether there was anything wrong with my body.

“I can feel some kind of energy inside, but I cannot tell exactly what it is. However, it does not feel harmful.”

I looked at the coffer I held. As Father said, fate had found me. I could feel that this fate was by no means a bad one.

“If there is anything wrong with my body, I will inform you immediately.”

I couldn’t feel the black essence energy, but I could feel Father’s worry. It was an energy more precious and empowering than any energy in the world.

“Ah, and the Pungcheon Cult Leader remained at his cult. Through this matter, my relationship with the current Cult Leader has deepened. Like yours and mine, Father.”

“Do you think our relationship has deepened?”

“‘Deepened’ is insufficient. It is sticky, thick, and passionate. Ah! This feeling cannot be expressed in words.”

A smirk tugged at the corners of Father’s mouth. Seeing that expression, I felt a pleasant sense of relief that I had truly returned.

“Is there not someone else with whom you should deepen your relationship?”

I said to Father with a smile,

“I was planning to greet you and go see Hyung first anyway.”

* * *

Tension hung in the air at the Iron Workshop of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

It was the moment for the final test of a newly developed weapon. The weapon to be tested today was a gauntlet for those who practiced fist arts.

“The gauntlets developed this time are made of a material so thin and soft that you can’t even feel them on your hands. However, their strength has been greatly improved over previous versions, enough to prevent being cut by swords.”

The one explaining was Artisan Gwak of the Iron Workshop. Standing before him was the Great Prince, Geom Muyang.

The one who had planned and driven the development of these gauntlets was Geom Muyang himself. He was in charge of several major affairs of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

At first, Geom Muyang had naturally expected to be pushed into a meaningless position.

But Geom Mugeuk had shared important tasks with him. Moreover, these were not limited to administrative work. He entrusted him with matters related to the major elite organizations within the Alliance and duties connected to the Eight Demon Venerables. It was truly a demonstration of trust in his older brother.

The strength test was a success. Even after being struck with a sword, the gauntlets were not cut. The onlookers cheered in unison.

“Not only that, the power of the fist has increased by more than ten percent.”

“You have truly worked hard.”

“It is the Great Prince who worked hard.”

In truth, Artisan Gwak had shaken his head many times during this task. If it came to doing things meticulously, he was not one to fall behind anywhere, but compared to the Great Prince, he felt like a slacker who did things roughly.

Geom Muyang visited several times a day to check on the progress, and if there was even the slightest irregularity, he wished to have it remade.

However, Geom Muyang did not merely order them around; he personally investigated, researched, and led by example, so the Iron Workshop could not express any dissatisfaction. Thanks to that, they succeeded in producing gauntlets more excellent than ever before.

“For now, these are gauntlets for fifty people. The Great Prince should deliver them to the Eastern Fist Gate himself.”

“I believe the Fist Demon will be very pleased to see these gauntlets.”

“One day, we shall have to make gauntlets that the Fist Demon himself can wear.”

Because although the quality was outstanding, they were still mass-produced gauntlets.

“Please have a different workshop make those.”

A faint smile crossed Geom Muyang’s face at Workshop Head Gwak’s joke.

Then someone spoke from behind.

“As expected! The arm bends inward.”

The two turned to see Geom Mugeuk entering.

“You develop things for the one who supported you first.”

During the succession struggle, the Fist Demon had officially declared his support for Geom Muyang.

Unlike before, Geom Muyang received those words gently.

“Isn’t it natural? From now on, I’ll only develop things for the Fist Demon and Lord Mabul. If you don’t like it, kick me out.”

“That would be difficult because Hyung is too good at his job.”

Geom Mugeuk courteously greeted Workshop Head Gwak.

“It has been a while, Workshop Head.”

“I heard you had returned.”

“May I try on a gauntlet?”

“Of course.”

Geom Mugeuk fitted the gauntlet onto his hand. Workshop Head Gwak watched with some nervousness. It was common knowledge that Geom Mugeuk had inherited the Fist Demon’s fist arts. This was the moment a master of fist arts conducted the first test.

Geom Mugeuk, having fitted the gauntlets and thrown punches this way and that, smiled brightly and said,

“These are excellent. May I take these?”

Only then did Workshop Head Gwak relax.

“Of course.”

“Please leave your name here as a memento. Wouldn’t its value increase tenfold then?”

Workshop Head Gwak smiled and excused himself, telling them to talk.

When Geom Muyang tried to lift all the boxes of gauntlets at once, Geom Mugeuk took half.

The two walked side by side.

“Lord Mabul was a great help on this trip.”

“He is a good man. You must not act rudely toward him.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Geom Muyang and smiled.

“Why are you smiling?”

“I mean that when I look at you, Hyung, you really take care of the people around you. Developing the gauntlets first like this.”

“It’s like fixing the barn after losing the ox.”

“But that ox didn’t wander far from the barn.”

Geom Muyang also burst into a laugh.

“Thank you, Hyung.”

Then Geom Muyang spoke somewhat coldly.

“Do you know how many times a day I recall the past and regret it? Do you know that every night I fall asleep imagining myself as the successor? I am not someone you should thank, so do not thank me.”

“That’s why I’m thankful.”

“What?”

“I’m thankful that you say these things to me. If it bubbles up inside you so much that you can’t stand it in the future, come find me and say this to me. If I’m not there, go to Lord Mabul and say it. If that doesn’t work, go to the Fist Demon. If you really can’t hold it in, go to Father.”

“Why is Father last? You should leave Father out.”

“Wouldn’t talking to Father be the most relieving?”

Geom Muyang stopped walking and looked at me.

“You need to get close to Father. Leaving aside all this Young Cult Leader business. We are Father’s sons, aren’t we?”

I try to comfort Hyung in my own way.

“Father and Lord Mabul worry about you. But I don’t worry about you. I have no intention of comforting you either. You’ll do well on your own. I know better than anyone how tough you are, don’t I?”

Geom Muyang smiled faintly, self-deprecatingly.

Of course, I know. Once a person begins to collapse, how quickly they crumble.

When the mind begins to tempt and whisper to us, we come to know. Our will is not a lock made of eternal iron. Sometimes it is a knot tied with cloth that comes undone with a slight tug.

“Have you ever asked Father?”

“Asked what?”

“What is hard for him.”

For a moment, Geom Muyang flinched.

“Did you ask him?”

“I didn’t ask either. I thought that was something the eldest son should do first.”

Hyung stared at me.

That was why this was the method I chose to comfort Hyung. If he falls into self-pity, he will sink into an endless abyss.

“I think it’s time we took care of them. You go first, because you’re the eldest.”

Absolute Regression — Chapter 333

Chapter 333: When Revealing Old Desires.
----------------------------------------
Chwima and Ilhwa Geomjon arrived at Daechwirim.

“Leave the liquor chest here. I’ll carry it from here.”

At Chwima’s words, Ilhwa Geomjon kept walking.

“Shall we have a drink before we go?”

“And here you were about to leave so coldly when I held you back?”

“I got my hands on some good liquor.”

Of course, that wasn’t the reason. She had sensed Chwima’s mood had sunk and wanted to comfort him. After all, he was the one friend she had.

Ilhwa Geomjon spoke to Yeobin, one of the Three Drunken Guests, who greeted them.

“Let’s drink together today.”

“I’m fine.”

“I want to drink, so indulge me.”

At that, Yeobin looked at Chwima. Chwima glanced at Ilhwa Geomjon once, then nodded to Yeobin.

“Then I’ll bring some anju.”

Ilhwa Geomjon and Chwima ascended Chwimongru first.

“Why are you doing something you don’t usually do?”

Even as she asked, Chwima thought of the answer himself.

Ilhwa Geomjon had secretly worked behind the scenes to reconcile him with Hyeolcheon Doma. Thanks to that, much of the old conflict between the two had been resolved.

Even though Ilhwa Geomjon didn’t know that fact, she was trying to connect him with Yeobin. Was life truly something that went around and came around?

“Because I’m tired of doing the same things every day.”

When Ilhwa Geomjon extended her hand, a bottle of liquor rose from the chest she had brought and flew to the pavilion where she sat.

“You have a golden hand. To pick out the rarest and most expensive liquor among so many. Well, a Sword Venerable’s hand is the most precious after all.”

If it had been the past, she might have flared up slightly at that joke.

But now, no anger arose. Because she was doing her best now.

Yes, her hand now was a precious hand.

Ilhwa Geomjon poured liquor for Yeobin, who had returned with anju.

The woman received the drink with both hands and emptied her cup. Ilhwa Geomjon saw her hand trembling with nervousness.

“Have another.”

Ilhwa Geomjon poured again, and again until the third cup.

After drinking three cups in a row, her hand stopped trembling.

“It feels like just yesterday that you came to Daechwirim. So much time has already passed.”

“Do you remember that time?”

Ilhwa Geomjon nodded. She remembered because Yeobin had been one of the few women among the guests. Of course, that didn’t mean Yeobin had held any significance in Ilhwa Geomjon’s life. It was only recently, as Ilhwa Geomjon underwent a change of heart, that she had come into view.

Only when life changes does martial arts change.

It was the opposite of what Geomugeuk used to say. Because martial arts changed—more precisely, because her heart toward martial arts changed—her life was changing. Thus, life and martial arts were like two sides of a coin, influencing each other.

“You were young then, I was quite young, and your Demon Venerable over there was quite dashing.”

Chwima smiled silently. Ordinarily, he would have spouted all sorts of nonsense—saying he was more dashing now, asking how one could compare that youthful spirit to the charm of a middle-aged man. But today’s Chwima was calm.

The three of them drank. Ilhwa Geomjon didn’t draw Chwima into the conversation. She left him to drink alone and spoke with Yeobin. She asked this and that, but as befitting a seasoned Sword Venerable, she did not ask questions that would make Yeobin uncomfortable.

Except for this one.

“Do you not wish to marry?”

Chwima looked at Ilhwa Geomjon with a gaze that said, *Why ask something so unnecessary,* but ignoring that gaze, Ilhwa Geomjon waited for Yeobin’s answer.

“No, I haven’t thought about it.”

“Why not?”

“What man would like a wife who drinks like this every day?”

“You could just meet someone who likes drinking. Aren’t there plenty of men in Daechwirim?”

Yeobin merely smiled.

“What about your Demon Venerable?”

Then Chwima interjected.

“Nonsense! Are you drunk?”

Ilhwa Geomjon spoke to Chwima.

“Yes, I’m drunk. How do you drink so much without getting drunk? If you’re the Drunken Demon, you should get drunk!”

Their gazes met in the air.

Ilhwa Geomjon spoke the words she had prepared.

“Do you know why you’ve been so sulky and angry lately?”

Chwima flared up. Sulky? You’re going to say that in front of my subordinate?

But Ilhwa Geomjon continued.

“It’s because you hold it in. You hold it in because you’ve grown old, because you’re a Demon Venerable. What does age matter? What does dignity matter? Listen, friend. As you grow older, you need to know how to reveal your desires gracefully. If you want to stop being angry and stop sulking, that’s what you need to do.”

Though she herself had failed to reveal her desires gracefully.

That was why. Why she had something to say on this matter. Whenever she fell into thought, she would inevitably recall her own shameful desires from the past.

“Stop talking nonsense and go. Stop wasting someone else’s precious gift.”

“No.”

“Then I’ll go.”

Chwima descended Chwimongru with a bottle in hand and set a boat afloat on the lake alone.

The two women watched Chwima’s boat slowly drifting toward the reflection of the moon on the water.

“Actually, those words were meant for you. I know that friend won’t listen to such talk.”

Yeobin looked at her with a surprised face.

“If you keep holding it in, and holding it in again. At first, you’ll become sulky, and later, you’ll grow angry.”

Because she understood the meaning all too well, Yeobin couldn’t answer at all.

“Are you scared? That even this life of watching from the side might disappear?”

She still said nothing.

“If so, don’t be afraid. You’ve watched enough by now. That’s attachment and habit. Let that life drift away.”

Silence fell over both Chwimongru and the lake.

How much time passed like that? A cool breeze blew from somewhere. That breeze sobered Ilhwa Geomjon, and her mind snapped into focus.

Am I in any position to give advice to anyone? What do I even know about Yeobin?

She thought she had gone too far in her concern for both of them. Should she say she didn’t remember because the Outland liquor was too strong?

“Don’t think too deeply into words spoken while drunk.”

Just as Ilhwa Geomjon tried to rise from her seat, Yeobin finally opened her mouth.

“You saw correctly, Sword Venerable. I have already passed through being sulky and am now angry. It has been several years since I became angry.”

It was the first time she had revealed her true feelings. Because she had always shown a polite demeanor, no one had realized she was angry.

“Seeing how I still tremble like this, I suppose I’m afraid even this angry life will disappear.”

The eyes of Yeobin, who was revealing her innermost thoughts to someone for the first time, trembled.

Ilhwa Geomjon had no more words to say. So she filled Yeobin’s cup with the greatest courage she could offer a guest.

“For today, become a true drunkard.”

After saying that, she leaped away. Though she claimed to be drunk, she crossed the lake with a display of lightness skill too brilliant and elegant for such a claim.

When she landed on the lakeshore and looked back, she saw Yeobin flying toward Chwima’s boat on the lake with a bottle of liquor in hand.

A smile formed on Ilhwa Geomjon’s lips.

She envied that girl’s youth, which allowed her to act first and regret later.

But it was fine. From now on, she too intended to live revealing her desires.

Elegantly and gracefully, instead.

* * *

I arrived at Donggwonmun with my brother.

Because I had trained from White Fist to Black Fist here in the past, every passerby knew my face.

The gazes and attitudes toward me were completely different between then and now.

Receiving polite greetings from the Iron Fists, I entered the inner courtyard.

In the training ground of the inner courtyard, Gwonma was training the Black Fists.

He personally guided only the Black Fists’ training, while Cheonsohui handled the training of the remaining Iron Fists.

“Master! I’ve returned.”

“So you’ve come?”

Despite Gwonma’s fearsome face, seeing him after so long was a joy.

Compared to Gwonma’s blunt welcome, my brother greeted me with gentle eyes.

“You’re here, Eldest Young Master? Please set down anything heavy over there.”

I wasn’t one to just stand by.

“My chest is heavier! Are you discriminating against people?”

The reason for the discrimination was soon revealed.

“I’ve heard many things from Branch Leader Gwak. The Eldest Young Master suffered greatly making those gauntlets.”

“It was only right that I did. Today I’ve brought the completed gauntlets.”

Geomuyang opened the chest to show the gauntlets.

Meanwhile, Cheonsohui, who had missed the chance to greet me properly and only bowed her head, came over and whispered a greeting.

“I’m glad you returned safely, Young Cult Leader.”

“How many times must I tell you to call me Martial Brother when it’s just us?”

Cheonsohui looked at Gwonma and my brother with eyes that said, *But it’s not just us right now.*

I said it again so those two could hear. It was a shout disguised as a whisper.

“It’s a relief we have such a kind Martial Sister under our heartless Master.”

Gwonma looked at me and said,

“The kinder your Martial Sister is, the shorter her lifespan will be.”

“That won’t do. I take it back about the kind Martial Sister! Martial Sister, this is a cruel murim!”

Those watching smiled. Everyone knew my personality well, so the atmosphere was good.

Gwonma picked up the gauntlets and examined them.

“You’ve put a thin steel mesh in the palm.”

“That’s right. It’s light yet incredibly durable. The technology of Cheolbang is concentrated in that part.”

Gwonma handed the gauntlets to Cheonsohui.

Cheonsohui wore the gauntlets and tested their power.

“The fit is excellent.”

She had an Iron Fist bring a sword.

“Strike with all your might!”

The Iron Fist who brought the sword swung it forcefully, and she blocked the blade with her palm.

Though it was quite a powerful strike, her palm was unharmed.

The watching Black Fists exclaimed in admiration.

Gwonma nodded with satisfaction.

“Excellent gauntlets. You’ve worked hard.”

“We can supply fifty for now, but within a few months, we’ll produce enough for all of Donggwonmun’s Iron Fists.”

“It’s thanks to your care. Thank you.”

Gwonma expressed his gratitude sincerely, and Geomuyang smiled with the satisfaction of his past toils being resolved.

While Cheonsohui distributed the gauntlets to the Black Fists, we walked through the inner courtyard together.

Gwonma mainly spoke with Geomuyang rather than me. From matters regarding this gauntlet production to what support Donggwonmun needed. They conversed about various work-related matters.

I walked silently together and felt it clearly. My brother was indeed better at this sort of thing. It was a moment that made me newly realize that for proper results to come about, someone needed to have a meticulous and thorough personality.

Gwonma sent a transmission just as we were bidding farewell and turning to leave.

—Welcome back.

—I’ll stop by again later.

That one line of transmission from my Master was enough of a welcome for me. It felt better than setting off hundreds of firecrackers.

At the entrance of Donggwonmun, I spoke to Geomuyang.

“I’m hungry. Let’s eat together.”

“Since when do we eat together?”

“I went out to the Outlands this time, and it made me think a lot about Father and you.”

After a brief pause, Geomuyang said,

“Let’s have a meal with Father soon.”

I understood. It was his answer to my suggestion to take care of Father. It meant we would do it together.

“You’re buying.”

“No. You, who has plenty of money, should buy.”

Surprisingly, my brother is getting better.

But I mustn’t let my guard down. Mistakes between people happen precisely at times like this. When things are just starting to get better. The more it’s like this, the more you need to keep your eyes wide open and watch the other person.

I stood for a moment watching my brother’s retreating figure, then turned in the opposite direction. Where I headed was Namdojong.

Hyeolcheon Doma was in his quarters.

“I’ve returned, Elder.”

“Why come here instead of resting from your journey?”

“Seeing you is my rest. But why are you sorting books?”

He was placing martial arts secret manuals on the shelf instead of poetry collections and literary works.

“These are secret manuals I read when I was young. I’m having Daeryong read them.”

“Everyone overflows with love for their disciples.”

“‘Everyone’?”

Originally, it had been about the Heavenly Wind Cult Leader.

“I mean that I too am loved by Master Gwonma.”

“Hardly. You should count yourself lucky if you don’t get beaten.”

“Shall I help you organize?”

“I’m done.”

I crouched down and rummaged through the books Hyeolcheon Doma had stacked.

“There are many books I haven’t seen before. Let me borrow them.”

“Do you have time to read?”

“I have to, even if I don’t.”

As I was rummaging through the books, Hyeolcheon Doma asked while organizing the bookshelf.

“Were they the same as the ones from the Murim Alliance?”

I had been with Hyeolcheon Doma when capturing Baekcheongyeong of the Murim Alliance.

“Yes, they seem to be on the same side.”

“This murim never has a peaceful day.”

Hyeolcheon Doma asked no more about it and selected a few books to lend me.

“Borrow more when you’re done reading.”

“I’ll visit again.”

I returned to my quarters with the books Hyeolcheon Doma lent me.

Jeokyeon, who had been waiting, held out a bottle of liquor.

“What’s this?”

“A gift.”

“A gift?”

“When buying gifts for Chwima-nim, we pooled our money and bought one bottle.”

Looking at it, it was one of the more expensive liquors among those gifted to Chwima. It seemed the guards had secretly bought one bottle for themselves while purchasing them.

“Where would you guys have money?”

“You may not know who we are, but we are the guards of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s Young Cult Leader. We get paid a lot.”

It was Jeokyeon’s self-deprecating joke. Because rather than protecting me, they probably felt like they were the ones being protected.

“Thank you for the gift. I’ll save it and drink it.”

“Then we shall take our leave.”

After Jeokyeon left, I washed up and changed into new clothes.

Instead of extinguishing the lights and lying on the bed, I gathered books and liquor.

Snap.

I opened my own space with the Spacetime Shift Technique.

A clear sea, white sandy beach, blue sky, broad-leaved trees, and a comfortable chair beneath them.

“You rascals, I’ve missed you!”

I laid my body in the comfortable chair.

“Ah, this is nice.”

Such happiness in this place! Though the Heavenly Wind Cult Leader warned that those who learned illusion techniques would fall into their own worlds, I could not compromise on this space alone.

Looking back on my past life, what had been most important and necessary as I grew older was a space of my own, and time of my own.

At least here, I could forget everything in the world and enjoy my own quiet rest.

Today, I added one more thing to my sanctuary.

A small chair appeared beside my chair.

I placed Bigwe on it.

“How is it? Do you like it?”

Bigwe was smiling widely, pleased.

Reclining in the chair, I took a sip of liquor and read my book. All that could be heard were the sound of the wind, the cries of seagulls, and the turning of pages. I wouldn’t trade even all of murim for this moment.

The first day back at the cult, which had been even busier since my return, was passing by like this.

Absolute Regression, Episode 334

Chapter 334: Do You Like the Wicked Little Demon That Much?
-----------------------------------------------------------
The next morning, someone I never expected came to my quarters.

It was Jang Ho.

"Young Cult Lord."

"What brings you here at this early hour?"

"I heard you had returned, so I came to pay my respects. Did you have a good trip?"

"I was battered raw by sandstorms the whole way."

"I've never been beyond the frontier, so I'd like to go someday."

"When sand crunches in your rice, that's when you'll think, 'Ah, now I know why our Young Cult Lord complained so much about being battered.'"

Jang Ho laughed. He rarely smiled in front of his subordinates, so this wonderful smile was always mine alone.

"Oh, and please take this."

Jang Ho held out what he had brought. When I unwrapped the paper, a painting emerged.

It was the back view of a man with a sword at his waist, standing on a cliff. The moment I saw the painting, I knew who it was. It was me.

"Who is this terrifyingly handsome man?"

"It is you, Young Cult Lord. My skill is still lacking, but I wanted my first painting to be of you. I dared not paint your noble countenance, so I painted your back."

When he had said he wanted to paint after retirement, I had bought him the tools artists used. I hadn't known his first work would be of me.

"I had no idea you were this skilled."

It wasn't something I said just to be nice. It was truly a magnificent painting, as if done by a master artist.

"This is the first time I've received a painting as a gift."

"It's my first time as well. Giving a painting as a gift."

"I should go brag to Father. I'll tell him, 'Though the Demon Lord receives his salary from Father, he gave a painting gift to me.'"

Though he knew it was a joke, Jang Ho panicked, as if merely imagining it made him break into a sweat.

"Please, anything but that."

His flustered look was amusing, so the teasing continued.

"How much could I get if I took this to the Central Plains to sell? A painting drawn by the Demon Lord of the Heavenly Demon Cult—wouldn't it be priceless? You wouldn't buy it? Really? You're saying you'd buy it for just that much?"

With a troubled expression, Jang Ho waved his hands, pleading for me to stop.

"Thank you. I shall treasure it for life."

"When my skills improve later, I'll paint you an even better one."

"Then paint my front view. It'd be a waste not to capture this handsome face, don't you think?"

I didn't miss Jang Ho's slight flinch.

"What? Don't tell me you don't think I'm handsome?"

Now Jang Ho's counterattack began.

"It is a face favored by the youth of today."

"But not a handsome face?"

He didn't answer, but his expression was one of agreement.

"Then who is handsome in your eyes? Pick from those we know."

Was it the Poison King? No, the Poison King looked younger than me, so probably not. The Asura of Extreme Evil was handsome, but he wouldn't have seen beneath the mask. Ah, then it must be the Drunken Demon—just as I thought, an unexpected name came out.

"Isn't Lord Fist Demon handsome?"

"What? Are you crazy?"

The words slipped out automatically.

"He's manly and cool. Isn't he handsome?"

"You really think that?"

"Yes."

Knowing he was sincere, I shook my head and sighed deeply.

"I'm truly disappointed in your taste. Please, before you leave, tell me it was a joke!"

Jang Ho smiled as if he had expected this reaction. But he never relented.

"Then I shall see you again."

Jang Ho bowed politely and left my quarters.

After he left, my guards, including Jeogyeon, came to admire the painting.

"It's splendid, the painting."

"Isn't it? To think such talent was going to waste. You all should take up hobbies too. For your lives, a single brushstroke may be better than a hundred strikes of the blade."

Knowing that these words of mine were now sincere, the guards answered with booming voices.

"Understood, Young Cult Lord!"

I spoke as I left my quarters.

"I'll ask you about it later. So think hard. Ah, and hang the painting somewhere visible in the room. I'm going to the Valley of Evil. You don't have to follow."

* * *

When I entered the Valley of Evil, it was different from usual.

The place had a festive atmosphere.

Every building was decorated splendidly, and the masks of the Faceless Guests were all painted in gaudy, colorful hues. Bonfires were lit here and there, roasting meat, and wine was being carried about in jars. They were not the frightening masks of usual times. Today, everyone was in high spirits.

I asked a Faceless Guest who recognized me and greeted me.

"What is the occasion?"

"Today is the birthday of Lord Asura."

"The Asura of Extreme Evil's birthday?"

"Yes, my lord."

"Do not spread word that I came and went."

For now, I turned around and left the Valley of Evil.

I hadn't known it was the Asura of Extreme Evil's birthday. Not knowing is one thing, but now that I knew, I couldn't go empty-handed.

What should I give as a gift?

Last time, for the Blood Heaven Blade Demon's birthday, I gave the finest blood poison wine. But the relationship then and now was different; at the time, that alone had been a great gift.

After pondering briefly, I went straight to the Heavenly Demon Hall.

"I came again because I wanted to see Father."

Father looked at me with suspicious eyes. Coming again today after paying respects yesterday was somehow suspicious.

"Would you like to play a game of Baduk?"

"I'm busy."

"Then would you care to spar?"

"What is your business?"

I spoke carefully to Father.

"Please let me take just one more treasure from the Heavenly Demon Treasury."

Father didn't even ask what it was for.

"Impossible!"

Yes, he had specially opened it to commemorate me becoming the Young Cult Lord. It was only natural he wouldn't give it just because I came asking so suddenly.

"What must I do for you to give it to me?"

"What can you do?"

"I can massage your shoulders. I'll massage them for life."

"I absolutely despise others touching my body."

"Am I a stranger?"

Of course, it was a futile attempt nonetheless.

"I'll come to play Baduk every day."

"Playing with a novice gives bad habits."

"I'll train in martial arts diligently every day."

"Do as you please."

"Do you not have a hidden daughter? I'm talking about a daughter you secretly hid away, concealing her identity to keep her from danger. She's grown up somewhere under an ordinary status. However, that daughter is now in danger. 'My son, go save her in secret. You are the only one I can trust. In exchange, I shall give you one treasure.'"

The corners of Father's lips curled upward, brimming with scorn.

"If you were worried about danger, shouldn't you keep her at the main cult?"

"Well, perhaps the elder brother, blinded by ambition, was a concern, so you hid her."

"Looking at your current state, I worry more about you."

"What can I possibly give to one who has everything in the world? Just give it to me!"

At the shameless demand, Father rose from his chair. It seemed like he was about to ask if I needed a beating to come to my senses.

"Let's walk."

I walked with Father through the inner garden. All the demonic practitioners passing by respectfully bowed their heads.

To my surprise, the walk led to the Heavenly Demon Treasury.

"Choose one and come out."

That he gave it so readily made me rather uneasy.

"Why are you giving it so easily?"

"So that futile desire does not accumulate."

"What do you mean?"

"'When I become the Heavenly Demon, I'll use it to my heart's content.' Such desire."

I understood Father's heart.

"You thought that far ahead?"

"Because it is that important. Both you, and the Heavenly Demon Treasury."

At the words "you too," my heart welled up.

What was more surprising was that Father already knew.

"Is the Asura of Extreme Evil that dear to you?"

"You knew."

He had known that today was the Asura's birthday and had guessed that my visit was to get a gift for him.

He could have asked what was so good about that person.

"Take it and go out."

Shortly after, I came out with a treasure.

"Futile desire has not accumulated. Instead, respect and affection for Father have filled me..."

Without listening to the rest, Father strode away.

I stood for a moment, watching Father's back. Normally, Father's back seemed large, dependable, lonely, and desolate—all sorts of emotions—but today, I felt his deep affection for me.

When I first regressed, there had certainly been a part of me that was confident because I had lived longer than Father. 'I lived longer than Father!' I had harbored such thoughts.

And now I feel it.
