Moved by the sight, Yang Seon broke into a bright smile and said,

“My brother-in-law was right. This is a moment that will never come again.”

Jo Chunbae and Yang In entered their room.

Lady Yun had directed that the largest room be given to her eldest son-in-law.

The two sat on the bed, staring blankly out the window. A day like a storm had truly passed. Jo Chunbae wondered if he was dreaming.

Yang In felt exactly the same.

“Live long enough, and even a day like this arrives.”

“So it seems.”

In the distance beyond the window, the sunset was beautifully setting. They could not even remember when they had last sat side by side watching the sunset like this.

“You have suffered much all this time, my dear.”

Today, those words sank especially deep into his heart. Yes, this one line was enough. Because failing to say this easy single phrase was what made this precious hardship into a living hell.

“You have suffered too.”

Jo Chunbae knew. He knew how tenaciously his wife had lived, raising their children and doing all manner of difficult work.

Just then, a maid came from outside and said,

“Dinner is ready.”

The two sat blankly for a moment at this reality that felt entirely unreal.

Then Jo Chunbae stood up first, extending his hand to his wife.

“My lady, let us go eat.”

Yang In smiled, took her husband's hand, and stood up. It was the first vacation she had ever had in her life.

“Then shall we go eat a meal prepared by others? I—I am going to eat a great deal.”

Absolute Regression, Episode 339

Chapter 339: Impossible to Refute, Killing to Silence Is Also Impossible.
-------------------------------------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk and the escorts of the Yellow Dragon Escort Agency were waiting in Cheongsu Forest. Dressed in an escort’s uniform, Geom Mugeuk looked like a young, newly recruited escort.

―He still hasn’t come, even though the appointed time has passed.

Geom Mugeuk answered Hwang Won’s telepathic message. Hwang Won was acting as temporary chief of the agency.

―Give the order to turn back immediately.

―Shouldn’t we wait a little longer?

―No. Give the order at once.

Hwang Won followed Geom Mugeuk’s advice.

“We’re turning back.”

At his command, the escorts turned the carts around.

Just as they were about to leave Cheongsu Forest, a man stepped in front of the escort convoy.

The man had pulled down a bamboo hat to conceal his face. He was Black Serpent Corps’ Black Thirteen.

“Where is Chief Escort Gong?”

He had been hiding and watching until he saw them turn back, then revealed himself.

Hwang Won answered Black Thirteen’s question.

“Chief Escort Gong is dead.”

Geom Mugeuk had told him in advance: if the contact asked, answer truthfully that he was dead.

“Dead? Why did he die?”

“He was killed by those demonic cult bastards.”

They were telling him the truth in case he had somehow heard a report about what had happened.

“I don’t know the details myself. All I know is that he got mixed up with the demonic cult and was beaten to death.”

Black Thirteen was not particularly surprised. Gong Chan, whom he had met before, did not seem like the type to live a long life. He had been arrogant despite not knowing his place, and his eyes had a way of irritating people.

“The chief sent me in his place as the person in charge.”

Black Thirteen looked at the escorts behind Hwang Won. Apart from the young escort who looked like a new recruit, they were all people he had seen last time.

“Hand over the cargo. You can confirm the details with the chief later.”

Hwang Won’s businesslike, rigid attitude was also something Geom Mugeuk had instructed him to adopt.

When Black Thirteen failed to respond, Geom Mugeuk quickly sent Hwang Won a telepathic message.

―Be more forceful.

Hwang Won hardened his expression and spoke the words Geom Mugeuk had given him.

“To be honest, I don’t like these secretive escort missions. I’m only doing this because it’s the chief’s order, even though I don’t want to. I’ll report everything exactly as it happened. That you refused to hand over the cargo.”

Hwang Won turned away and said to the escorts,

“We’re leaving.”

They had nothing to lose. That was the heart of their plan.

After glaring at Hwang Won’s back for a moment, Black Thirteen let go of his suspicions.

“Wait. Follow me.”

Following him, they arrived at a secluded spot in the forest, where a cart loaded with luggage and more than ten Black Serpent Corps warriors were waiting. They had not concealed their faces, and the pitch-black snake-head tattoos carved into their foreheads looked truly menacing.

The instant he saw the boxes, Geom Mugeuk released his qi and examined them for signs of life. They could have been a trap.

Fortunately, he sensed breathing. It was an exceedingly faint breath—children!

When Geom Mugeuk nodded, Hwang Won gave the order.

“Move them quickly.”

The escorts began transferring the boxes to their own carts.

Hwang Won asked Black Thirteen,

“Where should we take them?”

“I already told you the destination, didn’t I?”

When the conversation reached this point, Geom Mugeuk had given Hwang Won the following response:

“Do you really think that tight-lipped Chief Escort Gong would have told us before he died?”

When Hwang Won had asked whether it was all right to be that confrontational, Geom Mugeuk had said it was a trick meant to make them look at the finger instead of the moon.

As expected, the Black Serpent Corps focused on Hwang Won’s attitude. One of the swordsmen stepped forward and warned him.

“You! Watch your mouth.”

Black Thirteen stopped his subordinate, then said to Hwang Won,

“Deliver the cargo to the Flowing Sword Sect by midnight the day after tomorrow. If you’re even a quarter-hour late, I’ll kill you with my own hands.”

It was at the very moment the destination was revealed.

Whoosh!

A flying dagger embedded itself in the neck of the swordsman who had issued the warning.

As he crumpled to the ground, warriors launched a surprise attack from every direction.

“It’s an ambush! Stop them!”

But the attacking warriors were overwhelmingly superior to the Black Serpent swordsmen.

Unable to properly counterattack, the swordsmen fell one after another.

Geom Mugeuk kept the escorts clustered together in a circle so they would not be drawn into the fight.

Black Thirteen hurled himself toward the forest behind them.

But with a thud, he went tumbling across the ground. His bamboo hat flew off, revealing his face. A pitch-black snake-head tattoo was also carved into his forehead. Now that he could see it clearly, the shape of the snake differed from those tattooed on his subordinates. Apparently, the snake’s appearance varied according to rank.

Someone walked out of the forest.

Amazingly, the man who had subdued Black Thirteen was none other than Jin Hagun, Commander of the Demon-Slaying Corps.

He sauntered over and sealed the acupoints at Black Thirteen’s throat and waist as the man clutched his abdomen in pain.

By then, all the other Black Serpent swordsmen had either been killed or subdued. The entire matter had been settled in the blink of an eye.

The Demon-Slaying Corps warriors surrounded the escorts and pointed their swords at them.

One of them ran over and opened a box. When he lifted away the silken cloth used as camouflage, a sleeping child was revealed inside.

“There’s a child here.”

“Is the child unharmed?”

“Yes. The child is asleep under the Breath-Hiding Technique.”

Jin Hagun joyfully opened another box himself. A child was sleeping inside that one as well.

Just then, another Demon-Slaying Corps warrior approached Jin Hagun and reported,

“These men are escorts from the Yellow Dragon Escort Agency.”

Jin Hagun shouted in fury.

“Righteous-path escorts trading with the Black Serpent Corps?”

The Demon-Slaying Corps had come tracking the children, unaware that the people who had arrived for the exchange were from the Yellow Dragon Escort Agency.

“Drop your weapons and kneel, all of you.”

Hwang Won’s heart sank.

The young cult master of the demonic cult was here with him at the place where the Black Serpent Corps were transporting children. Would the Demon-Slaying Corps warriors really believe their story?

The truth would eventually come to light. There had been too many witnesses to what happened at the Yellow Dragon Escort Agency.

But if a fight broke out here, they might all be killed before they could even properly reveal the truth.

All those worries flashed through Hwang Won’s mind in that brief moment.

“Kill anyone who refuses to kneel!”

When the order came down, Hwang Won instructed the escorts to kneel for the time being and do as they were told.

Jin Hagun was holding one of the children. Then he noticed that the Demon-Slaying Corps warrior beside him was staring at someone. The other warriors were doing the same.

Jin Hagun turned around while still holding the child.

Everyone else was kneeling, but one young escort remained standing. He grinned at Jin Hagun and greeted him.

“Have you been well?”

The moment Jin Hagun realized that the man was Geom Mugeuk, his eyes widened.

“You!”

He was the man who appeared in his dreams—the irritating man who always seemed to be one step ahead of him, even there. Now he was dressed in an escort’s uniform and smiling at him.

“Why are you here?”

“I came to obtain children to use as materials for the ritual.”

Hwang Won, who was kneeling, was horrified at those words.

What if he actually believed him? Wait! Could that really have been the case? Had Geom Mugeuk deceived him and brought him here? That was right—the young cult master of the demonic cult would never have come to rescue children, would he?

Unaccustomed to Geom Mugeuk’s joking manner, Hwang Won even entertained such thoughts for a moment.

Fortunately, Jin Hagun was used to Geom Mugeuk’s jokes.

“Are you joking again the moment we meet?”

“It’s a long story. To cut to the conclusion, I came to rescue the children.”

“If I’d known this would happen, we wouldn’t have needed to come.”

Though he said it jokingly, it was high praise for Geom Mugeuk.

“Everyone, rise.”

At Jin Hagun’s words, the kneeling escorts stood up.

They were relieved, but at the same time astonished. They had known that the young cult master of the demonic cult was extraordinary, but they had not realized he was on such terms with the commander of the Demon-Slaying Corps.

“We obtained information that the Black Serpent Corps had been kidnapping and selling children, so we began an investigation. We finally found the evidence.”

“We discovered that they were selling the children, but what will you do about the buyers?”

“Of course, we won’t leave them alone either.”

Jin Hagun wanted to storm into the Flowing Sword Sect immediately and punish them for purchasing the children. But the Flowing Sword Sect was a sect belonging to the unorthodox path.

For that reason, they could not simply attack without clear evidence. If they made one wrong move and the matter escalated into a conflict with the Unorthodox Alliance, the consequences would be irreversible.

“From now on, we’ll investigate the Flowing Sword Sect in secret. If we find evidence that they purchased the kidnapped children, the main corps will not forgive them.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled at his firm resolve.

“That sounds like the Commander of the Demon-Slaying Corps.”

He was not being sarcastic. He truly meant it. Jin Hagun possessed a spirit unlike the one he had shown before.

“However, there’s a problem with your plan. If the children promised this time fail to arrive, they’ll realize that something has gone wrong and destroy all the evidence. They might pin everything on some expendable subordinate and cut off the tail.”

Jin Hagun agreed with him and asked for Geom Mugeuk’s opinion.

“Then what should we do?”

“Let us continue the escort mission.”

Jin Hagun, the warriors of the Demon-Slaying Corps, and the escorts all looked at Geom Mugeuk in astonishment.

“The children are asleep under the Breath-Hiding Technique, so we can’t force them awake. They’ll only awaken when the proper time comes. Let’s deliver the children and find evidence that the master of the Flowing Sword Sect was involved in this affair.”

“And if something happens to the children in the process?”

“If something happens despite you and me being there, wouldn’t that be a natural disaster?”

Jin Hagun fell into thought. If anyone else had made such a proposal, he would have rejected it. He had no intention of taking risks with the children’s lives.

But the person who would be accompanying him was Geom Mugeuk. He knew exactly what kind of person Geom Mugeuk was.

He did not deliberate for long. Unless they uprooted this matter properly, the children would continue to be kidnapped in other ways.

When Jin Hagun nodded, Geom Mugeuk shouted to the escorts,

“Let’s continue the escort mission.”

* * *

Hwang Won looked back.

He saw Geom Mugeuk and Jin Hagun sitting side by side on a cart. They were not two escorts sitting together, nor were they merely two powerful martial artists. They were the son of the Heavenly Demon and the grandson of the Martial Alliance Lord sitting beside each other.

It was a sight he would never witness again in his lifetime.

Hwang Won and the escorts were truly having an astonishing experience.

“How is the Alliance Lord?”

“He remains in good health.”

After a brief pause, Jin Hagun said,

“He sometimes speaks of you.”

“What does he say?”

“If you have to kill one person among the demonic cult, make sure it’s the young cult master.”

Geom Mugeuk laughed and took up the remark.

“He must have meant the opposite. If you have to spare one person among the demonic cult, make sure it’s the young cult master.”

“Think of it however you like.”

Through their conversation, Hwang Won and the escorts realized that what Geom Mugeuk had told U Sochu was true. He was undoubtedly well acquainted with the Martial Alliance Lord.

“What about Miss Jin?”

“She’s still as reckless as ever.”

Despite his words, Jin Haryeong had also changed since the past. She was no longer merely an immature young lady, but was growing into a central figure among the younger generation of the righteous path.

“But why did you come out alone?”

“I had to leave in a hurry, so I couldn’t notify anyone.”

Jin Hagun looked at Geom Mugeuk. He was not the type to lie, so he really must have come out alone.

“Looks like even your cult has given up on you.”

“Seems that way. So at least you should treat me warmly.”

Jin Hagun turned away with an incredulous expression.

But soon, he spoke in a calm voice.

“Since that day, I’ve been trying to look people straight in the eye.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled without a word. Jin Hagun understood. The true nature of a man named Geom Mugeuk could be seen when he was silent like this—when he looked up at the sky with those clear, deep eyes. One must not be deceived by his chatter.

The cart continued onward, rolling and rolling, until it arrived at the Flowing Sword Sect.

* * *

Late that night, the carts entered through the rear gate of the Flowing Sword Sect.

Manhong of the Flowing Sword Sect received the cargo.

“Move them carefully. Don’t stack them. Set them down separately.”

The escorts from the Yellow Dragon Escort Agency carried the boxes into the warehouse.

Once all the boxes had been moved, Manhong handed the escorts a heavy pouch filled with silver.

After the escorts left, Manhong opened the boxes to check the children.

Just then, someone entered the warehouse. It was none other than Taejo, master of the Flowing Sword Sect. He was an absolute master of the unorthodox path who had established his own school in the martial world through the Flowing Sword Technique.

“Did you really have to use righteous-path men?”

“It couldn’t be helped. They say the Demon-Slaying Corps is searching everyone connected to the Black Serpent Corps, right down to those who merely shared a drink with them.”

“Damn those unlucky bastards! How many children arrived this time?”

“Fifteen.”

“When will they wake up?”

“They’ll wake up in three shichen.”

“Call me when they do.”

Taejo was just about to leave the warehouse when—

A commotion sounded from outside. Just as the clash of weapons could be heard—

Bang!

The warehouse door was smashed open, and a group of martial artists entered. Leading them was none other than Jinhagun.

Jinhagun walked up to Taejo and calmly introduced himself.

“I am Jinhagun, Commander of the Demon Extermination Unit.”

“It is a pleasure to meet you. I am honored to meet the renowned Commander of the Demon Extermination Unit.”

Though Taejo could guess why they had come, he remained composed.

“Even if you are a renowned figure, I wonder whether it is acceptable to barge into another sect’s grounds like this.”

“I apologize for that. The matter was urgent, and I had no choice.”

“What is the matter?”

“Recently, the Black Death Gang has been kidnapping children and selling them. Our unit has been monitoring and tracking their vile deeds, and we confirmed that a box containing children had been brought into the Yuggeom Sect.”

Jinhagun’s gaze turned toward the boxes.

“I believe those are the ones. May I inspect them?”

“If I said no, would you leave them alone?”

“If that were the case, I would not have come in here in the first place.”

At Jinhagun’s nod, two members of the Demon Extermination Unit opened the boxes. They confirmed that children were asleep inside.

“I know nothing about this.”

Even with the evidence right before him, Taejo denied everything without hesitation.

“I came here because I had something to discuss with one of my subordinates. What is the meaning of this?”

“I do not know either. I will call the person in charge of this place and find out.”

Just as Geom Mugeuk had said, he intended to pin the blame on a subordinate and slip away.

Then a voice came from behind.

“Isn’t that a lie?”

Taejo turned around in surprise. Someone rose from behind the boxes. It was Geom Mugeuk, who had been hiding there.

“There’s no point denying it. I heard everything.”

Of course, Taejo had no intention of meekly accepting that. He calmly challenged Jinhagun.

“I do not know what that young martial artist claims to have heard, but who would believe the words of a subordinate you planted?”

“He is not my subordinate.”

“You expect me to believe that?”

“I wish he were my subordinate, but he is someone no one in this world could possibly take as one.”

Unable to understand what he meant, Taejo looked back and forth between Jinhagun and Geom Mugeuk in puzzlement.

“Whether he is your subordinate or not makes no difference. Judging by his clothes, he appears to be a caravan escort, nothing more than a petty thief who sneaked into the warehouse. I cannot acknowledge someone like him as a witness.”

Both men appeared at ease. And the winner of this confrontation had been decided from the start.

Jinhagun spoke calmly.

“Unfortunately, you cannot refute him, nor can you easily insist on your own version of events. You cannot silence him by killing him, either. In fact, everyone except you will believe what he says…”

Jinhagun turned his head and looked at Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk’s lips twitched impatiently, as if asking, *When will it be my turn to speak?*

“You have encountered a witness you should never have met.”

Absolute Regression

Chapter 340

Chapter 340: Confess and go to the Murim Alliance, hold out and go to the Demonic Cult.
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Taejo examined Geom Mugeuk with a startled expression.

What in the world was this young bastard saying?

Killing to silence them was impossible? Ridiculous.

If he set his mind to it, there was no one he could not kill. He had, in fact, lived that very sort of life.

Those he could defeat, he mocked and killed before everyone’s eyes; those he could not kill openly, he killed by stabbing them in the back. If that proved difficult, he commissioned assassin organizations, or won over the target’s acquaintances and had them poison him.

And to someone who had lived such a life, what? Killing to silence them was impossible? It was laughable.

‘It’s a bluff. He’s trying to draw out my confession somehow.’

With a relaxed smile, Taejo asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Seeing how highly the Demon Extermination Unit Leader regards you, you must be quite someone. Just who are you?”

Geom Mugeuk revealed his identity.

“I am the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

Taejo stared blankly for a moment, then burst into laughter. It was one of the most absurd and out-of-nowhere answers he had ever heard in his life.

“Indeed, only someone of such status would make the Demon Extermination Unit Leader’s words true.”

He spoke as if acknowledging it, but he did not believe it in the slightest.

“Then let me ask you one thing. What is the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult doing with the Demon Extermination Unit of the Martial Alliance, which was created to destroy demons, of all things?”

Geom Mugeuk opened the box, looked down at the sleeping child, and answered.

“We are carrying out an operation to sweep away trash.”

In that instant, Taejo’s expression twitched.

Geom Mugeuk gently adjusted the child, who had been lying uncomfortably, and continued.

“If you ask the martial artists of the world who is the most evil, most of them answer that it’s us. It’s truly unfair. Once you come out and look around, there are so many vile bastards who make you wonder whether they’re even human. This incident is the same. Kidnapping someone else’s child, selling them for money, and who knows what they intended to do after buying that child? Even we don’t commit trashy acts like this.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze moved from the child to Taejo.

“What exactly were you planning to do?”

Since he had called it trash just before and now asked this, it was no different from calling Taejo trash.

If Taejo had followed his heart, he would have rushed over at once and hacked the man to pieces.

But he could not do that. Since the box containing the child had been found in his warehouse, he had to get through this moment somehow.

He quickly sent a sound transmission to Manhong.

—You’ll have to suffer a bit. I’ll get you out as quickly as possible.

—Yes, Sect Master.

Manhong truly did not want to. This was an incident the Demon Extermination Unit Leader had personally broken into, and if he were dragged away like this, there was no telling what would happen. But he could not defy the Sect Master’s order. His only choices were death or being taken away.

Manhong stepped forward.

“The truth is, I am the one who did it. I apologize.”

Taejo pretended to be shocked and rebuked him.

“Is that true?”

“I did not know they were kidnapped children. I merely asked for children because I needed them.”

“You fool! You should have looked into it properly before proceeding.”

“I apologize.”

Taejo also put on an apologetic expression.

“It seems my subordinate also did not know they were kidnapped children. It is partly my fault as well for failing to check in advance.”

He drove the matter toward being a minor mistake that had occurred in the course of business.

His subordinate insisted he had not known they were kidnapped children, and he insisted he had not known this fact. Now then, if that was what they were saying, what could you do about it?

Unfortunately for them, the person they were dealing with was not only Jin Hagun.

Geom Mugeuk raised his hand high.

“That is why a witness is needed. The witness is right here. I heard everything. I even heard you say to call you when the child woke up.”

Taejo glared fiercely at Geom Mugeuk, but like a true madman, he was not frightened at all. Yes, this was precisely why they must have entrusted him with the role of witness.

“Since there is a witness, you will have to come with us for now.”

“I will not go.”

A taut tension flowed between Jin Hagun and Taejo. Taejo knew very well that the moment he was dragged away, the problem would become troublesome.

“You must go.”

“Are you threatening me now?”

“If you have committed a crime, it would be a threat. If you are innocent, it would be cooperation.”

Taejo could sense it. They would not leave simply because he asked nicely.

“I was going to understand and let this pass, valuing Unit Leader Jin’s righteousness in wanting to save the children, but if you insist on being this stubborn, I cannot allow it. Unit Leader Jin, do you understand what kind of situation this is right now?”

“Please tell me.”

“The Martial Alliance has attacked an unorthodox sect. This could become the beginning of an Orthodox-Unorthodox War.”

He deliberately mentioned the provocative phrase Orthodox-Unorthodox War. This was the moment to push hard.

Taejo knew Jin Hagun’s weakness well.

“Unit Leader Jin has not yet become the official successor. What do you think will happen if you cause such a major conflict with the Evil Dao Alliance? Should you not be prudent, if only for your own sake?”

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk cut in obnoxiously.

“Are you going to be fine? If you cause a dispute with the Martial Alliance?”

In the end, Taejo could not hold back and roared.

“You shut your mouth!”

Unable to endure Geom Mugeuk’s taunting, his composure had shattered. Whenever he tried to do something, the man kept cutting him off from the side.

Seeing him explode in fury, Jin Hagun laughed inwardly. Yes, it makes you angry. I know that well.

As if he had decided words would no longer suffice, Taejo shouted toward the outside.

“Come in.”

Then the martial artists of Yugeommun, who had been waiting outside, surged in all at once.

The Demon Extermination Unit showed no fear at all and faced them with swords drawn. The number of Yugeommun martial artists who had rushed in was greater than expected. Moreover, their momentum was no ordinary thing. The thought naturally arose.

‘Yugeommun has this many experts under its command?’

This was the reason Taejo could remain composed. He was confident that, at the very least, he would not be dragged away from this place.

“Did you think we would tremble in fear just because you are the Demon Extermination Unit?”

They were not the only ones. The two people Taejo trusted most decisively entered last. They were two old men dressed in distinctive clothing.

Jin Hagun recognized them at a glance.

“The Azure-White Twin Swords!”

As their epithet suggested, they wore blue and white martial robes. Their shoes and sword scabbards were also blue for one man and white for the other.

They were notorious experts among the unorthodox factions. If the Demon Extermination Unit were young rising experts, then these were seasoned old masters of the martial world.

The aura of the Azure-White Twin Swords overwhelmed the martial artists of the Demon Extermination Unit. If they joined forces with the experts of Yugeommun, it looked as though it would not be an easy fight.

“I did not expect you two to be in Yugeommun.”

Taejo took Jin Hagun’s words as a reprimand.

“You two? These two seniors roamed the martial world before Unit Leader Jin was even born. Show proper respect.”

It seemed he wanted Jin Hagun to treat them as seniors of the martial world, but Jin Hagun’s reaction was cold. He had not the slightest intention of respecting men of such ill repute.

Of course, there was one exception.

The two old men conversed as if to let everyone hear.

“How many years younger was the Martial Alliance Leader than us?”

“I believe he was three or four years younger.”

“Even from his youth, that man had no ordinary temperament.”

“How could we have known he would become the Martial Alliance Leader like this?”

In truth, they likely had never even met him, but when they mentioned his grandfather in that manner, Jin Hagun’s eyes turned cold.

“He is not someone you may carelessly speak of.”

At that, White Sword smiled and spoke in a gentle tone.

“You are right. He is a man deserving of respect. We had no intention of insulting your grandfather. In the same way, we too do not wish to be insulted. We will handle what happened here properly and inform you, so withdraw now.”

“What discourtesy is this, for someone who can be called the face of the Martial Alliance to come to Yugeommun, which carries on the orthodox lineage of the unorthodox path?”

“If you do this, the Alliance Leader will be put in a difficult position as well.”

“Listen, we are people who no longer have any attachment to life. If you and we fight, who do you think has more to lose?”

The two old men took turns attacking Jin Hagun in a dizzying stream.

There were many words that grated on his nerves one by one, but because they were old experts acknowledged by the unorthodox factions, Jin Hagun could not treat them carelessly.

Of course, one person was an exception.

“Of course you would have more to lose.”

The one who cut in was Geom Mugeuk. Jin Hagun was still far too young to deal with old men of the unorthodox factions.

“How greedy must you be to appear even in this warehouse packed to the brim with desire? You should have elegantly washed your hands in a golden basin and retired, living like an old tale inside a folding screen. What greed made you tear through the screen and come out?”

The two men’s expressions hardened coldly, but Geom Mugeuk continued speaking.

“All the things you’ve gathered over a lifetime must be piled up somewhere. You probably don’t even trust each other enough to say where and how much you’ve hidden, do you? Am I right?”

The faces of the two men flushed bright red.

Blue Sword openly revealed killing intent as he asked,

“Who is that ill-mannered bastard?”

At that, Taejo smiled and said,

“He says he is the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.”

When the Azure-White Twin Swords looked startled, Taejo quickly added,

“It is mad nonsense.”

Only then could the Azure-White Twin Swords understand the situation. Could a normal person possibly mock them in this way?

“That man is someone the Demon Extermination Unit Leader hired as a witness in order to pin crimes on me.”

Taejo quickly sent a sound transmission.

—Please eliminate that man alone.

He was a thorn in Taejo’s eye, and thankfully, he had committed such rudeness. Now there was more than enough justification to kill him.

As the Azure-White Twin Swords stepped forward, the martial artists of the Demon Extermination Unit moved as one and blocked their path.

White Sword said to Jin Hagun,

“As Unit Leader Jin also heard, that man has been rude to us. Leaving aside orthodox, unorthodox, or demonic affiliations, there are laws of the martial world that martial artists must uphold. Even in your eyes, can this truly be overlooked?”

At that, Geom Mugeuk laughed and said,

“Open the way. Let us see whether, as they said earlier, they truly have no attachment to life.”

When the Demon Extermination Unit looked at Jin Hagun, he glanced once at Geom Mugeuk.

Even in this situation, Geom Mugeuk was overflowing with ease. Yes, at first glance, he truly looked like nothing but a madman. But once you came to know him, you understood. You understood just how thoroughly calculated that composure was.

“Open the way.”

At Jin Hagun’s command, the Demon Extermination Unit split left and right, creating a path.

The Azure-White Twin Swords slowly walked along that path. Even as they walked with the Demon Extermination Unit on both sides, they showed no fear at all.

As they walked, they suddenly stopped.

Blue Sword looked down at his hand. His hand was trembling as if in spasms.

White Sword looked at him in surprise.

“What is wrong?”

Blue Sword shook his head. This was the first time his hand had ever begun trembling so suddenly.

This time, Blue Sword was the one shocked. White Sword’s cheek was quivering.

“What about you?”

Not only White Sword’s face, but his whole body began to tremble. Blue Sword was the same. Their legs began to shake. Since entering the martial world, this had never happened to them before.

—Is it poison?

—It is not poison.

—Then what is it?

As the two exchanged sound transmissions, their gazes turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk had been looking at the child inside the box, then slowly raised his head and looked at the two of them.

The moment their eyes met Geom Mugeuk’s, the Azure-White Twin Swords knew.

‘It is because of him.’

It was not the abyssal aura Geom Mugeuk usually displayed.

The energy Geom Mugeuk was now emitting toward the two was killing intent. His clear will to kill them announced that the path the Demon Extermination Unit had opened was a road leading to hell.

“What are you doing? Aren’t you coming?”

The Azure-White Twin Swords swallowed hard.

Taejo, who was watching, found it strange. Since that killing intent was directed only at the Azure-White Twin Swords, Taejo did not know why the two had stopped walking.

“What is the matter, seniors?”

The one who answered Taejo’s question was Geom Mugeuk.

“Your precious seniors are trembling.”

Taejo did not know what he meant, but it was certain that something had gone wrong. This was the moment when the enraged Azure-White Twin Swords should have shouted in fury, yet no shout came.

Geom Mugeuk took one step toward the two of them.

Pressed down by that momentum, the Azure-White Twin Swords retreated at the same time.

“You said you had no more attachment to life, didn’t you?”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze sank terrifyingly. The Azure-White Twin Swords did not dare meet those eyes and lowered their heads.

At that sight, Jin Hagun understood.

‘His skill has grown again in the meantime!’

Geom Mugeuk was completely overwhelming the Azure-White Twin Swords, old experts of the unorthodox factions.

He was showing yet another side different from when they had last met. It felt as though if Jin Hagun chased him by one step, Geom Mugeuk would grow ten steps farther away.

“Great Hero, who are you?”

From White Sword’s mouth came not Young Hero, but Great Hero.

“The man told you earlier, didn’t he?”

Startled out of his wits, White Sword quickly turned to look at Taejo and asked.

“Is this man truly the Demon Cult’s young cult leader?”

Taejo answered in surprise.

“No. What are you talking about……”

At that moment, Taejo flinched and this time looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“Really the Demon Cult’s young cult leader?”

“I told you earlier, didn’t I? I’m the young cult leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

Taejo’s voice trembled on its own.

“Why is the Demon Cult’s young cult leader here?”

“I told you that too, didn’t I? I’m in the middle of cleaning out trash.”

The Blue-White Twin Swords rebuked Taejo.

“Did you push us into the Demon Cult’s young cult leader?”

“I didn’t know either.”

The Blue-White Twin Swords looked at each other and exchanged glances, then soon stepped back.

“It seems there was a misunderstanding, so I hope the young cult leader will understand.”

Putting aside the fact that their opponent’s skill overwhelmed theirs, they knew that if they mishandled the Demon Cult’s young cult leader, they would not die peacefully. Now was not the time to think of pride.

Only now could Taejo understand what Jin Hagun had meant.

The meaning of those words: that he could not refute it, could not insist otherwise, and could not kill to silence the witness.

“I heard it myself. I am the witness.”

Hearing Geom Mugeuk’s declaration ring out resoundingly, he also understood the meaning of the words that everyone but him would believe that statement.

If the Demon Cult’s young cult leader said he had heard it, who would dare refute him? Even the Blue-White Twin Swords, who had stepped forward for him, had tucked their tails and withdrawn.

Geom Mugeuk said to him.

“If you confess obediently, you’ll be dragged to the Murim Alliance. If you hold out to the end, you’ll be dragged to our cult. The choice is yours.”

Taejo’s heart sank. Dragged not to the Murim Alliance, but to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult? At least he could talk to the Murim Alliance, but if he was dragged to the Demon Cult, it would truly be the end. Just looking at the young cult leader was enough to tell. That bastard, who went around with the captain of the Demon-Extermination Unit shouting about cleaning out trash, was a madman. He could not be dragged into a den of madmen.

“You are people belonging to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and the Murim Alliance, while I am merely from a single sect.”

“For someone from merely a single sect, you were frightening us quite a bit earlier.”

“A frightened dog is bound to bark loudly.”

He was desperate enough to say such things. And there was only one way for him to survive.

“I demand that this matter be handled under the mediation of the Sado Alliance.”

The Sado Alliance would never abandon him and the Yugeom Sect. He had made contributions until now. He believed they would surely save him.

“I believe that would be the fair way to handle this. As things stand, I am being terribly wronged. Rumors will spread through the jianghu that we were framed.”

“And if the Sado Alliance also acknowledges your crimes?”

“Then I will acknowledge them cleanly as well.”

Geom Mugeuk, who had been silently gazing at Taejo, readily nodded for reasons unknown.

“Fine. Call them. Let all three of us gather and determine who the true villain is.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 341

Chapter 341: Do You Trust Me? Do You Trust the Young Cult Leader?
-----------------------------------------------------------------
The martial artists of the Demon Extermination Unit took the children out of the boxes and carefully carried them to the carriages waiting outside.

Geomugeuk and Jin Hagun stood side by side, watching.

“What on earth are you thinking?”

“What do you mean?”

“I mean, why did you call even the Sado Alliance?”

With the force he had shown earlier, enough to make even the Blue-White Twin Swords back down, he could have simply taken Tae Jo away. Yet Geomugeuk had deliberately called the Sado Alliance.

“He says he’s been wronged so badly, so I’m trying to handle this fairly.”

“As if you would be so considerate of a villain’s feelings.”

Jin Hagun could sense it. There was definitely another reason Geomugeuk had called the Sado Alliance.

‘Could it be that he wants to see Bi Sain?’

He could only guess as much; he could not know Geomugeuk’s exact intention.

This time, Geomugeuk asked,

“What do you intend to do about the Black Serpent Corps?”

The ones who had kidnapped the children were the Black Serpent Corps. They must have made a fortune from it, and even without the Yugeom Sect, they would surely continue kidnapping children.

“Once we secure evidence through the Yugeom Sect Master this time, we will see that they are dealt with properly.”

Jin Hagun declared his resolve firmly, but it would not be easy.

The Black Serpent Corps was a vast organization rooted all across the Central Plains. With the enormous profits they made by involving themselves in all manner of interests, they had recruited many experts, and they were also known to provide large sums of money to the Sado Alliance.

“It won’t be easy.”

“Even so, can we simply leave those who kidnap children alone?”

Geomugeuk smiled at Jin Hagun’s spirit. Sensing that it was a smile of acknowledgment, Jin Hagun felt pleased inwardly.

There is a thrill in being acknowledged by an enemy. And if that enemy was Geomugeuk?

“Let’s start by changing the name of the Demon Extermination Unit. How about the Evil Extermination Unit or the Wickedness Extermination Unit? Is there really any need to exterminate us when we’ve been so well-behaved lately?”

At Geomugeuk’s banter, Jin Hagun looked dumbfounded. Yet inwardly, he agreed. If the Demon Cult was led by Geomugeuk, then the phrase “demon extermination” did seem unnecessary. Of course, he did not reveal that thought.

“You may be well-behaved lately, but how do I know when you people will stir up trouble again?”

“You don’t know? How much I love peace?”

“I don’t. I only know that wherever you go, blood overflows.”

“That’s because wherever I go, villains are swarming.”

Even as they spoke, Jin Hagun had that thought. If things continued like this, they might truly have to change the name of the Demon Extermination Unit. Certainly, the Demon Cult of today was different from before.

While the martial artists of the Demon Extermination Unit escorted the children away, only three or four of them remained beside Jin Hagun.

On the other hand, the experts of the Yugeom Sect were still numerous. An invisible battle of nerves unfolded between them and the martial artists of the Demon Extermination Unit, but despite being outnumbered, the latter showed no sign of fear at all. It was clear how well trained they were.

Tae Jo walked over to the two of them.

“I have sent an urgent message to the Sado Alliance, so someone from the alliance will arrive as quickly as possible.”

Unlike when he had been pleading earlier, Tae Jo now seemed at ease.

Jin Hagun said to him,

“Until then, Sect Master, I think you will have to stay with us.”

“I wouldn’t leave even if you pushed me out the door and told me to run.”

His attitude had changed from when he had been begging them to please call the Sado Alliance.

“It will take quite a while for someone to come from the Sado Alliance, so I will host you comfortably in the meantime. Please come inside and have a drink first.”

He was certain that once someone from the Sado Alliance arrived, this matter would be resolved. Since he had explained the situation here, at the very least, someone of division leader rank or higher would come.

The Sado Alliance would absolutely not allow him to be dragged away by them.

“It’s stifling. Let’s get out of here first.”

Geomugeuk walked out of the warehouse.

The Yugeom Sect martial artists gathered near the entrance outside split to the left and right, making a path.

Among them were the Blue-White Twin Swords. They were the ones who had rebuked Tae Jo for making them step forward without even knowing the other party was the young cult leader of the Demon Cult. It seemed they might have stormed off somewhere in anger, but they had remained near Tae Jo.

Whether it was for money or some other reason, Geomugeuk guessed that they were deeply connected to the Yugeom Sect.

The Blue-White Twin Swords still could not believe that Geomugeuk had overwhelmed them with his aura alone. Had they perhaps fallen victim to some trick? Or had they been mistaken for a moment?

As he walked out, Geomugeuk glanced at the two of them. The moment their eyes met, all the doubts of the Blue-White Twin Swords vanished. Their instincts for danger scolded them. For experts like you, wasn’t it enough to have felt it once?

Jin Hagun followed behind him and came outside. In the meantime, the carriages carrying the children departed, escorted by some of the Demon Extermination Unit’s martial artists.

Tae Jo casually asked Geomugeuk,

“Why has the young cult leader not called his demonic practitioners?”

“That is because of you, Sect Master Tae.”

“Because of me?”

“The fewer people there are, the more options you have, don’t you think?”

“Options?”

“The option of killing us.”

“No matter how bold I may be, how could I dare do such a thing?”

Tae Jo was inwardly startled. The question of why the demonic practitioners had not come had contained, if only slightly, the very intent Geomugeuk had mentioned. One had to prepare for even the slightest possibility.

“It seems like just the opportunity to me. Many of the Demon Extermination Unit’s men have left their posts to escort the children, too. What do you say? You could kill us all, then insist that the young cult leader of the Demon Cult and the leader of the Demon Extermination Unit fought each other and both sides suffered grievously. You seem to have a talent for shamelessly forcing the issue.”

Had the Blue-White Twin Swords not been suppressed by Geomugeuk’s aura, perhaps that might have become the final option.

“How could I dare do that? Now then, the weather is cold, so please come inside.”

Jin Hagun said,

“We will stay here.”

“It is cold. Please come in.”

“We’re fine.”

The Demon Extermination Unit set up tents in the Yugeom Sect’s training ground and made lodgings there.

They had no desire to be entertained by members of the unorthodox faction, and it was safer to remain in an open place like this, with the surroundings clear, than to enter the enemy’s interior.

“Then, young cult leader, please come inside.”

“I will stay here as well.”

Jin Hagun nodded to his subordinate and ordered,

“Prepare a place for the young cult leader to rest as well.”

The martial artists of the Demon Extermination Unit lit a campfire in the training ground.

Though he had given the order himself, Jin Hagun found the scene utterly unfamiliar. Camping with a campfire in the training ground of an unorthodox sect? He truly felt that ever since meeting Geomugeuk, he had been experiencing all sorts of things.

The more one came to know a man named Geomugeuk, the more astonishing one’s experiences became. Perhaps that was why. In a situation like this, where in the past his nerves would have been so on edge that he could not sleep a wink, his heart felt at ease.

And through this night’s encampment, Jin Hagun learned one thing for certain.

Geomugeuk did not waste even a moment of time. Judging from his usual behavior, Jin Hagun had expected him to loiter around and tease the Yugeom Sect martial artists, or come over to pester him to drink together. But Geomugeuk shut himself inside his tent and quietly immersed himself in cultivation, circulating his qi and regulating his breath.

His composure and confidence must come from this effort. This man was truly not simple.

* * *

At last, news reached Tae Jo that someone had arrived from the Sado Alliance.

“Who came?”

His close aide, Man Hong, reported with a startled face.

“The young alliance leader has come.”

“What?”

Tae Jo was shocked. He had requested that they send someone of high status, but he had not imagined the young alliance leader would come in person.

“Why has the young alliance leader come?”

“It seems he heard that the heirs are here and came personally.”

“But why?”

For him, who did not know the relationship among the three of them, it was a natural question.

Originally, if a division leader-level figure had come, he had intended to escape this crisis even if he had to shower him with bribes.

‘Is he going to abandon me?’

An inexplicable suspicion bloomed in Tae Jo’s heart.

“Let us go out for now.”

Tae Jo hurried outside together with Man Hong.

A group of martial artists entered the Yugeom Sect.

The one at the front was Bi Sain, the heir of the Sado Alliance, and those who followed him were the Thirteen Wolves of the Sado Alliance.

The scars on Bi Sain’s face—cut, crushed, and distorted—were terrifying enough to be difficult to look at directly. His aura, too, was dark and cold. He looked unchanged. Only his eyes were different from when he had last met Geomugeuk. They were far more mature than before.

“We greet the young alliance leader!”

The Yugeom Sect martial artists all prostrated themselves at once and paid their respects. The Blue-White Twin Swords were no exception. To martial artists of the unorthodox faction, the heir of the Sado Alliance possessed absolute authority.

Tae Jo also prostrated himself and welcomed him.

“It is truly an honor to meet you, young alliance leader.”

“It is good to meet you. Rise, all of you.”

Tae Jo and all the Yugeom Sect martial artists rose to their feet.

“I do not know whether you remember me, but I met you at the Sado Alliance when you became the heir.”

“I remember.”

“I am deeply honored that someone as esteemed as you has come personally.”

Tae Jo bowed deeply once more. Though an unexpected person had come, in any case, his life and death now depended on this Bi Sain.

Bi Sain’s gaze turned toward Geomugeuk.

Geomugeuk was crouching in the yard, warming himself by the campfire.

He had come knowing that Geomugeuk was here. If not for him, he would not have come personally. Even though he had known, the moment he saw Geomugeuk, he could not stop his heart from surging.

“It’s cold. Come warm yourself by the fire.”

Geomugeuk waved his hand and smiled brightly.

Tae Jo, who had no way of knowing their relationship, raised his voice.

“This is the young alliance leader of the Sado Alliance. Show proper respect!”

Geomugeuk looked at Bi Sain and said,

“Your young alliance leader isn’t the only precious one here. We’re precious people too.”

Seeing his eyes sparkle with the desire to play a prank, Bi Sain felt that he had indeed met Geomugeuk again.

Geomugeuk sprang to his feet and offered an exaggerated salute.

“It is an honor to meet you, young alliance leader!”

At the prank of him bending deeply at the waist, Tae Jo fell into a great misunderstanding.

‘Good! He’s frightened! The young alliance leader’s foul face worked.’

He never imagined that the two of them were exchanging sound transmissions like this.

—Are you eating a balanced diet? Not being picky?

His first sound transmission was worrying about picky eating? Truly, one day he would push this man off a cliff.

—Seeing that your tiresome nagging is exactly the same, you must not be a fake young cult leader.

—You’ve become more impressive than before.

—What are you trying to ask of me now, laying the groundwork like this?

—You and Leader Jin both know me far too well now!

Just then, Jin Hagun walked out of the tent. Of course, Bi Sain knew Jin Hagun was here as well. But he had not expected them to have pitched tents like this.

Bi Sain and Jin Hagun silently exchanged fist-and-palm salutes. They were not close enough to treat each other as comfortably as they did Geomugeuk. Of course, with their eyes, they did exchange a conversation like this.

—Have you been swept up by this man again and ended up like this?

—You know it too, don’t you? That avoiding him does not mean he can be avoided.

—True. If it did, I would not be here either.

After thus exchanging greetings with their eyes, Bi Sain walked over to the campfire.

Then he took a seat in front of it.

“Sect Master, you come here as well.”

“Yes.”

Feeling bewildered, Tae Jo headed there. Why the campfire, of all places? He had a bad feeling.

And so they all sat around the campfire. Behind Bi Sain stood the Thirteen Wolves of the Sado Alliance, and behind Jin Hagun stood the Demon Extermination Unit. A taut tension flowed between them. Regardless of the relationship between their leaders, they were like mortal enemies.

“What happened?”

At Bi Sain’s question, Tae Jo explained the situation. Of course, it was an explanation favorable to himself.

“My subordinate made a mistake while seeking out children with martial talent. He failed to confirm that the Black Serpent Corps had handed over kidnapped children. I knew nothing of it. Yet they are trying to pin that crime on me and drag me away.”

Bi Sain looked at Geomugeuk.

“That is a lie. I personally heard the Yugeom Sect Master speaking with his subordinate. That man knew the children had been kidnapped.”

Tae Jo flatly denied it.

“That is a lie. I knew nothing of it. This is a conspiracy by the orthodox faction and the Demon Cult to eliminate our sect.”

He tried by any means to expand this issue into a conflict between the orthodox and unorthodox factions.

“Young alliance leader, do you believe me? Or do you believe the young cult leader of the Demon Cult?”

“Of course I believe our sect master.”

But Bi Sain’s gaze turned toward Geomugeuk. He believed Geomugeuk.

—What exactly do you want?

If Geomugeuk had called him, there was surely a reason.

Then, instead of answering, Geom Mugeuk asked Taejo a question he had not asked until now.

“Why did you buy children with talent for martial arts?”

“I intended to raise them as elites of the main sect from a young age.”

“Then let’s see the children you bought before.”

At that moment, Taejo flinched.

“This is the first time I have bought a child.”

Geom Mugeuk fixed his gaze on him and spoke.

“It isn’t your first time, is it?”

“It is the first time. Why do you think otherwise?”

“Because I heard the sect leader conversing with his subordinates.”

Their conversation had not been that of people buying a child for the first time. It was undoubtedly a frequent occurrence.

“Where are the children you bought previously?”

“Did I not say this was the first time?”

At that, truly unexpected words spilled from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“If we confront the Heuksadanju, the truth will be revealed.”

At the mention of summoning the Heuksadanju, Taejo was startled. Bisain and Jin Hagun were also surprised.

“The Heuksadanju will come here?”

Even Jin Hagun, who had asked the question, did not think he would come.

“If we call him, he will never come. However, did not someone capable of summoning him just arrive?”

Everyone’s gaze turned toward Bisain.

“If the Young Leader of the Sado Maeng commands his presence, could he truly ignore it?”

Only now did Jin Hagun understand.

Why Geom Mugeuk had brought Bisain here. It was to make her summon the Heuksadanju directly.

By summoning him directly, he intended to prevent the kidnapping of children. He was trying to handle both the Yu Geommun matter and the Heuksadan affair at once.

Because if he could persuade just one person, he could stop both the sellers and the buyers.

‘This person, truly…’

It occurred to him that this was someone who thought of things that never crossed his own mind.

Meanwhile, Bisain’s expression grew grave.

—Do you know how difficult a position that puts me in?

—Isn’t it an even more serious matter that your people are kidnapping and selling children?

There was no trace of playfulness left in Geom Mugeuk’s expression or voice.

Bisain remembered. That look in his eyes was the same as when he had asked that question in the past.

What is your path?

Now he was waiting for the answer. Was her path one that permitted the kidnapping and selling of children?

As the atmosphere shifted to summoning even the Heuksadanju, Taejo grew desperate. If the Heuksadanju came and revealed that he had sold children on numerous occasions, his guilt would be established. And there was a matter even graver than that.

Taejo sent a desperate voice transmission to Bisain.

—It absolutely cannot happen.

To Bisain’s questioning gaze, an unexpected voice transmission followed.

—Those children were being sent to the Sado Maeng.

Absolute Regression — Chapter 342

Chapter 342: Such a long way to say cool.
-----------------------------------------
The element of surprise wields its power when it is least expected.

And so it was with Bisain’s surprise now.

—What do you mean?

His voice transmission trembled as his eyes flew wide open.

—It is exactly as I said. The children purchased from the Black Snake Guild were being sent to the Sacred Path Alliance. I am merely a middleman.

Taejo had truly wished to keep even this secret hidden. But with the atmosphere turning toward summoning the Black Snake Guild Leader itself, he could not simply stand by. Being dragged off to the Martial Alliance or the Demonic Cult to pay for his crimes was something he could not allow.

—I merely handled the work as I was told.

—Who ordered it?

—I cannot say.

To Bisain’s knowledge, there was no organization within the Sacred Path Alliance that purchased children. Which meant someone had been carrying out this affair in secret.

—Tell me who it is.

Even though Bisain’s tone had taken on an icy edge, Taejo did not speak the name.

—If you do not speak, you may very well die by my hand.

Though his life was threatened, intimidation did not work on Taejo.

—Even so, I cannot tell you.

Taejo was certain. Bisain could not kill him over this matter. After all, buying and sending the children had ultimately been for the sake of the Sacred Path Alliance. You cannot kill me.

—Can you take responsibility for what you just said?

—With my life hanging by a thread, why would I dare tell a lie? First, you must send those two back.

Even to Taejo’s voice transmission, Bisain offered no reply.

—Young Alliance Leader, if this becomes known outside, it will become troublesome.

Bisain fell into deep agony.

As the two men’s telepathic conversation deepened, Geommugeuk deduced that the children were being sent to the Sacred Path Alliance. In this situation, that was the only thing that could make Bisain agonize so.

Geommugeuk offered no advice. This was the moment when a martial artist named Bisain would decide his own fate. Depending on that choice, not only Bisain’s personal fate but the fate of the Sacred Path Alliance, and indeed the fate of the murim itself, would be affected.

Bisain, please make the right choice!

After agonizing for a long while, Bisain finally opened his mouth.

“Just now, Sect Leader Tae confessed of his own accord. He said he knew that children were being purchased regularly.”

Taejo was horrified. He had never expected Bisain to say such a thing. And this unexpected behavior was only the beginning.

“Young Alliance Leader! Why are you doing this? Did you not understand what I told you?”

“I understood. You sent those children to the Sacred Path Alliance, did you not?”

For a moment, silence fell over the room.

Taejo was aghast. He had never expected him to speak of such an important matter in front of everyone. It was a gathering where even the Demon Extermination Squad and the Sacred Path Thirteen Wolves were present.

The words *Are you crazy?* rose to his throat. He had very nearly blurted them out.

Geommugeuk had already guessed the content, but Jin Hagun had not expected it. What was surprising was not that the children had gone to the Sacred Path Alliance, but the fact that Bisain had revealed it. The Sacred Path Alliance he knew was an organization always desperate to cover up and hide incidents. Its very existence was that of a villain.

He had actually expected the secret voice transmission to go something like this:

—They are making a mountain out of a molehill over a few measly kidnapped children.

Would Bisain’s thoughts have been much different? He would likely be conflicted only because of his relationship with Geommugeuk. Yet Bisain’s current response completely surpassed his expectations. That he would admit it so readily? This was not something even he himself could do easily.

Bisain stated the reason he had revealed the truth.

“To hide one secret, ten lies are needed. I have no intention of keeping this matter buried in secrecy.”

He saw Geommugeuk smiling at him. Seeing that smile, he felt that his choice had not been wrong.

Yes, perhaps he was being swayed by that Geommugeuk. Yet his heart answered the worry in his head thus:

Even so, it is fine.

Geommugeuk was in a good mood. Though time would pass and today’s matter would become but a moment in the past, this choice would greatly influence Bisain’s life going forward. Well done, Bisain. Only by living this way can you establish your own sacred path.

Jin Hagun sent a voice transmission to Geommugeuk.

—Young Cult Leader, did you know things would turn out this way?

—I did not know the children were being sold to the Sacred Path Alliance. However, I never believed the Young Alliance Leader would overlook this matter.

Jin Hagun looked at Geommugeuk. Geommugeuk’s deep gaze was fixed on Bisain.

—We may now be witnessing the birth of a magnificent next-generation Sacred Path Alliance Leader who will be remembered in the history of the murim.

And Bisain was handling this situation wisely.

Bisain asked Taejo.

“When the Main Alliance desired children, did it order you to kidnap and send them?”

“It did not.”

“Then it is not the Main Alliance’s fault. Why did you entrust the matter to the Black Snake Guild?”

Taejo could not answer at all.

The one who saw straight through his inner thoughts was Geommugeuk.

“It must be because it costs less money. In this affair, finding talented children is not the difficult part. Persuading their families is difficult. And after spending so much time persuading them, you must also provide them fitting compensation. But if you join hands with the Black Snake Guild, matters become too simple. You merely kidnap them, and that is the end of it. Could you not earn a great deal of money sitting idle, doing nothing?”

Taejo could not refute a single word. Geommugeuk had spoken as if he had entered Taejo’s very heart.

Bisain did not miss this opportunity.

“You have tarnished the Main Alliance’s honor for the sake of personal greed.”

He steered the matter so that it was not the Sacred Path Alliance’s fault. He limited the blame to the wrongdoing of Taejo and the Black Snake Guild.

“Young Alliance Leader, I cannot understand why you are doing this.”

Bisain acknowledged his words.

Yes, had he not met Geommugeuk, a matter of this degree might have passed as nothing. He would have lived a completely different life. But meeting Geommugeuk had greatly influenced him, and decisively, at the moment Geommugeuk asked what his sacred path was, his own life had changed as well.

“Until now, this may have been something taken for granted, but from this moment on, it is not.”

Bisain gave an order to the Sacred Path Thirteen Wolves.

“Send a letter to the Black Snake Guild Leader immediately and arrange a meeting.”

He showed the will to resolve the child kidnapping issue definitively.

“Convey that if he does not appear at the appointed place, the name Black Snake Guild will disappear from the murim.”

A martial artist under the Sacred Path Thirteen Wolves ran out to send the letter.

Taejo realized that he had not known at all what kind of person the Sacred Path Alliance’s Young Alliance Leader was.

*Was the Young Alliance Leader this kind of person?*

With a trembling heart, Taejo asked.

“Then what becomes of me?”

Bisain pressured him with a calm tone.

“Sect Leader Tae. I am not so magnanimous a person. Nor am I broad-minded enough to overlook those who challenge my authority.”

Bisain glanced at Geommugeuk. The quick-witted Geommugeuk understood from that gaze alone what he had to do.

“We will take Sect Leader Tae.”

At Geommugeuk’s words, Taejo’s heart beat madly. He wondered if they would truly send him to the Demonic Cult, but Bisain gave a slight nod. Before Bisain’s permission could even be granted, Taejo cried out first.

“It cannot be. The Demonic Cult is absolutely unacceptable.”

Geommugeuk put on a kind smile.

“We will treat you well.”

He knew that could not possibly be true. If dragged away, his dantian would be destroyed, and after being tortured, he would be imprisoned in the inner dungeon. Death would be preferable.

“Young Alliance Leader, I beg you.”

He pleaded, but Bisain remained cold. What was needed now was to hear from him about the mastermind behind this affair.

“If you must send me away, please let me go to the Martial Alliance.”

Jin Hagun also joined their combined ploy.

“I refuse.”

“Why on earth do you refuse? Please take me!”

“I refuse. Your crimes are too severe for that.”

In other words, he should be dragged to the Demonic Cult and suffer more terribly.

Geommugeuk spoke with a deliberately aggrieved expression.

“You are all too much. Anyone watching would think our cult is a den of monsters. Our cult’s inner dungeon is also a place where people live. One can grow accustomed to it.”

This was even more frightening than a threat. In the end, Taejo could endure no longer. He could accept anything else, but being dragged to the Demonic Cult was absolutely unacceptable.

—Commander Pyo orchestrated this matter.

An unexpected name came out.

—Commander Pyo? Which Commander Pyo?

There was no one surnamed Pyo among the current commanders. Then he remembered someone.

—Could it be? The former Black Dragon Corps Leader?

—That is correct.

Pyo Gigung.

He, who had been the corps leader of the Sacred Path Alliance’s Black Dragon Corps, had stepped down from his position over ten years ago after suffering severe injuries. He had heard that afterward, the man lived like a cripple, scraping by drinking at banquet halls. He was someone who had faded from memory.

—Why would he…?

—Several years ago, he came to me. He said he was secretly creating a secret organization within the Sacred Path Alliance. That was why he needed children of outstanding martial talent. Because it was top secret, he firmly threatened me with death if this was leaked.

—He is someone who stepped down from his position. You believed his words?

—He said that the higher-ups had entrusted him with this matter. He said the fate of the Sacred Path Alliance rested on the secret organization he was creating. He gave me a large sum of money as an advance. It was money that Commander Pyo could not have gathered alone. He also said the Alliance would secure him various profit rights afterward.

—Were the promises kept?

—They were.

Thanks to that, Yugungmun had been able to expand its influence, and even host Cheongbaek Igeom as a guest.

Bisain could see it now. This was not a simple child kidnapping incident. Something was happening inside the Sacred Path Alliance—something he, the Young Alliance Leader, did not know about.

“We will take charge of this man’s punishment.”

Geommugeuk and Jin Hagun readily accepted Bisain’s wish.

“Escort Sect Leader Tae to the Main Alliance.”

At Bisain’s command, the Sacred Path Thirteen Wolves suppressed Taejo’s acupoints and led him out. Taejo dared not resist. As he was dragged away, he looked at Bisain with fervent eyes. At least he was grateful not to have been sent to the Demonic Cult.

—If you wish to live, assist me from now on.

He had secured Taejo because his help would be needed to uncover this affair.

Bisain spoke to the Sacred Path Thirteen Wolves.

“We will go straight to meet the Black Snake Guild Leader.”

Geommugeuk and Jin Hagun naturally moved with him. Geommugeuk had something to say to Bisain, and Jin Hagun wanted to see this matter through to its end.

Geommugeuk, Bisain, and Jin Hagun rode together in one carriage. The Sacred Path Thirteen Wolves and the Demon Extermination Squad rode horses and escorted the carriage.

The martial artists of Yugungmun, including Cheongbaek Igeom, could only watch as the storm that had swept away their sect leader receded.

Cheongbaek Igeom finally relaxed and spoke.

“Jeong Sama, this is the first time I have seen three successors traveling together.”

“It is probably the first time in the history of the murim.”

“Will the sect leader return alive?”

That was something even Cheongbaek Igeom did not know. However, one thing was certain. They had survived until now because they had strictly followed one principle. When a storm rages, lock the gates well and refrain from going out.

The two men turned back inside and said.

“Close the door.”

* * *

Wind entered through the carriage window.

Bisain silently watched the wind flutter Geommugeuk’s sleeve.

Even this morning, he had never dreamed that he would ride in a carriage with them to meet the Black Snake Guild Leader. He had even less expected that he himself would be the one to issue a warning to the Black Snake Guild. Not knowing whether this matter would bring calamity or fortune to him, who would one day become the Sacred Path Alliance Leader, he felt inwardly anxious.

Just then, Geommugeuk spoke to Bisain.

“I witnessed your sacred path.”

Yes, this was why. The reason he had made such a decision. To show that his sacred path was one that protected children, at the very least.

“How was my sacred path?”

“It was magnificent.”

“You are so good at sweet lies that I cannot believe you.”

It seemed he wanted to hear that it was magnificent even more.

Geommugeuk smiled and said.

“It was truly magnificent.”

Jin Hagun, who was beside them, was drawn in as well.

“What did you think? Wasn’t the Young Alliance Leader truly magnificent?”

“I am not someone who speaks such words to a person’s face as easily as you.”

Jin Hagun turned his head and looked out the window.

“But I do think today’s decision and going to meet the Black Snake Guild Leader are the right things to do.”

Geommugeuk smiled and said.

“You are stretching out the word magnificent quite long.”

* * *

That night, only Geommugeuk, Bisain, and Jin Hagun stood side by side on a plain where clusters of stars spilled across the sky.

The Sacred Path Thirteen Wolves and the Demon Extermination Squad had been left at the campsite; only the three men were present.

Now was the time to inform Bisain.

“A certain force is scheming to seize control of the murim.”

“!”

Even more astonishing words followed from Geommugeuk’s mouth.

“They have infiltrated our cult, and the Martial Alliance as well. They were even in the foreign Pungcheon Cult.”

Bisain was startled. From Jin Hagun’s serious expression, it was no joke.

“In our cult, they targeted the Soul-Devouring Demon Lord; in the Martial Alliance, they targeted Lord Jin here. In the Pungcheon Cult, they targeted the young new cult leader.”

“Surely not?”

“Indeed. I believe that in the Sacred Path Alliance as well, there are agents they have sent hiding in secret.”

It was hard to believe. Had these words not come from Geommugeuk, he would not have believed them.

“I do not wish to fall to such bastards even if I die. They are ones who sneak in secretly, find our weaknesses, and aim for our backs. The moment we doubt one another, we will be defeated one by one. At least in dealing with these ones, let us combine our strength. Let us show them that the gangho is not something to be handed over to bastards like them. For the sake of the murim we will lead in the future as well.”

After a brief pause, Bisain suddenly asked.

“Is that truly the only reason? You are not the one behind that dark curtain, are you?”

“If you ask so openly, I might as well answer that I am the mastermind.”

“If that is the case, we are ruined anyway. Then tell me in advance. From today on, I will eat only what I want and be picky.”

Geommugeuk felt that Bisain had certainly grown. Being able to joke so comfortably meant that much composure had taken root in his heart.

“There is another reason.”

Geommugeuk first looked up at the night sky, and the gazes of the other two men also wandered among the twinkling stars. As they gazed, wondering what their respective stars might be, Geommugeuk calmly conveyed his true feelings.

“Because though we are enemies, we are also friends.”

Absolute Regression

Chapter 343

Chapter 343: Today is Situ Shiwulang.
-------------------------------------
The gazes that had been fixed on the stars gathered in one place.

Bisain and Jin Hagun looked at Geom Muguk. He was still looking up at the night sky with those clear, deep eyes.

The word *friend* sounded so foreign. Was the word this man had spoken truly the same one they knew?

“Since when were we friends?”

Bisain asked bluntly.

Jin Hagun also waited for Geom Muguk’s answer with the same thought in mind.

“Did you think I was the only one who considered us friends?”

“Do you truly believe we can become friends?”

“I believe we can.”

After a brief pause, Bisain asked again.

“What if one of us, believing we are friends, ends up betraying the other?”

Even childhood friends changed, betrayed each other, and went through chaos. And you’re saying the three heirs of the orthodox, sacred, and demonic factions would become friends? That betrayal would be one involving the lives of thousands or tens of thousands.

“Then let us think of it this way. Ah, right—we were enemies, weren’t we? Let us not feel slighted. Let us not even think of it as betrayal.”

“By then, your sword would already be piercing my heart. Would I even have the leisure to think such thoughts?”

Though he wore a fearsome expression and spoke frightening words, Bisain’s gaze was softer than ever. He was imagining a life spent as friends with Geom Muguk.

He pictured himself as the Sacred Path Alliance Leader, Geom Muguk as the Heavenly Demon, and Jin Hagun as the Martial Alliance Leader, meeting one another and laughing together. He pictured himself, white-haired with age, still dumbfounded by Geom Muguk’s unchanging banter.

But just as ideals differ from reality, the words that left his mouth were different from his heart.

“If rumors spread that I’ve become friends with the Young Cult Lord of the Demonic Cult, I’ll be driven from the position of Young Alliance Leader.”

At that, Jin Hagun also entered the conversation.

“How much worse would it be for me? Becoming friends with the heir of the Demonic Cult and the heir of the Sacred Path Alliance? Far from becoming a successor, I might be beaten to death by my grandfather.”

Geom Muguk looked at Jin Hagun.

“Now that I think of it, Young Master Jin hasn’t become the successor yet. Let us help make you the successor.”

Jin Hagun then spoke with a stern expression.

“It is not as if I can ascend to the seat of Alliance Leader just because you help me. Only when I possess the qualifications and the capacity worthy of the position can I finally rise to it.”

Pride could be felt in his words—the pride of a righteous faction heir, that such a successor could not be decided so easily.

“As expected, you draw a line between us.”

Geom Muguk squeezed in close to Bisain’s side, acting friendly.

But Bisain stepped away to avoid him.

“You’re too cold!”

Now the three stood facing one another, as if drawing a circle.

Jin Hagun asked Geom Muguk.

“What kind of martial world do you dream of?”

It was a question Bisain had wanted to ask as well.

Geom Muguk’s gaze turned once more toward the stars in the night sky.

“The martial world I dream of is one where your orthodox path, your sacred path, and my demonic path harmonize together. I do not believe fate gave us these statuses, and even bestowed upon us heaven-shaking martial arts, so that we could dream of unifying the martial world by sacrificing countless people.”

Trust in one another would likely only be possible in a relationship built on the conviction that none of them dreamed of unification.

“I was not close with my father, nor was I close with my elder brother. I lived without comprehending the hearts of guards who would lay down their lives for me. That is why I always ask myself: what do you intend to do by unifying the martial world, when you cannot even properly look after those around you?”

Geom Muguk’s gaze, which had been on the stars, turned back toward the two of them.

“I believe that becoming friends with you is more difficult than unifying the martial world.”

He spoke as if joking, but his words were filled with sincerity.

“For martial artists, the easiest thing is to fight and kill one another. So let us not live such easy lives. Let us take on a harder challenge. Let us become true friends.”

Neither Bisain nor Jin Hagun had ever thought this before. Geom Muguk was right. Becoming true friends was a hundred times harder than fighting.

“There are too many evils in this world that must be slain. There are absolute evils that cannot be killed unless we do it. See, is there not even evil that seeks to devour us? Let us live while striking them down. Let us gather to drink together, let us mock our subordinates together. Let us travel together. That is the martial world I wish for.”

Could it really be possible?

Bisain blurted out without realizing.

“It sounds like a dream.”

“Then let us dream for the rest of our lives.”

It was precisely as Bisain and Jin Hagun were lost in their own thoughts that—

A shooting star fell, tearing across the night sky.

Geom Muguk clapped his hands together and quickly made a wish.

“Please let me unify the martial world!”

Jin Hagun shook his head in dumbfounded disbelief, while Bisain smiled faintly.

And for a while longer, they stood there beneath the pouring stars.

* * *

The leader of Heuksadan, Chu Goeng, looked out the window with a hardened expression.

“Now they’re openly keeping watch.”

Across from the building where he was, two martial artists could be seen staring this way.

They were experts from the Martial Alliance’s Demon Extermination Corps. They had begun pressuring them several months prior, and now they were outright surveilling them.

Chu Goeng was a man who defied the stereotype that a large physique and quick temper meant ignorance. He was perceptive and adept at scheming.

In business, his ruthless and cruel nature drove him to eliminate competitors by any means necessary.

Yet even he could do nothing but watch the surveillants across the street. Since they were the Demon Extermination Corps—a thorn in his side—he could not remove them as he pleased.

“What of Black Thirteen?”

“There is still no word.”

The one who answered the Heuksadan leader’s question was his right-hand man, Song Chung.

All word from Black Thirteen—who had gone to deliver the kidnapped children to the Yellow Dragon Escort Agency—had been cut off.

“I believe he ran afoul of those Demon Extermination Corps bastards.”

The Heuksadan leader furrowed his brow. The very name of the Demon Extermination Corps made his teeth chatter.

“I suppose we’ll have to ask the Sacred Path Alliance for help.”

It was not an easy decision. The Sacred Path Alliance never helped without a price. If he received help for this matter, he would have to provide them with a massive sum under the guise of sponsorship. And there was already so much money flowing into the Sacred Path Alliance.

Just then, another subordinate came, delivered a letter, and left.

“This is a missive from the Sacred Path Alliance.”

The letter had arrived right as they were speaking of the Sacred Path Alliance, surprising the Heuksadan leader and Song Chung.

Having read its contents, the Heuksadan leader crumpled the paper in irritation and threw it at Song Chung.

“Impudent bastard!”

Song Chung unfolded the crumpled letter and read it.

Surprisingly, it was a summons from the Young Alliance Leader of the Sacred Path Alliance, stating that if he did not come, they would erase Heuksadan from the martial world.

Song Chung began by soothing his enraged leader.

“It seems the brat has become a successor and grown arrogant.”

Killing intent erupted from the Heuksadan leader’s body. Normally, when he was this furious, corpses would pile up. But his opponent being who it was, his anger could only continue to simmer.

“But why does the Young Alliance Leader suddenly want to see me?”

The missive contained only those words, the time, and the place. The purpose was not written. That was why the Heuksadan leader was even angrier.

“It isn’t an attempt to assert discipline?”

“We’re not even directly under the Sacred Path Alliance; what discipline?”

“Since when have those Sacred Path Alliance bastards cared about such things?”

Unlike the offended Heuksadan leader, Song Chung saw this contact as an opportunity.

“If you invest in the Young Alliance Leader through this opportunity, it will be much easier to conduct business in the Central Plains going forward. And when you meet him, subtly bring up the matter of the Demon Extermination Corps.”

A bad mood was a bad mood, but this was obviously what had to be done. Business was business.

“This year’s harvest was for nothing. In the end, I’m going to get squeezed no matter what. How much will it take?”

There was something he believed as an immutable truth: money was the surest way to win someone over. If it failed, it was because there was not enough money.
