“Since I am not a man lacking money, why not give something else?”

“What?”

“The Sun-Moon Sword.”

“Are you mad?”

He had every reason to flare up. The Sun-Moon Sword was the treasured blade he cherished most among his possessions.

“How often does an opportunity to meet the Young Alliance Leader present itself? Let us make him our man through this opportunity.”

“What if he fails to take the seat? We won’t know until the day he actually rises to it, will we?”

“Isn’t that precisely why investment is difficult?”

After a brief deliberation, the Heuksadan leader made his decision.

“Fine. Let us hand over the Sun-Moon Sword.”

The Demon Extermination Corps issue was a factor too. It was regrettable, but the opponent was worth that much. He would give it, and find a way to recoup everything.

“In exchange, I’m taking everyone from Black One to Black Ten. Call Black Night and Black Hound as well.”

Song Chung flinched in surprise.

“That is excessive.”

“It has to be excessive.”

“You know Black Hound’s temperament. There is no telling what trouble he’ll cause. He is the type to go berserk even in front of the Young Alliance Leader of the Sacred Path Alliance.”

“That is exactly why I am bringing him.”

The Heuksadan leader would not yield on this point.

“Even when giving generously, we must not look like pushovers. They must feel the tension of how dangerous a presence we are. Only then does the value of our gift increase.”

Knowing that once he made a decision he never changed his mind, Song Chung accepted his will.

“Understood. I shall carry out your orders. Shall we depart now?”

The Heuksadan leader rose from his seat.

“We have to go. If we don’t, they’ll erase us from the martial world.”

* * *

When they had almost arrived at the appointed place, Geom Muguk had the carriage stopped.

And he spoke to Jin Hagun.

“For today’s protagonist, let us change our clothes.”

“What do you mean?”

“Isn’t today’s occasion one where the Young Alliance Leader of the Sacred Path Alliance imparts teachings to the unorthodox sects? It is not a place for the Demon Extermination Corps. Have the Corps wait, and let us change into the uniforms of the Sacred Path Thirteen Wolves.”

Jin Hagun had planned to go with Bisain and pressure them together. He had thought that if the Young Alliance Leader of the Sacred Path Alliance and the Demon Extermination Corps Commander of the Martial Alliance warned them together, the effect would be doubled.

However, Geom Muguk was trying to elevate Bisain’s authority from Bisain’s position.

“Very well, let us do it that way.”

Jin Hagun readily accepted.

Bisain felt Geom Muguk’s consideration for him, but pretended not to notice and said nothing.

“Just give us two sets of clothes.”

Then the Sacred Path Thirteen Wolves provided the two with spare uniforms.

As they changed, Geom Muguk spoke.

“Today, we shall become not the Sacred Path Thirteen Wolves, but the Sacred Path Fifteen Wolves. Fourteenth Wolf, Fifteenth Wolf, receive your new recruits!”

In truth, Jin Hagun needed considerable courage to wear this uniform. While he himself thought nothing of it, if someone took issue with it, he could face severe criticism.

But watching Geom Muguk change while chattering away—complaining that his legs were too long and the pants too short, that the color did not suit him, and spouting all manner of nonsense—Jin Hagun could not help but reflect on himself.

This was nothing. What truly mattered was who wore the clothes. Looking back, would people remember a petty man who worried over nothing, or a great man who willingly donned another’s attire out of consideration for them?

Having finished dressing, Geom Muguk did not forget a final quip.

“Will you be the Fourteenth Wolf, or the Fifteenth Wolf?”

* * *

The Heuksadan leader had arrived first and was waiting.

The elites of Heuksadan stood in lines around him.

In the martial world, Heuksadan was seen as a pack of strays. An organization that swarmed every source of profit to tear off a piece, that overwhelmed with numbers, that crawled back out no matter how many you killed.

But that was only half of what Heuksadan was.

At the very least, the Heuksadan leader was no dog, but a tiger. A clever tiger that knew pretending to be a dog was how one survived for long.

From Black One to Black Ten, they were tigers as well. Beyond those ten, some might be wolves, foxes, or dogs. But those cherished by the Heuksadan leader were experts worthy of recognition.

Of course, the most reliable among them was Black Night.

In terms of martial prowess alone, he was the strongest after the Heuksadan leader.

He was a quiet and taciturn man by nature, yet he loved slaughter so much that he would enter battles he did not need to fight, enjoying the creation of seas of blood.

The one who appeared last was Black Hound.

He, too, was second only to the Heuksadan leader. If Black Night was second in martial arts, Black Hound was second in foul temper.

In Heuksadan, where murder, assault, and rape were commonplace, he was a notorious villain who handled deeds that humans could not bring themselves to commit.

“Chief, what in the world did you call us all here for?”

He was the one who called the Heuksadan leader “Chief.” Because he had assisted him since youth, he was also the only one who did not fear him.

“The Young Alliance Leader of the Sacred Path Alliance has contacted me to meet.”

“Why him?”

“He probably became the successor and now wants something.”

Black Hound’s gaze turned to the long box beside the Heuksadan leader. Seeing the sun and moon symbol drawn on the box, he asked with an expression of disbelief.

“Don’t tell me it’s the Sun-Moon Sword?”

“It is.”

“You’re giving it to the Young Alliance Leader?”

When the Heuksadan leader nodded, Black Hound shouted in a flare of temper.

“It cannot be done. Why give that precious sword to him? Just give him some money and send him away.”

Black Hound knew well how much the Heuksadan leader cherished this sword. He glared at Song Chung and spoke.

“It must have been that loudmouth who told you to give it.”

Black Hound disliked Song Chung. He had originally been by the leader’s side, but had been pushed aside by Song Chung.

Song Chung’s expression stiffened.

*This ignorant mutt is already kicking up a fuss.*

This was precisely why he had said they should not bring Black Hound.

Just as the two were locked in a staredown—

“They’re coming.”

In the distance, a carriage and escorting martial artists came into view.

The Heuksadan leader spoke to his subordinates.

“We must at least let this esteemed guest know how filthy his hands will get if he tries to erase us from the martial world.”

Absolute Regression, Episode 344.

Chapter 344: Because I Do Not Allow It.
---------------------------------------
The carriage that had come racing stopped over ten zhang away.

The fifteen martial artists who had been guarding the carriage also dismounted. Among them were Geom Mugik and Jin Hagyun.

But the Heuksa Guild Leader was staring only at the carriage. The thought of meeting the Young Leader of Sadomeng made him nervous; he had only ever heard rumors, and today was the first time meeting him in person.

‘Just what kind of person is he?’

The carriage door opened, and Bisain stepped down.

The Bisain of this moment was not the one who had been teased by Geom Mugik and joked about being a picky eater.

He was the Young Leader of Sadomeng, radiating an intense presence with a cold, frigid aura reeking of blood.

‘As expected! The successor is indeed a successor!’

The onlookers could not help but exclaim in admiration.

Bisain slowly walked out toward the Heuksa Guild Leader. The Sadomeng’s Thirteenth Wolf—no, the Fifteenth Wolf—followed behind him.

The Heuksa Guild Leader’s first impression upon seeing Bisain’s face up close was this:

‘He’s terrifying as hell!’

He had heard rumors that the Young Leader had a strong presence, but he had not known it was to this extent. Moreover, the martial artists guarding him also looked far stronger than expected.

Then the Heuksa Guild Leader’s gaze naturally turned toward one person. Despite the opponent not revealing any aura, his eyes were drawn to the youngest martial artist standing at the very end.

When their eyes met, the opponent grinned.

‘Is he insane?’

It was the first time in his life he had seen a guard smile in such a situation.

But now was not the time to concern himself with that.

“I greet the Young Leader.”

The Heuksa Guild Leader bowed respectfully at the waist, but Bisain only gave a slight nod.

“I have heard much of the Guild Leader’s renown.”

At the cold reaction, the Heuksa Guild Leader judged that his expectations had been correct.

‘As expected! Is he trying to establish his authority?’

In truth, Bisain’s indifference was not for that reason. They were already notorious for all manner of sordid affairs, and the fact that they even kidnapped and sold children left him with no goodwill.

“I have prepared fine liquor and dishes in the village below. Let us go together.”

“That won’t be necessary. Let us speak here.”

“Very well. Perhaps because it knows a precious guest has arrived, the weather is very fine today.”

The Heuksa Guild Leader gave Songchung a glance. At that, Songchung brought forth a box containing the Ilwol Sword.

“A small token of my sincerity.”

He extended the box holding the Ilwol Sword to Bisain.

‘Can he continue to act so stiff even after receiving this?’

It would be a gift that changed those stiff boar bristles into soft lambswool.

One of the Sadomeng wolves came out and received the box in his stead, in case a hidden weapon was concealed within.

Once it was confirmed to be safe, he took out the sword inside and handed it to Bisain.

Bisain drew the Ilwol Sword.

The Ilwol Sword, revealing its sharp blade, captured the gazes of all who wielded swords in an instant.

“A fine sword.”

It could not compare to the Black Demon Sword that Geom Mugik possessed, but even so, it was a famous blade that could be counted among the finest ordinary treasured swords. It was truly a gift the Heuksa Guild Leader had given with great resolve.

“It suits the Young Leader well.”

Right then, Geom Mugik sent a telepathic message to Bisain.

—Give me that sword.

He was not someone who would beg out of greed for a blade.

—What scheme are you plotting now?

—You should at least give a gift of that caliber to your Fifteenth Wolf, your youngest.

Bisain looked at Geom Mugik. He wondered what the man was scheming, but how could he fathom those abyssal inner thoughts just by looking?

“Fifteenth Wolf!”

“Yes.”

As Geom Mugik walked forward, Bisain bestowed the sword upon him.

“A welcoming gift for becoming the Fifteenth Wolf of Sadomeng.”

“I shall receive it with gratitude, Young Leader!”

Geom Mugik accepted the sword without a word of refusal.

Watching that, Jin Hagyun understood. There was no way the Young Leader would give him a sword received from the Heuksa Guild, so this had clearly been Geom Mugik’s doing.

Geom Mugik, having accepted the sword and turned around, playfully sent a telepathic message to Jin Hagyun.

—That is why I told you to become the Fifteenth Wolf, did I not? He gave it to me because I am the youngest and cute.

—If you act cute twice, he would probably give you the Sadomeng itself.

—Then Yuseong would say half an empty wish has been fulfilled.

Having given the Ilwol Sword to a subordinate, not only the Heuksa Guild Leader but all the Heuksa martial artists he had brought were shocked.

Especially the Heuksa Guild Leader fell into shock. He had given a sword he cherished dearly, only for it to be handed to a subordinate right before his eyes?

‘This is a slight against me.’

Unbearable anger welled up, but he suppressed it with effort.

The one who could not endure was Heukgyeon. He shouted in a fury.

“Young Leader!”

Having lived his whole life as an unbridled overlord, even the name of the Sadomeng Young Leader could not stop him.

Rather, he had been waiting for an opportunity. An opportunity to show loyalty to the Heuksa Guild Leader, and to show others how gutsy a man he was.

Especially, it was a chance to thoroughly suppress Songchung, who was sucking up at the Guild Leader’s side.

“That sword is a gift our Guild Leader presented to the Young Leader. Is it not an act contrary to etiquette to hand it to a subordinate?”

Bisain only looked at him with that fearsome face, as if finding him pathetic.

The Heuksa Guild Leader quickly intervened.

“Contrary to etiquette? What insolence is this toward the Young Leader? Apologize at once!”

Of course, the Heuksa Guild Leader’s inner thoughts were the same as Heukgyeon’s. Heukgyeon had spoken the words he himself had wanted to say. Indeed, that was precisely why he had brought him.

At the Heuksa Guild Leader’s roar, Heukgyeon lowered his head and spoke.

“I apologize. I considered that sword not one just anyone could wield, and thus committed a discourtesy.”

But what could be felt from him was not a sincere apology, but lingering resentment. Had the other not been the Young Leader, curses would have already flown from his mouth.

Not just Heukgyeon, but the other Heuksa martial artists standing behind had the same thoughts. Naturally, the atmosphere stiffened.

Bisain sent a telepathic message to Geom Mugik.

—Is this what you wanted?

The unexpected reply that returned:

—It is still lukewarm. We must make it boil over.

Geom Mugik spoke to Heukgyeon.

“Did you say just anyone cannot wield it? I am not just anyone.”

Geom Mugik’s overbearing manner caused Heukgyeon’s face to harden instantly. He was barely holding back because it was the Young Leader, yet the one who looked the youngest had stepped forward treating him with contempt.

“The Young Leader cherishes me more than this mere sword. Is that not so, Young Leader?”

When Geom Mugik asked directly, Bisain made an awkward expression. Jin Hagyun, watching, counted himself fortunate not to be caught in that situation.

In the end, Bisain nodded his head slightly.

“See? He cherishes me more, does he not?”

Watching Geom Mugik’s triumphant appearance, Bisain inwardly sighed. Already he could hear Geom Mugik’s voice by his ear: ‘You said you cherished me, so why do this? Did you not say you cherished me?’

‘With this, he will tease me for a while.’

Meanwhile, the Heuksa Guild Leader could not understand the situation. Giving the sword to a subordinate, and then that subordinate asking the Young Leader if he did not cherish him?

‘What scheme are they trying to pull?’

But the situation was only beginning. Geom Mugik took out something new to heat the atmosphere even further. What he took from his bosom was several sheets of paper.

“In truth, we investigated you beforehand. I received this before arriving here.”

The contents written on the papers were information about the Heuksa Guild sent by Eunwol. Who had committed what crimes, and how they had conducted their business until now—it was all written there.

The Heuksa Guild Leader looked at Bisain with an expression asking just what kind of situation this was.

But Bisain was staring at Geom Mugik with eyes identical to his own. He looked as though he had not read the contents held in that young man’s hand.

Judging by this reaction alone, it did not look like a staged act.

‘Just what are these people…?’

No, and if they had investigated, they should have shown it to the Young Leader—why had he kept it tucked in his bosom? The Heuksa Guild Leader found it strange no matter how he thought about it.

Geom Mugik pushed one man into the gradually blazing flames.

“Who here is Heukgyeon? Let me see your face.”

Startled at being called out of nowhere, Heukgyeon was taken aback.

Moreover, the one calling him was the youngest brat who had been an eyesore throughout this meeting.

“It’s me.”

The dissatisfaction he had been unable to express toward the Young Leader turned into rage directed at Geom Mugik.

“You have eyes, a nose, and a mouth all intact. No matter how I look at it, you are human too; how could you commit such acts?”

Heukgyeon flared up in anger. Had it not been the Young Leader, he would have already drawn his sword and charged.

Seeing Heukgyeon on the verge of exploding, the Heuksa Guild Leader stepped in. If he did not stop him now, blades would be drawn.

“He is an attendant of the precious guest. Do not act recklessly!”

He then asked Bisain.

“But for what matter did you call me here?”

Unexpected events had continued one after another, so only now did he ask the reason he had been called. As unexpected as the question was, Bisain’s answer was equally so.

“I called you because I have a command for you.”

A command? He used the word command, not a request.

The Heuksa Guild Leader was inwardly displeased, but smiled even more brightly and spoke.

“Please give your command.”

Unexpected words flowed from Bisain.

“You are kidnapping and selling children, are you not?”

So surprised, the Heuksa Guild Leader was momentarily at a loss for words.

‘What is this now?’

He seemed to already know, so lying was impossible. No, there was no need to lie. What was the problem with something of this level?

“That is correct.”

Then Bisain issued a resolute command.

“From now on, cease.”

Not only the Heuksa Guild Leader, but all the Heuksa martial artists were surprised.

“May I ask the reason?”

This very moment was when Bisain could feel that he had changed. Had he not met Geom Mugik, this would have been something that never happened in his life, words he would not have spoken.

And Bisain asked himself. Is this something you truly want to do? Or is it something you are doing inevitably because of Geom Mugik?

At least, he could find the answer to that question.

“Because I do not permit it.”

The cause might be Geom Mugik, but he himself could not tolerate this matter. Those of the Sadomeng who kidnapped and sold children were not his Sadomeng.

“From now on, if you kidnap even a single child, your Heuksa Guild will disappear from the murim.”

Since descending from the carriage, Bisain had not emitted even the slightest hint of aura. Though he spoke these words calmly, a fearful and heavy silence settled over the place.

The Heuksa Guild Leader had reached his limit. His pride was wounded to the utmost, and his anger had reached its peak.

“Do you understand?”

Since the Heuksa Guild had established itself, no one had been able to order him around. Those who opposed him had their eyes gouged out and their limbs cut off to be thrown to the pigs. Such had been his life.

The Heuksa Guild Leader eventually lowered his head and answered.

“I understand.”

Just as Bisain turned to walk back to the carriage.

Someone shouted from behind.

“Return the sword before you go!”

The one who shouted was Heukgyeon. The Heuksa Guild Leader could endure, but Heukgyeon could not. Enduring until now had only been possible by drawing upon all the patience he would use in a lifetime.

Geom Mugik turned to look at him and spoke.

“If you can take it, try.”

As if he truly intended to do so, Heukgyeon walked forward.

The ones whose expressions hardened now were not Geom Mugik, Bisain, or Jin Hagyun.

They were the martial artists of the Sadomeng’s Thirteenth Wolf. Though Geom Mugik was not truly the Thirteenth Wolf, he wore the same uniform as them.

Yet a Heuksa brat dared to challenge him. They were clearly being looked down upon.

The Heuksa Guild Leader quickly shouted.

“You will face retaliation. Stand down!”

Geom Mugik moved exactly as the Heuksa Guild Leader intended. He looked at Bisain and spoke.

“Let us promise that even if I die, you will absolutely not seek revenge.”

How could the Heuksa Guild Leader or Heukgyeon know that all of this was Geom Mugik’s intention?

Bisain nodded.

“I promise in the name of the Young Leader. This duel will end between the two individuals.”

Heukgyeon drew his sword and stepped forward. Though he could not compare to Heukya, he was still ranked third in strength within the Heuksa Guild.

Geom Mugik drew the Ilwol Sword.

“Shall we test how good this sword is?”

Knowing Heukgyeon would fight as things stood out of pride, the Heuksa Guild Leader intervened.

“That would be unfair. Let him use your sword.”

Geom Mugik looked down at his own Black Demon Sword.

“Then wouldn’t that be too unfair?”

The Heuksa Guild Leader had no way of knowing what he meant.

“Well, if that is what you wish. Let us do so.”

Geom Mugik walked forward.

Heukgyeon thought only of defeating his opponent somehow and earning merit. He would reclaim the Ilwol Sword and retake his place at the Heuksa Guild Leader’s side.

“Rising from the bottom, I will show you the strength I have forged through real combat!”

Heukgyeon charged forward, swinging his sword.

Shhk!

Before he knew it, Geom Mugeuk's Black Demon Sword was lodged in his left shoulder.

"Huh?"

It had been so fast that he didn't even realize how he'd been struck. He wondered if this was a dream.

Again, shhk!

This time, his right shoulder was pierced. Only then did a terrible pain flood in from his left shoulder that had been stabbed moments ago. The instant Heukgyeon let out a shriek, the Black Demon Sword successively stabbed his elbows once more.

Geom Mugeuk would normally finish things with a single stroke, but today was different.

He severed the joints at the most painful spots, one after another.

"Uwaaaaaack!"

A terrible scream erupted, but Geom Mugeuk's sword did not stop. With each repeated stab, the places where a human feels the greatest agony were cut away.

Bisain and Jin Hagun exchanged glances. It was the first time they had seen Geom Mugeuk kill someone in such a painful manner. He wasn't someone who acted without reason, so the two silently watched his actions.

The Heuksa Lord couldn't dare intervene. No, it was already too late to intervene. Heukgyeon was already battered after being stabbed dozens of times. What was more surprising was that he still hadn't drawn his last breath. It meant that the one doing the stabbing was an expert of such caliber.

With no place left to stab, a streak of sword light slashed across the last remaining place.

His severed head rolled away from his body to beside the Heuksa Lord. It was the moment his dream of standing by the Lord's side was fulfilled.

The Heuksa Lord looked down at Heukgyeon's head, its eyes wide open and staring up at him.

'The martial arts of a mere guard were at this level? And the youngest one, at that?'

Had he put on airs, boasting that he would show them how filthy their hands would have to get to wipe them out, when he kept men like this under him? His body trembled involuntarily.

Bisain's voice transmission reached Geom Mugeuk.

—This was your intention from the start.

He had never planned to simply warn the Heuksa Order and leave.

—That is correct. By my standards, these men too belong to the Absolute Evil.

After a brief pause, Bisain asked.

—If I too come to belong to that Absolute Evil later, what will you do?

Geom Mugeuk walked over to him and handed him the letter he had received earlier.

—Surely you aren't someone who could become such a person?

As Bisain read the contents, his expression gradually darkened. The acts committed by them, led by Heukgyeon, were not simple villainy. Their wickedness was cruel and inhumane to a degree that even demons would shake their heads. Violating a young daughter before her parents was nothing, and they had also locked an entire family ignorant of martial arts in one place and burned them alive.

Now he understood why Geom Mugeuk had asked for the Sun Moon Sword and guided the situation to this point. He could also see why he had killed Heukgyeon so painfully.

Originally, Geom Mugeuk would have simply swept them all away, but considering his position and authority, he was deliberately creating a situation to kill them, exercising the utmost restraint.

Bisain's face burned with heat. After all, these people too had belonged to the same unorthodox faction.

Bisain strode toward the Heuksa Lord. He held out the letter and asked calmly.

"Was all of this your doing?"

While the Heuksa Lord hesitated, wondering how he should answer, a ghastly, chilling killing intent flowed from Bisain's eyes.

"I'm asking if you ordered it."

Absolute Regression: Chapter 345

Chapter 345: Beware of That Person More Than the Enemy.
-------------------------------------------------------
“No! Please, spare me!”

Words begging for his life spilled naturally from Heuksa Danju’s mouth.

The youngest of the Young Lord’s escorts had left Heukgyeon a mangled wreck before slitting his throat. During that entire process, Heukgyeon had not managed to swing his sword properly even once.

So how could he possibly think of resisting?

Moreover, the killing intent radiating from Bi Sain’s body oppressed Heuksa Danju terrifyingly.

As a martial artist of the demonic path, Heuksa Danju had encountered his share of killing intent, but what he felt now was more powerful than any he had ever experienced. Thick with the stench of blood, it poured from an enraged Bi Sain.

“Then you mean your subordinates acted on their own accord?”

Cornered, Heuksa Danju dragged in an innocent party.

“What the hell is going on?”

The man at whom Heuksa Danju roared was Songchung. Dragging him into this situation? Even though he knew full well that Songchung and Heukgyeon were not on such bad terms? It was absurd, but this was hardly the time to blame Heuksa Danju.

“Even I failed to know of this. Heukgyeon is an uneducated, lowly man. It seems he committed wicked deeds behind the Danju-nim’s back.”

Was there anyone present who did not know of his evil deeds? Yet if he admitted that Heukgyeon had always been that sort of man, the backlash would demand to know why he had not stopped him.

Bi Sain sent a telepathic message to Geom Muguk.

—The information written here—how accurate is it?

Since it came from Eunwol, its reliability went without saying.

—Information sent by the person I trust and cherish most. Consider it on par with the intelligence from the Tongcheon Pavilion of the main cult.

Having confirmed the reliability of the information, Bi Sain asked Songchung, “What is your name?”

“I am Songchung.”

Bi Sain flipped through the paper in his hand, searching for Songchung’s information.

Songchung’s heart began to pound wildly. Depending on what was written there, his life or death would be decided. That paper was truly the Book of Life and Death held by the Grim Reaper.

Fortunately, no sword came flying. The evil deeds recorded there were apparently not severe enough to warrant death.

In the meantime, having caught his breath, Heuksa Danju prostrated himself on the spot and vowed, “There will be no more kidnapping of children. I will also properly restrain my subordinates from crossing the line.”

Instead of answering, Bi Sain walked toward where Heukgyeon’s corpse lay. One might have thought he went to look at the body, but that was not the case.

Bi Sain drew his sword and stained its tip with blood. Then he drew three lines in blood across the paper he held.

He handed that paper to Heuksa Danju.

Three names had lines of blood drawn through them. They were ones whose evil deeds were severe even within the Black Fiend Squad. Without saying a word, Heuksa Danju understood what Bi Sain meant to convey.

“Heukya, come here for a moment and look at this.”

He called over Heukya, who had been standing there in silence until then.

The moment Heukya walked over and saw what was written on the paper—

Sshick!

Heuksa Danju’s sword was drawn by surprise, slashing at his chest.

But Heukya seemed to have sensed it beforehand and evaded the attack.

“I expected as much.”

“Do not resent me! It is because your evil deeds run deep.”

The two engaged in a bloody battle.

Since the two strongest members of the Black Fiend Squad were suddenly waging a bloody battle, the squad’s martial artists were bewildered, at a loss for what to do.

“Kill Heukya!”

“Kill the Danju! He’s trying to have us all killed!”

“It’s a lie! Join forces and kill Heukya! That is an order!”

In an instant, the place became a scene of utter chaos.

Heuksam, who had quietly slipped to the rear, tried to leap away to escape. He knew well that avoiding the situation first was the best course of action.

But the First of the Thirteen Apostles, having received Bi Sain’s order, was already blocking his path.

Unable to even think of breaking through, Heuksam retreated. Having seen Heukgyeon die without putting up a single hand, no one today would dare draw their sword against the Thirteen Apostles.

The other Apostles also surrounded the area perfectly, making escape impossible.

Bi Sain watched the fight silently, arms crossed.

Though Heukya’s martial arts were inferior to Heuksa Danju’s, the rage of being betrayed drove him to frenzy. Since it was a fight to the death, sword energy flew wildly.

Geom Muguk and Jin Hagun watched the battle from outside the Thirteen Apostles’ encirclement.

Swoosh.

Jin Hagun pulled Geom Muguk’s sleeve toward himself.

A stray blade of sword energy flying from afar brushed past where Geom Muguk had stood.

“You saved my life!”

“You say that boldly. You could have dodged it ten more times on your own.”

“Great Lord Jin.”

“Why are you raising your voice?”

“I truly hope you become the successor.”

“Why so sudden?”

“It just suddenly came to me that I would like that. I say it whenever it comes to mind.”

He spoke as if joking, but one could feel he meant every word.

“How could that happen according to my will?”

His grandfather, the Alliance Lord Jin Paecheon, was a man who would ruthlessly push aside even his own grandson from the position of successor if he erred.

“Why do you wish for me to become the successor?”

Because I seem easy? He had expected an answer like that.

“Because I think you would do well.”

Jin Hagun looked at Geom Muguk.

“Be it friend or foe, one must be capable. Only then is it fun to play and worthwhile to fight.”

This time, Geom Muguk pulled him toward his own side.

Swoosh.

Another flying blade of sword energy passed through where Jin Hagun had been standing.

“I saved your life! Don’t you ever forget it!”

Listening to his banter, Jin Hagun wondered. How would his relationship with this man ultimately end? Could they truly become friends?

Meanwhile, the fight ended.

One man stood; the other had become a corpse upon the ground.

There was no upset. As expected from their skill, the victor was Heuksa Danju.

Heukya had fought many battles, but only battles he could win. Killing unrelated to skill was of no help in saving his own life.

That was not the end. Since there had been three names with lines drawn through them, Heuksa Danju ambushed and killed Ilsam and Ilpal as well. With skill lower than even Heukya’s, they could not endure Heuksa Danju’s surprise attack.

“It is all finished.”

In response to Heuksa Danju’s report, Bi Sain spoke.

“Now four of the five most vicious people in the Black Fiend Squad are dead.”

“Five? There were only three names with lines drawn through them.”

Even including Heukgyeon, that made four. Wondering if he had missed one, Heuksa Danju raised the paper again.

It was at that very moment.

Swoosh.

Slash.

Bi Sain’s sword split Heuksa Danju’s throat.

In the process, the paper Heuksa Danju held was also cut, and by coincidence, the part bearing Heuksa Danju’s own name was severed away.

Beneath it, a red line was visible.

That line, which looked as though someone had drawn a mark through it, was the line drawn across Heuksa Danju’s throat.

Puaaaak!

In the next moment, blood sprayed from that line and Heuksa Danju collapsed.

Bi Sain did not forgive him.

Though the evil deeds Heuksa Danju had committed to establish the Black Fiend Squad were evil in themselves, his greatest crime was his complacency in allowing the evil deeds of subordinates like Heukgyeon and Heukya to continue unchecked.

The remaining Black Fiend Squad martial artists trembled in fear. They were certain they would be massacred.

But Bi Sain did not kill them.

“From now on, do nothing and stay exactly as you are. Go back and wait without even breathing until someone from our side arrives!”

The Black Fiend Squad simultaneously bowed at the waist and shouted, “Thank you for sparing our lives.”

Having narrowly escaped death, they left that place.

From this time on, new tidings would spread through the demonic martial world. News that the Young Lord of the Apostles Alliance had personally delivered a crushing blow to evil deeds that had gone too far. There would be those who opposed it, and those who welcomed it.

One thing was clear: whether that evaluation was positive or negative, it would be a different evaluation from yesterday. Bi Sain had taken one step into another world.

Bi Sain walked over to Geom Muguk and Jin Hagun.

“You probably wanted to kill them all, but this was my best.”

“Kill them all? Did you take me for a murderer? Let us go look in a mirror together and see whose face is closer to a murderer’s.”

“People who look as upstanding as you often commit terrifying deeds once you get to know them.”

“It is infuriating, but I cannot deny it.”

After such an exchange, the playfulness vanished from Bi Sain’s face. Words he had hesitated to speak spilled from his mouth.

“This is our demonic path.”

He spoke not out of pride, but out of shame. At least for today, he felt deep self-loathing.

Jin Hagun had been about to say that just as there were bad men in the orthodox path, there were good people in the demonic path.

But before he could, Geom Muguk spoke.

“It was trash.”

Bi Sain did not get angry. Rather, having it said so openly made him feel at ease.

Watching that, Jin Hagun realized that the words he had been about to say would have been a completely useless comfort.

How did Geom Muguk understand people’s hearts so well? Despite being younger than him.

“Still, you have no reason to be depressed. That is not your demonic path.”

“What do you mean?”

“Is not your demonic path not over there, but right here?”

Wondering what he meant, he saw Geom Muguk look toward the Thirteen Apostles.

“Aren’t these people your demonic path? They are not even your direct subordinates yet, are they? You are not even the Alliance Lord yet.”

Jin Hagun thought inwardly, *Well done.*

Yes, this was exactly the kind of consolation Bi Sain needed right now.

One had to be able to call it trash. One had to be able to say, “You’re not even the Alliance Lord yet.”

For that to be possible, all the many things Geom Muguk had done for Bi Sain up to now had to serve as the foundation. Tirelessly coming to find him, tirelessly exchanging banter, tirelessly showing consideration.

And so the word “trash” became a jewel lodged in Bi Sain’s heart.

“Great Lord Jin, say something as well.”

Even in this situation, look at him looking out for him.

“I am not yet prepared to say the word trash.”

Geom Muguk smiled at Jin Hagun.

“This is why you must become the Alliance Lord. Only if you become an interesting person will life as an Alliance Lord be enjoyable.”

“It is the first time in my life I have heard the word ‘interesting.’”

A faint smile formed on Bi Sain’s lips as he listened to their conversation. When he was with Geom Muguk, he found himself smiling like this without realizing it.

Geom Muguk said to Bi Sain, “You handled today’s matter well. I could not have made such a wise choice as you.”

Bi Sain knew. It had only been possible because of Geom Muguk’s concession. Thanks to that, he had been able to preserve his authority before his subordinates.

And then the decisive words that followed.

“Your apostles—I like them.”

Though he showed nothing outwardly, Bi Sain was deeply moved. It was a more pleasant thing to hear than a hundred words of praise.

Geom Muguk asked Jin Hagun, “What do you think?”

“I am not someone who easily says ‘I like them’ to a person’s face, as you do.”

Jin Hagun spoke, looking at the corpses scattered in the distance.

“But I do think the Black Fiend Squad matter was handled very well today.”

Then Geom Muguk smiled and said, “Do you understand why he is going on at such length?”

The three of them smiled together.

Geom Muguk returned the box containing the Sun-Moon Sword to Bi Sain.

“Here, take this.”

But Bi Sain uttered unexpected words.

“This is your gift.”

“A gift?”

“A commemorative gift for entering as the youngest of the Thirteen Apostles.”

Everyone present knew well how great the value of the Sun-Moon Sword was. A treasured sword that could not be easily obtained no matter how much money one had. Thus, they could also tell how grateful Bi Sain was to Geom Muguk.

“Tonight you will regret giving it away needlessly and be unable to sleep.”

“That may be so.”

“Are you still fine with that?”

Bi Sain nodded.

“Have I ever told you? Not because I gave you a gift, but your face—you look magnificent. The Fist Demon Master, the Demon Lord, and you. Three great figures who are grotesque and fearsome, yet more magnificent the more one looks upon them.”

“That is praise because you gave me a gift. Admit it.”

Geom Muguk hugged the box containing the Sun-Moon Sword happily. He was so happy that he truly felt he had given a good gift.

“Having received such a precious gift, I cannot simply stay still. From now on, boast about it. Spread rumors far and wide that the Demonic Cult’s Young Lord came and went as the youngest member of your Thirteen Apostles. Spread them to your heart’s content.”

The Thirteen Apostles had already experienced Geom Muguk and knew him well enough, so they took his banter as mere banter.

“If such rumors spread, will you not be chased out of the Young Lord’s position?”

To Bi Sain’s concern, Geom Muguk replied, “Then I will come find you, so accept me. Let us play together for the rest of our lives.”

Shaking off the absurd thought that he would truly like that, Bi Sain spoke to his subordinates.

“You heard how troublesome it would become, so none of you are to spread rumors.”

“Yes!”

A thunderous reply rang out.

Bi Sain spoke to the two of them. “I intend to return to the main alliance.”

It was time to root out those lurking within the Apostles Alliance.

Geom Muguk added, “Let me go as well.”

Since this matter was not merely the Apostles Alliance’s problem, Bi Sain nodded. If they were enemies scheming against the entire Murim, insisting on solving it alone out of pride was something only fools would do.

“I will gratefully accept your help.”

Now only Jin Hagun’s decision remained.

“I shall return to the Murim Alliance now.”

Truthfully, coming here dressed as one of the Thirteen Apostles had already been a tremendous strain. Moreover, he could not take the Demon Extermination Corps to the Apostles Alliance headquarters.

Bi Sain conveyed his gratitude to Jin Hagun.

“I am grateful.”

“Think nothing of it. Be more careful of that person than of your enemies.”

“Thank you for the advice.”

Geom Muguk, who had been listening, said, “At least pretend to whisper!”

And so, Jin Hagun left that place.

The martial artists of the Demon Extermination Corps, who had been hiding in the distance, revealed themselves and welcomed Jin Hagun.

Before leaving, Jin Hagun lined up on the ridgeline with his subordinates and watched the carriage rushing toward the Apostles Alliance in the distance.

The sunset, embodying Jin Hagun’s wish to go with them, dyed the world red and began to swiftly chase the receding carriage.

Absolute Regression — Episode 346

Chapter 346: Let’s Kill Them First
----------------------------------
The carriage raced swiftly toward the Sado Alliance.

Bi Sa-in silently gazed out the window, thinking about who might be behind this affair.

An enemy plotting against the entire murim. How grand.

Looking back through the history of the murim, people who caused incidents on this scale tended to appear once every few generations.

Normally, they would have continued weaving their schemes. But because of one man, they had been exposed. Of all times, they had chosen to plot in an era when that man existed.

Bi Sa-in’s gaze turned toward Geom Mu-geuk, who sat across from him.

Geom Mu-geuk had his eyes closed. Even while conversing with him, whenever there was the slightest gap, he would recite some mnemonic verse and sink into training. He seemed like a man made entirely of talent, but through this opportunity, Bi Sa-in had learned that he was in fact made entirely of effort.

Bi Sa-in’s gaze shifted to the seat beside Geom Mu-geuk. The box containing the Sun-Moon Sword, which he had given him as a gift, stood there. Though he had told him to load it into the carriage’s baggage compartment, Geom Mu-geuk had stubbornly insisted like a child that he would keep it beside him.

Just then, the carriage lurched violently with a loud rattle.

The passengers’ backsides bounced up, and the sword box beside him floated into the air.

It seemed as though the box would slam down onto the floor, but instead it hovered in midair before quietly settling back into place.

Geom Mu-geuk had protected the sword box with Void Grasping. Bi Sa-in had thought he was circulating his qi or asleep, but even in the midst of that, he had been paying attention to the sword box.

“I’m grateful that you treasure my gift so much, but wouldn’t it be more convenient to throw away the box and just carry the sword?”

Geom Mu-geuk answered with his eyes still closed.

“I’m going to give the sword as a gift. Wouldn’t it be better if I gave it in its box?”

Because it was the official box for storing the Sun-Moon Sword, the sun and moon were magnificently engraved upon it.

“Who are you giving it to, that you’re taking such care?”

“To my heart.”

When Bi Sa-in looked at him with an expression asking what sort of joke that was, Geom Mu-geuk opened his eyes.

“There is someone as important to me as my own heart. Do you think I would give such a precious sword to just anyone?”

The sight of him sincerely caring for someone suited him, but at the same time, Geom Mu-geuk was also the sort of person who gave off this impression.

“Somehow, you seem like the sort of person who would hand a treasured sword to a stranger passing by on the road.”

“Then you misunderstand me. I hate taking losses. If I gave a sword to someone I didn’t know, it would be because I was aiming for something ten times more precious that person possessed.”

“Then what are you aiming for by giving it to that heart?”

“The heart is an exception. Even if I aim for nothing, that person is someone who would try to give me everything they have.”

At those words, Bi Sa-in wondered who he himself would give a gift to.

No one came to mind.

If he absolutely had to give it to someone, perhaps he would give it to the First Wolf among the Sado Thirteen Wolves. Thinking about it, he realized that he still did not have a friend to whom he could readily give such a precious gift.

It would be troublesome if the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult became his only friend.

Bi Sa-in looked at Geom Mu-geuk again. Geom Mu-geuk was not wearing his usual glib expression, but was gazing at the passing scenery with calm, deep eyes.

Just as Bi Sa-in turned his head toward the window on the opposite side, Geom Mu-geuk suddenly spoke.

“My demonic path and your unorthodox path both have a long way to go. If they’re built wrongly, we build them again. If a better idea comes to mind, we tear them down and build them again. So let’s not rush too much.”

“Why say that all of a sudden?”

“It just came to mind. I’ve found that if I think, I’ll say it later, I end up forgetting and never saying it. So these days, I’m trying to say everything when it occurs to me.”

“So that’s why you talk so much.”

“Isn’t a chatterbox Young Cult Leader better? Better than a taciturn Young Cult Leader whose thoughts you can’t read?”

“The problem is that even though you’re a chatterbox, I still can’t read what’s inside you.”

Geom Mu-geuk smiled an inscrutable smile and looked back out the window.

Bi Sa-in would not know. How lonely his life had been before his regression. How precious this chatterbox life felt to him.

“What did the Lord of the Yugeom Sect confess?”

Geom Mu-geuk had guessed that Taejo, the Lord of the Yugeom Sect, had revealed something.

“He said the one who arranged the job for the Bigeom Sect to bring in children was Pyo Gi-gwang, the former leader of the Black Dragon Corps.”

Pyo Gi-gwang.

He was not someone in Geom Mu-geuk’s memories. Most likely, he had been used by someone as a disposable pawn and then discarded.

“He suffered a severe injury over a decade ago and stepped down from his position as leader of the Black Dragon Corps. Since then, he has been living like a crippled wreck.”

“Someone must have recruited him.”

“It seems so. Considering that he secured various interests for the Bigeom Sect, the person behind Corps Leader Pyo must undoubtedly be someone with power.”

Geom Mu-geuk naturally thought as much.

“It may even be someone you know well.”

Bi Sa-in acknowledged Geom Mu-geuk’s words. If it was a person of power within the Sado Alliance, there was no way he would not know them. The problem was who it was.

“What did he say they needed the children for?”

Bi Sa-in hesitated for a moment over whether he should answer this question. What if this affair had nothing to do with the mysterious organization said to have infiltrated the Demonic Cult and the Murim Alliance, and it was truly a secret organization within the Sado Alliance? Then he would be revealing the Sado Alliance’s secrets to the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.

“Did he say they were creating some secret organization?”

Bi Sa-in flinched for an instant. Truly, Geom Mu-geuk was so perceptive that even if Bi Sa-in wanted to deceive him, he could not.

“That’s right.”

Bi Sa-in answered honestly. Geom Mu-geuk had read Bi Sa-in’s concerns precisely.

“Are you worried that this secret organization might not be connected to the organization I mentioned?”

“That’s right.”

“Even if that is the case, if it is a secret organization being prepared without your knowledge, wouldn’t it be a problematic organization? As much as you leak information to me, there will be advantages for you as well. So think of it positively.”

Normally, Geom Mu-geuk would have had every right to be angry, saying that he had come all the way to the Sado Alliance to help, and yet Bi Sa-in intended to hide information from him.

But Geom Mu-geuk was looking at this matter with consideration for Bi Sa-in, his counterpart.

“If it truly bothers you, handle that part discreetly. If you need help, say so.”

He was still making Bi Sa-in the protagonist.

Yes. If he had intended to calculate profits and play petty tricks with this kind of Geom Mu-geuk, he should never have brought him along in the first place.

“No. I will share all information, so please help me.”

Geom Mu-geuk smiled. It was the same smile he had shown earlier when he handled the matter with Taejo openly.

You’re doing well.

It was a smile that seemed to say just that.

Whether he knew Bi Sa-in’s feelings or not, Geom Mu-geuk began narrowing down who was behind this affair.

“If a secret organization is actually being created within the alliance, who would be in a position to know?”

“Most information of any significance is supposed to reach me as well. But if something was carried out without my knowledge, then the Alliance Leader and the Chief Strategist would have been the ones leading it.”

The two most difficult people to deal with in the Sado Alliance had been mentioned.

They were connected to the ones behind this?

Geom Mu-geuk first ruled out one person.

The Sado Alliance Leader. He had been killed by Hwa Mu-gi before the regression, so there was no way they were on the same side.

“What sort of person is the Chief Strategist?”

“He is someone with truly strong loyalty toward the Alliance Leader. If you suspect the Chief Strategist, I would like to tell you that it is a waste of time. Not only to the Alliance Leader, but to the Sado Alliance as well, his loyalty runs very deep.”

One could never know the hearts of men. But if, for now, they excluded even the Chief Strategist as Bi Sa-in said…

“Who else is there?”

“What do you mean, who else?”

“Someone may have proceeded with the matter without those two knowing.”

If the matter had been carried out without the Sado Alliance Leader or the Chief Strategist knowing, then this affair could be called treason. In that case, there was a high possibility it was the doing of the people Geom Mu-geuk had spoken of.

“If it was power sufficient to grant interests to the Yugeom Sect, then it could be someone at the level of a pavilion master or corps leader. It could also be an elder-level figure.”

“Let’s keep various possibilities open and think about it.”

“First, I will investigate Corps Leader Pyo discreetly.”

At Bi Sa-in’s words, Geom Mu-geuk shook his head.

“There’s no time for that. By the time we arrive at the Sado Alliance, the one behind this will also know that the head of the Yellow Dragon Escort Agency is dead, and that even the Lord of the Yugeom Sect has been escorted to the Sado Alliance. The one behind this will think that not only can he no longer obtain children, but that his own identity might be exposed as well.”

He was right. Investigating discreetly would only give the other party time to prepare.

“Let’s break through head-on. The martial prowess of those active in the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, the Murim Alliance, and the Pungcheon Cult was nearly at the level of a Demon Venerable. Their methods of infiltration were also far from ordinary. Unless we push through swiftly and decisively, they will cut off their tail and vanish.”

Bi Sa-in also agreed with Geom Mu-geuk’s words. But there was a problem.

“It would not be difficult to seize Corps Leader Pyo and investigate him. But there is something he said that troubles me. When the Lord of the Yugeom Sect confessed, he said Corps Leader Pyo had told him this: that the fate of the Sado Alliance rested upon the secret organization he was going to create. If he truly believes he is carrying out a secret operation for the alliance, he will not confess easily. He may even kill himself.”

That was not his only concern. Bi Sa-in voiced one more worry.

“The one who ordered this may kill Corps Leader Pyo to cut off the tail.”

Geom Mu-geuk had a solution to that concern. Though it was a method Bi Sa-in could never have imagined.

“Then let’s kill him first.”

“What?”

Bi Sa-in stared at Geom Mu-geuk with wide eyes, but the incomprehensible words continued.

“Let’s cut off that tail and take it first.”

* * *

“Just how long are you going to live like that?”

At someone’s shout, the noisy surroundings fell quiet. Everyone’s gaze turned to the two people in the banquet hall.

“There was a time when I respected you, and yet how did you end up so ruined?”

Pyo Gi-gwang had once again gathered martial artists in the banquet hall today and was boasting of his exploits from when he had been corps leader. Perhaps because he had gotten drunk and grown too excited, someone had stepped forward to rebuke him.

“Aren’t you ashamed before your juniors?”

The other man was none other than a martial artist who had once served under him in the Black Dragon Corps. Just as his words implied, he had once been someone who followed Pyo Gi-gwang’s orders well.

Ashamed before his juniors? Of what? What was so shameful about getting drunk and telling a few old stories?

He wanted to shout angrily.

‘I devoted myself so much to the alliance! And you can’t even listen to a few stories?’

But he could not bring himself to say it. The gazes around him were all looking at him pitifully as well. After all, this was not the first time he had shown such a side of himself.

Pyo Gi-gwang staggered out of the place. He was not that drunk, but he staggered on purpose. If he did not do even this, it felt as though he would become even more miserable.

In the past, he had truly acted like a crippled wreck. But recently, he had been acting out the life of the ruined man he used to be. In order to show that he was still a useless person.

Yet after experiencing something like this today, his heart sank coldly. At the very least, he had not intended to receive such reproach and humiliation from a former subordinate.

If he had truly been a ruined man, he would have smashed a liquor bottle, shouted, and caused a scene. But now he was simply turning away like this with a bitter heart.

‘If my martial arts were still what they used to be, would any of you have dared act this way?’

So long as that thought arose, his bitterness could only deepen.

That was when he entered a quiet footpath.

Suddenly, a masked man blocked his path.

Before Pyo Gi-gwang could even ask who he was, the masked man drew his sword and charged.

Pyo Gi-gwang tried to draw his own sword to block, but the opponent’s movement was too fast. So this is how I die! Despair swept over him at that very moment.

Clang!

Someone stepped in front of Pyo Gi-gwang and blocked the attack.

Having failed in his ambush, the masked man fled from the place in an instant.

The man who had blocked the attack turned toward Pyo Gi-gwang. When Pyo Gi-gwang confirmed the identity of the person who had saved him, he was startled.

“Young Alliance Leader?”

Several martial artists of the Sado Thirteen Wolves shot forward in pursuit of the fleeing masked man.

“Are you all right?”

“Thanks to you, I live.”

“I’m glad I wasn’t too late.”

Pyo Gi-gwang was inwardly startled and puzzled.

“Young Alliance Leader, why are you here?”

“I did not save you by chance while passing by. I was on my way to meet you, Corps Leader Pyo.”

“Me?”

It was truly unexpected for Pyo Gi-gwang.

Just then, the Sado Thirteen Wolves who had pursued the masked man returned.

“We lost him. His lightness skill was extraordinary.”

But Pyo Gigwang did not know. He did not know that the number of warriors who had pursued him had returned increased by one. The masked man who had attacked Pyo Gigwang earlier was Geom Mugeuk.

He had pretended to flee, changed into the attire of the Sado Thirteen Wolves, and returned together with them.

Of course, there was no way Pyo Gigwang could know that now. He was wondering why Bi Sain had come to see him.

“Who was the one who tried to kill you, Unit Leader?”

Pyo Gigwang shook his head.

“I don’t know either.”

One thing was certain: whoever it was had undoubtedly been trying to kill him. The sword that had flown toward him earlier had been filled with killing intent, aimed precisely at his heart. If it truly had not been for Bi Sain, he would be dead.

“Why have you come to see me?”

“While investigating the incident in which the Heuksa Corps kidnapped children and handed them over to Yugeommun, Unit Leader Pyo’s name came up. I have also heard that you asked the Yugeommun sect master to save the children. They said it was to create a secret organization. Is that true?”

Pyo Gigwang was flustered. He had never imagined that Bi Sain would suddenly come to him and ask about such a matter. Just as Geom Mugeuk had said, Bi Sain was charging straight in without wasting time.

“It seems the assassin earlier was sent to silence Unit Leader Pyo because I uncovered this matter.”

Bi Sain deliberately emphasized the words “silence.” Geom Mugeuk’s plan was to make it look as though those who had entrusted Pyo Gigwang with the task were trying to kill him, and thereby draw out his confession.

“What kind of secret organization are you creating? What is happening without my knowledge?”

Pyo Gigwang said nothing.

He did not suspect in the slightest that this attack had been arranged by the Young Alliance Leader. There was no reason for him to do such a thing. And most decisively, Pyo Gigwang had always anticipated a moment like this.

‘If the secret operation is leaked, they will try to eliminate me. So that day has finally come.’

A sacrifice for the great cause. Because of that, he instead thought that what was bound to come had finally arrived.

“I know that Unit Leader Pyo has devoted himself to our Alliance. I also know you are not someone who deserves to be treated like this. So please allow me to protect you.”

Because Pyo Gigwang had resolved to sacrifice himself for the Sado Alliance, he merely lowered his head and gave no answer.

“Very well. For now, I will withdraw. Instead, I will assign one of my subordinates as your guard.”

“Do not needlessly sacrifice your subordinate.”

“If you are worried about that, then tell my subordinate everything.”

Bi Sain turned to his subordinate and said,

“Protect Unit Leader Pyo well.”

“I shall obey your command.”

The person who stepped forward after receiving the order was Geom Mugeuk.

It was the moment an operation began: seizing a tail that did not even know which body it was attached to, and using it to find that body.

“I will protect you.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 347

Chapter 347: Please Tell Me Your Story
--------------------------------------
“Are we simply leaving those two be?”

On the way back to the Demonic Alliance’s main compound, Irang asked. Bisa In had sent Geom Mugeuk to guard Pyo Gigwang and had issued no further orders.

“The Young Cult Leader will handle it.”

These were words born not of indifference, but of faith. The faith that if Geom Mugeuk could not uncover it, no one could.

Irang, who had been watching silently until now, spoke the words he had been holding back.

“I know the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult is an extraordinary figure, but do not trust him too much. No matter what he says or does, he is ultimately nothing more than a demonic human.”

Irang had a deep-rooted distrust of demonic humans. Moreover, the relationship between Bisa In and Geom Mugeuk could only be described as abnormal.

“I shall do so.”

Because he knew well what Irang was worried about, Bisa In readily accepted his concern. After all, a prejudice held for so long would not change in an instant.

“Irang.”

“Yes, Young Alliance Leader.”

“Irang, what is the most memorable moment of your life as a martial artist?”

At the sudden question, Irang was taken aback.

“I don’t think I’ve ever thought about that question before.”

“Think about it now.”

Irang fell into thought for a moment. Swimming upstream against the current of time, he recalled a moment when a droplet of water, leaping forth, had shone dazzlingly in the sunlight.

“I believe it was when I first became one of the Demonic Alliance’s Thirteen Wolves.”

He remembered how joyful he had been. Many things had happened since. He had rendered great service and earned vast sums of money. But he didn’t think he had ever been as happy as he was then.

“I started as the youngest of the Thirteen Wolves, yet before I knew it, I became Ilrang.”

“How long has it been since you became one of the Thirteen Wolves?”

“It has been twenty-nine years.”

“Next year, we must hold a thirtieth anniversary celebration.”

Irang’s face flushed slightly.

“But why did you ask that?”

“Because I feel that moment is now. When I look back thirty years from now and ask what the most memorable moment of my life was, I believe it will be now.”

Now, when he had ridden a carriage through the Central Plains with the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult and the successor of the Righteous Faction; now, when he was uncovering a conspiracy alongside the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult. Would there ever again be events that excited and thrilled him so?

“But do not worry too much. I was not born with a temperament to sincerely trust and fall deeply for someone.”

Irang had reached an age where he knew that the human heart did not always obey one’s will.

But he did not say this. Because he thought that the problems arising from never having a moment where one felt oneself shine were no less serious than the negative events that might occur because of the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.

Shining more brilliantly than ever, Bisa In strode forward.

“Let us go. We too have much to do.”

* * *

Pyo Gigwang cast a sidelong glance at the young man following him.

When their eyes met, the young man smiled brightly. Naturally, it was Geom Mugeuk.

‘Poor fellow. He doesn’t even know what predicament he’s in.’

His young, handsome face was far too precious to die.

“What is your name?”

“It is Geomyeon. I use the ‘yeon’ from yeongi, meaning smoke. They say I will disappear like smoke when this meeting ends, so I am called Geomyeon.”

From the way he explained what had not been asked, his personality was plain to see.

‘How naive.’

Was it because he had nearly died to an assassin? The words “disappear like smoke” resonated deeply with Pyo Gigwang today.

“Do you know why you must protect me?”

“I have a rough idea.”

“That assassin from earlier might come to kill me again. Aren’t you afraid?”

As if believing in his own skill, Geom Mugeuk lightly tapped his sword.

It was a sword wrapped tightly in blood-stained bandages. Naturally, Pyo Gigwang did not know what kind of sword it was or what manner of bandages these were, so he took it for youthful arrogance.

“How long have you served the Young Alliance Leader?”

“It has been a few days.”

It was not a lie. It had indeed been that long since he had donned the uniform of the Demonic Alliance’s Thirteen Wolves.

Pyo Gigwang frowned.

‘I had wanted to die with dignity even in death, yet it seems I am fated to die burdened with a novice.’

Pyo Gigwang’s house was not far from the main compound, in the outer district. A mountain lay behind it, and a stream flowed nearby.

Though it was a scenic place, his house held no warmth. There was no food, and even the bedding was something used in summer—one couldn’t tell when it had last been washed. His usual life was plain to see.

“I only come here occasionally to sleep, so there is nothing of note.”

He lay down on the bed.

“There is no need to protect me.”

Pyo Gigwang was certain that this young man could not protect him alone. The assassin was someone whom numerous martial artists had chased earlier yet failed to catch.

‘If the Young Alliance Leader had truly been worried for me, he would have left more martial artists.’

He must have realized how formidable the assassin was during their clash. Thinking that even if he left several subordinates, they would all die, he had left only this young man.

Thinking so, the young man—who was about to die alongside him—felt as pathetic and pitiful as himself.

“Please rest.”

After a brief farewell, Geom Mugeuk went outside.

Pyo Gigwang tossed and turned, unable to fall asleep.

Emotions he had not felt earlier due to the chaos came flooding in. No matter how resolved he had been to face death, truly feeling it approach step by step filled him with fear.

—“The Alliance needs you.”

His expression when assigning the task came to mind.

At first, he had refused.

—“I am already broken beyond repair. Find someone else.”

—“That is precisely why I chose you. No one will suspect this operation.”

Honestly, it had stung. To be chosen because he was broken. But in the end, he had accepted. He had said it was out of loyalty, but in truth, it had been for himself. He had wanted to show those who scorned him: I am doing something more valuable than you. He couldn’t say it aloud because it was a secret operation, but he had wanted to shout it in his heart.

‘Scorn me to your heart’s content! I am doing something a hundred times more important than you.’

Perhaps that person had anticipated these very feelings of his.

Afterward, he had lived diligently as instructed.

But to think that just because the secret had been revealed, they would so easily cast him aside to die.

If he had come in person and said, “I’m sorry, it can’t be helped,” while killing him, Pyo Gigwang would not have felt this sorrowful.

He would have gladly died smiling. But to send an assassin without giving him a chance to leave a single word? It wasn’t the fact that they wanted to kill him that angered him—it was how thoroughly insignificant they had made him feel in the process.

‘It wasn’t for you. It was out of loyalty to the Alliance.’

In the end, he was so upset that he had to console himself with such words.

* * *

Pyo Gigwang dreamed of being chased by assassins.

In the dream, he was neither composed nor magnanimous. To survive, he ran through mountains, dug into the earth to hide, and concealed his body even in foul-smelling filth.

Then he woke at dawn.

A thick cotton blanket had been draped over him at some point. It seemed the young guard had found it and covered him.

Pyo Gigwang rose from his bed and walked to the window.

Geom Mugeuk stood alone in the courtyard, looking up at the night sky. Seeing him staying awake rather than sleeping to keep watch, Pyo Gigwang felt sorry.

Sensing his presence, Geom Mugeuk turned toward him.

“Are you awake?”

“Aren’t you cold?”

“I am fine.”

He said he was fine, but the early winter dawn wind was bitingly cold.

“What were you thinking about?”

“I was thinking about people.”

“Who?”

“People who I think might think of me at least once.”

From someone who would faint from shock if they heard of Pyo Gigwang, to someone enjoying a once-in-a-lifetime vacation right now.

Leaning against the window frame, Pyo Gigwang asked.

“What of your family?”

“I have a father and an older brother.”

“And your mother?”

“She passed away when I was young.”

“Your father must have had a difficult time raising you. How is he now? I suppose he must be pleased that you entered the Demonic Alliance?”

‘If he heard that I had joined the Demonic Alliance after leaving home, he’d probably be sneering in that characteristic way of his.’

“He will not be pleased.”

“Why?”

“He dislikes the Demonic Alliance.”

Pyo Gigwang smiled faintly, for it had been the same with him. His parents had been truly displeased at him entering the Demonic Alliance. They had wished he would enter the Martial Alliance. They hadn’t known how hopeless their son was.

“The dawn wind is cold; come inside. If you can protect me from there, you can protect me from here. Come in.”

Geom Mugeuk came inside, pretending he could not refuse.

“I will warm myself for a moment and then go back out.”

“There is nothing better than this for warming the body.”

He found some liquor. In a house with not a morsel of food, there was alcohol.

“Do you like to drink?”

“I do.”

He could have said he didn’t drink while on duty. Was it because he was a young martial artist of the current generation? He could sense that this was someone honest with his emotions.

Pyo Gigwang poured a cup for Geom Mugeuk and filled his own as well.

Perhaps he would die before seeing the morning sun. It would not be strange at all if a hidden weapon came flying through that window right now. If not today, then tomorrow, or within a few days, he would die. At this rate, he would end up dying alongside this young man.

Pyo Gigwang emptied his cup first, and Geom Mugeuk followed suit.

“I don’t wish to nag, but you shouldn’t recklessly drink alcohol offered by others. What if it’s poisoned?”

“I will be careful from now on.”

He felt pleased that Geom Mugeuk readily agreed to be careful. He was someone who missed such obvious reactions. Because for the past dozen years or so, no one had treated him properly. Though it was something he had brought upon himself.

“After I was injured, everything changed. My life and my surroundings all changed completely. Things that wouldn’t even dare meet my eyes began glaring at me. Trash, all of them.”

“They weren’t all trash. The trash must have covered your eyes and nose, keeping you from seeing what you should have seen.”

Pyo Gigwang looked at Geom Mugeuk with a gaze that asked, “What do you know?”

“I apologize. I overstepped. Memories of being cursed and yelled at linger longer in me than praise.”

Pyo Gigwang could not completely deny those words. Surely, there had been those who sincerely cared for him. But in the time he spent hating and raging against those who scorned him, they had all disappeared.

“Tell me honestly. What orders did the Young Alliance Leader give you? He told you to find out a name from me, didn’t he?”

“Yes.”

Geom Mugeuk answered readily. Pyo Gigwang had expected him to play dumb, so this unexpected reaction took him aback. Somehow, this young man kept defying his expectations.

“You will never hear it from me. So give up.”

“The reason you hide it must be your loyalty to the Alliance. I am the same. We each serve in our own way.”

The word “loyalty” felt strangely unfamiliar today. Perhaps it was because he kept thinking:

‘You and I have both been abandoned.’

It felt as though the person had been discarded, leaving only loyalty to drift about like a ghost. Pyo Gigwang emptied his cup again, using that ghost as his drinking snack.

“If I were you, I would have run away.”

Pyo Gigwang wanted to die alone. He wanted to calmly board the boat to the underworld by himself. He did not want to have to apologize to this young man who would already be aboard.

“I would have made some excuse and fled.”

Then Geom Mugeuk suddenly asked him,

“I heard you were the leader of the Black Dragon Division.”

“I was.”

“How was it then?”

“What do you mean, how was it?”

“I heard that in the past, the Black Dragon Division fought the Martial Alliance at Xinye Ping despite being outnumbered and achieved a great victory.”

“How did you know that?”

“It is a famous tale, is it not?”

Pyo Gigwang’s pupils trembled slightly.

“Yes, I was there too. I was a new recruit then, much like you.”

“I am curious. About the battle that day.”

The very story he had been proudly boasting about at the banquet earlier was this battle at Xinye Ping. It was then that he had been humiliated by his former subordinate.

Seeing someone ask about that moment with sparkling eyes in such a situation gave him a strange feeling.

“You wouldn’t know since you haven’t fought in real combat, but when you truly face an enemy, your heart pounds as though it might burst.”

“Were you afraid that day as well?”

“I was afraid. If I hadn’t had my fellow disciples and seniors with me, I would have turned and run. That day was especially bad. From the far end of that plain, the Murim Alliance bastards came swarming in like a horde of ants…”

He told him of the battle that day. Even as he spoke, the subordinate who had driven him into a corner in the banquet hall earlier kept coming to mind. Would this fellow sitting before him be sick of it inside, too? Would he be thinking, deep down, that it was just an old man bragging?

It was needless worry, an old man’s anxiety. Geom Mugeuk listened to his story better than anyone. He did not simply let it wash over him. He listened intently, asked questions, and responded in all the right places.

Listening to the embellished tale of heroics, Geom Mugeuk could tell. This moment was the most memorable and shining moment of the man’s life.

The long story came to an end. Geom Mugeuk had been with him in that battle from beginning to end.

“Thank you. For listening.”

“No, I should be the one thanking you for telling me. You were truly magnificent.”

The moment he heard those words, Pyo Gigwang felt an emotion he could not name welling up inside him.

It was a story he had repeated many times after injuring his body and stepping down from his position as unit leader. And yet, never once had he felt this surge of emotion.

Soon, he understood. No one had ever listened to his story this earnestly, or with such excitement. Everyone had been bored. They had only listened out of courtesy.

And today he realized that what he had longed for, for a very long time, was to see someone sincerely listening to his story like this.

Because after he had been injured, that was the only thing he could do.

* * *

When Pyo Gigwang opened his eyes, he was asleep on the bed.

‘When did I fall asleep?’

Seeing that he was covered with a blanket again, it seemed the youngster outside had taken care of him.

Bright sunlight was streaming in through the window. So he had survived, at least for now.

He got up from the bed and looked out the window. Geom Mugeuk was standing in the yard. It seemed he had stood there like that all night.

“Aren’t you hungry?”

“I am.”

At the honest answer, Pyo Gigwang came outside with a disheveled face.

“Let’s go eat.”

The two left the house and headed toward the marketplace.

Pyo Gigwang suddenly spoke.

“We’re like fish caught in a net. We don’t know when the fisherman will haul us up. You should at least get out while you can. There’s still time.”

An ordinary net won’t be enough for that. Unless the Majeongsa’s Heavenly Net is spread in three layers, perhaps.

“You know, don’t you? If I’m to ask my fisherman not to haul up the net, you have to tell me his name.”

Pyo Gigwang silently shook his head.

As if he had expected as much, Geom Mugeuk was not disappointed at all. Instead, he spoke with a bright expression.

“Then please tell me some more stories. I want to hear more about you, senior.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 348

Chapter 348: Where Does Loyalty Belong?
---------------------------------------
The backstreets of the Sado Alliance were no different from Maga Village.

Merchants laughing, chatting, and shouting among the passing crowd. If one looked only at this scene, excluding the auras of the martial artists, one couldn’t tell whether this was in front of the Murim Alliance or the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. It was a sight that made one automatically say that places where people live are all the same.

“What are you looking at so curiously?”

“I’ve been so devoted to training that it has been a long time since I’ve come out here.”

“If you’ve worked that hard, I suppose you serve the Young Alliance Lord at such a young age.”

Pyo Gigwang took Geom Mugeuk to a regular diner.

“It’s a place I come to often. The food is decent.”

But the owner and the waiter treating him had expressions nothing like those reserved for a regular.

“I’ve gotten into a few fights here.”

The fact that he could say such things naturally was because he felt comfortable with Geom Mugeuk. Could that happen in just one day? Moreover, he was someone who had been walking around with his quills raised like a hedgehog.

As anyone could see, that was indeed the case. He felt comfortable with Geom Mugeuk.

“Getting injured must have been a great blessing for the owner of this place.”

“What do you mean?”

“If it hadn’t happened, wouldn’t this place have been blown to pieces?”

Since he was someone who listened to his words so earnestly and even made such jokes, how could being together with him not be enjoyable?

An order reflecting Pyo Gigwang’s mood followed. He ordered good liquor and several of the most expensive dishes at this diner.

“Aren’t you overdoing it?”

“If I die anyway, it’s money I won’t be able to spend, isn’t it?”

“Please save it. As long as I am protecting you, you will never die.”

“Your confidence alone is something else.”

Instead of picking up his chopsticks, Pyo Gigwang poured alcohol into his cup.

“That person is not someone you can handle.”

For the first time, something about the one behind this came from his mouth.

“Even if I can’t handle it, don’t I have my fisherman?”

To the question asking what worry there was when he had Bisain, he drank his alcohol in silence.

Geom Mugeuk could sense it. That Pyo Gigwang thought Bisain would not be able to handle the one behind this matter.

“The food is delicious.”

As expected of a famous restaurant in the area, the food was truly good. If Jo Chunbae had been here, he would have joked around asking him to evaluate the Sado Alliance’s cuisine as a representative of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

“Please eat your meal instead of only drinking.”

“You eat plenty.”

Pyo Gigwang had no appetite. Thinking that he might die soon, his appetite had dropped away as if it were a lie.

And the one to bring back his appetite was not a skilled cook, but Geom Mugeuk.

“How did you rise to become the Black Dragon Commander?”

“You wouldn’t know how hard I worked.” Opening with those words, Pyo Gigwang gauged Geom Mugeuk’s reaction once more.

As if to show he wasn’t asking merely out of courtesy, Geom Mugeuk listened attentively to his words, his eyes sparkling even as he ate his food.

As he recounted past events, Pyo Gigwang became more and more exhilarated. How could he not be? He was boasting about the greatest achievement of his life.

“My competitor at the time was someone from a very good family. You couldn’t even imagine how much political scheming he did.”

“You overcame him with only your skill.”

“You could say that.”

“How could someone who relies only on backing and acts recklessly defeat someone with experience and effort built up from the bottom?”

A smile naturally formed on Pyo Gigwang’s lips.

Words saying his was a life that overcame obstacles through skill—come to think of it, he had never heard them even when he was a commander before getting injured. Was it because he had led an ill-fated life? Even though there were so many people around him, not a single person had uttered those words to him.

To the point where he felt as though he had endured such neglect for the past ten years just to meet this person today, Geom Mugeuk’s words pleased him.

“Please tell me another story.”

Geom Mugeuk understood Pyo Gigwang’s feelings very well. When he lived as a wanderer, the people he met most often were martial artists who had become wanderers after being injured. Those whose dantians were damaged so they couldn’t use internal energy, or those who had lost arms or legs.

After being expelled from their organizations, what other work could those who had eaten sword-rice their whole lives do? And so they lived on as third-rate wanderers.

When drunk, they would always boast about the times when they had been doing well. It wasn’t boasting, but rather a way of venting.

“Is my story interesting?”

“It is.”

Pyo Gigwang stared at Geom Mugeuk. This youngster kept showing him sides different from his expectations.

“Do you know what my first impression of you was? I thought you talked a lot. When I heard that the character was from ‘performance’ rather than ‘fate,’ I thought you would be talkative. Yet you are someone who listens well.”

“No, I talk a lot. I’ve just hidden my tongue because we’re not close yet.”

Perhaps because those words were amusing, Pyo Gigwang laughed. To think laughter could come when death was right before his eyes.

“Will we really have time to grow closer?”

To the self-deprecating question, Geom Mugeuk answered confidently.

“I will protect you.”

“Rather than that, let me hear your story now.”

“My story is too long.”

How could he know the meaning contained in that meaningful smile?

“You seem to have had a hard life.”

“Don’t even mention it.”

Geom Mugeuk waved his hand as if he didn’t even want to think about it.

“With your personality, it seems like you would have many people around you.”

“That’s why I’m lonelier.”

“More lonely?”

“When there are so many people, yet no one to actually meet. How sad it is when no one takes care of me. Everyone makes such excuses. They thought someone else would take care of me. That’s why people like me end up with a black hole in our hearts.”

“A black hole?”
