“It’s something someone I like said. They said everyone has a black hole in their heart. A hole you must not fall into, nor look into for too long.”

Pyo Gigwang thought the young lad was talking nonsense, but on the other hand, he thought of his own hole. He had one too. A deep and large hole that was so big he couldn’t help but fall into it, no matter how he tried not to.

He wished he had met this lad a little earlier.

If he had, would his fate have changed? Could he have lived a different life, rather than a fate of being silenced?

“If you’re done eating, let’s go now.”

“Let’s go.”

The two left the diner and headed home.

“Go get some sleep.”

“I cannot. I must protect you.”

“How long can you stay awake and protect me?”

He truly couldn’t understand the Young Alliance Lord. They should have sent at least someone to take shifts.

“Hey, did you perhaps make a mistake toward the Young Alliance Lord?”

I did. If you knew how much I embarrassed and teased him, you would faint.

“I told the Young Alliance Lord that I expected a magnificent Sado from him. Could that have been a mistake?”

Pyo Gigwang made an absurd expression.

“A new martial artist to the Young Alliance Lord?”

“Then when could I say it? The more senior one becomes, the less one can do such things, no?”

So this was why he had fallen out of favor. Those sparkling eyes must have been an eyesore. A figure that shone brighter than himself.

But to discard such a fine lad like this…

Then Pyo Gigwang suddenly had this thought.

A fine lad? Was he already thinking of him that way just because he listened to his words?

Pyo Gigwang could feel anew just how lonely he had lived.

Lost in such thoughts, it was when they had turned onto a secluded path.

Four masked figures blocked their path.

It was not something promised with Bisain.

The one behind this affair had sent people to silence Pyo Gigwang permanently. Judging by the revealed auras, they were hired assassins.

Pyo Gigwang’s heart sank.

‘The inevitable has finally come.’

That moment had arrived far sooner than he thought.

Fate is a cruel bastard. To bestow death at the happiest moment. Couldn’t it have come a few days later?

His gaze turned to Geom Mugeuk standing beside him. He wanted to hear more of this lad’s story. He still had stories he wanted to tell.

And more than anything, he was sorry.

‘This young man is going to die because of me for no reason.’

Pyo Gigwang spoke to Geom Mugeuk.

“It seems the fisherman is pulling in his net. I’m sorry. Truly sorry.”

To the sincere apology, Geom Mugeuk drew his sword and spoke.

“If it were my fisherman pulling, perhaps; but I have no intention of being pulled up by that fisherman’s hand.”

At the same time, the assassins leaped forward.

Two came at Geom Mugeuk, and the other two rushed at Pyo Gigwang.

Pyo Gigwang closed his eyes. Since he had already resigned himself to death anyway, he did not put up a clumsy resistance. He would end his life gracefully and coolly.

Swhiiish.

He heard the sound of sword wind flying toward him.

The moment he thought it was finally over.

Puh-uk!

He thought his body had been pierced, but it had not.

Geom Mugeuk had stepped in front of him and stabbed the opponent a step faster.

He felt another sword flying at him from behind. The one who had rushed at Geom Mugeuk to kill him had changed targets and attacked.

‘This time I’m done for.’

The moment he thought that, his body was pulled.

He was pulled toward Geom Mugeuk, dodging the flying sword.

Puh-uk!

Geom Mugeuk’s sword pierced the heart of the assassin who had attacked from behind. His movements, without a single superfluous motion, were truly fast and clean.

The remaining two assassins flew at Pyo Gigwang simultaneously. It was an attack with the resolve of mutual destruction—determined to kill Pyo Gigwang even if they died in the process.

‘Ah! This time I’m really dying!’

But this time too, both attacks missed and sliced through empty air. Just before being pierced by the swords, Pyo Gigwang fell over from an unknown force, causing the attacks to miss.

The price for that one-sided attack was death.

Geom Mugeuk’s sword, after piercing the left assassin’s neck, was instantly withdrawn to stab the right assassin’s heart. This continuous movement was as natural as if performing a prearranged sparring match.

Of course, Pyo Gigwang, who had fallen over, did not see this artistic move.

‘I survived again?’

Pyo Gigwang got up from the ground and looked around. The four masked figures who had blocked their path were all lying dead.

“What happened?”

“Didn’t I say I would protect you?”

It was right then.

Shi-shi-shi-shik!

Projectiles flew from the treetops.

Geom Mugeuk swung his sword to deflect the projectiles and shouted at Pyo Gigwang.

“Jump behind that tree!”

Pyo Gigwang ran frantically with Geom Mugeuk and hid his body behind a large tree.

Geom Mugeuk held out his sword to check the enemies reflected on the blade.

“There are three on the eastern tree. Another two are hiding behind the bushes beneath it.”

Of course, if Geom Mugeuk displayed his original strength, he could kill everyone here in an instant.

But he didn’t. Right now, to both Pyo Gigwang and the enemies, he was a martial artist of the Thirteenth Wolf of Sado. The youngest who had been hiding his skills—he was displaying exactly that level of ability.

“It’s still not too late. Leave me behind and go.”

Pyo Gigwang never imagined he would say such words to save a fledgling he had met yesterday—no, not a fledgling in skill, but someone who wasn’t even a day’s acquaintance.

“You took down four of them, so the Young Alliance Lord will understand.”

Then someone dropped down from the tree.

Swhi-ik!

Because it was a concealment he had never expected, this time Pyo Gigwang thought he was truly done for.

‘I’m really going to die!’

But just before the opponent’s sword struck his head, Geom Mugeuk gently pulled him.

He felt the falling sword brush past his ear. Then wouldn’t it hit his shoulder? Just as he thought that.

Puaaak!

Spewing hot blood, the assassin who had ambushed him rolled on the ground as a corpse.

How he had dealt with him, Pyo Gigwang could not see properly this time either.

‘I survived again? How in the world?’

Before he could ask anything, Geom Mugeuk grabbed him again.

“Behind that rock over there!”

Pyo Gigwang ran with Geom Mugeuk. He was confused. Since he wasn’t alone and Geom Mugeuk was with him, he couldn’t not run, yet this evasion wasn’t what he wanted either.

Pak-pak-pak-pak-pak!

Throwing daggers flew and embedded themselves at the feet of the two running men. No matter how excellent an assassin’s throwing skills were, could they hit even one while Geom Mugeuk was pulling him along?

Narrowly avoiding the attacks, the two safely hid behind the rock.

“Leave me behind, I said!”

“Why are you accepting it when they’re trying to silence you permanently?”

“You don’t understand my heart.”

Leaning against the rock, Geom Mugeuk looked at him.

“Is it because of loyalty to the Alliance?”

Naturally, Pyo Gigwang looked at Geom Mugeuk with eyes that said it was.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward the corpses lying in the distance.

“Those are hired assassins. What kind of alliance in the world sends hired assassins after a subordinate they ordered to do a task? Where in this filthy situation is there any room for loyalty?”

“!”

Then another assassin climbed over the rock.

Swhack!

Just as Geom Mugeuk cut the assassin’s throat—

Assassins who leaped out from behind the rear trees threw projectiles.

Shi-shi-shi-shi-shi-shi-shi-shi-shik!

Seeing the dozen or so projectiles flying toward him, Pyo Gigwang thought this was finally the absolute end.

‘Please, at least you must survive!’

Puk-puk-puk-puk-puk-puk-puk!

Opening his eyes at the sound of tearing flesh, Geom Mugeuk had already killed the assassin who had climbed over the rock and was standing before him using the corpse as a shield. All the flying projectiles were embedded in that corpse.

‘What, I survived again?’

Before he could say anything, Geom Mugeuk grabbed him and started running into the mountain.

“Run!”

Behind them, assassins who had fully revealed themselves began chasing in a swarm.

Running frantically with Geom Mugeuk, Pyo Gigwang had only this thought:

‘Why do I keep surviving?’

Absolute Regression, Episode 349.

Chapter 349: I Never Gave Permission.
-------------------------------------
Bi Sain entered the long corridor.

On either side of the path leading into the Alliance Leader’s Hall stood wolves made of steel, poised as if they might leap at any moment.

Whenever one passed through this corridor, one felt a unique, stale, suffocating air of menace.

Was that why?

He would find himself thinking that perhaps the old tale was true—that if an intruder entered, those steel wolves would come alive and bite at the intruder’s throat until their own bodies were torn to shreds.

After passing through the corridor and opening the great doors, he saw a man standing with his back turned at the far end of the Alliance Leader’s Hall. His build was ordinary. Neither thin nor fat, that back looked like one that could be found anywhere in the marketplace.

But Bi Sain knew. That was the back of a man who could be found nowhere else in all murim.

Baek Jagang, Leader of the Sado Alliance.

The greatest master of the Evil Path, and the absolute ruler of the unorthodox murim.

Bi Sain was Baek Jagang’s disciple. Baek Jagang had no blood kin of his own. His reason was that having family made one’s heart weak. Put another way, it also meant that his heart would not grow weak for Bi Sain.

Tense, Bi Sain walked to the foot of the stairs.

As always, standing before the Alliance Leader made his heart shrink and his nerves tighten.

“It looks as though it will rain.”

Baek Jagang’s voice rang through the Alliance Leader’s Hall. His voice was deep and resonant.

“It will be the first rain of this winter.”

Ordinarily, Bi Sain would have offered a stiff greeting, saying that he had returned safely. But today, he answered the Alliance Leader’s words.

He did not want to admit it, but it was Geom Mugeuk’s influence. He had accepted Geom Mugeuk’s advice to truly look at people, and had resolved to see the Alliance Leader differently than before.

A different response drew forth a different response in turn.

“Come up.”

Bi Sain climbed the stairs and walked to where the Leader of the Sado Alliance stood. This was the first time he had ever been called to this place, where the entire main hall could be seen at a glance.

Only after standing beside him did he bow respectfully.

“I have returned, Alliance Leader.”

Baek Jagang slowly turned his head and looked at Bi Sain.

His two eyes were as small as buttonholes. Truly, eyes so small they looked like dots. From time to time, Bi Sain thought that perhaps those eyes would someday widen in surprise. That he must have been hiding his real eyes somewhere. That was how small they were.

Because of that, it was not easy to read his emotions from his eyes. Yet conversely, the Alliance Leader was a man who could pierce through to another’s innermost thoughts with those tiny eyes.

“I hear you cleaned up the Black Serpent Corps?”

But his voice was better than anyone’s. Whenever Bi Sain heard it, he always thought he wanted to possess a voice like that.

“Yes. I eliminated the Black Serpent Corps Leader and part of his command.”

“Your reason?”

What should he answer? If he were to answer honestly, he would say this: because the Black Serpent Corps kidnapped children and committed evil deeds.

But that was not the correct answer. Bi Sain thought he did not know the Leader of the Sado Alliance well, but at the very least, he knew that was not the correct answer.

Bi Sain gave the answer he believed was right.

“He acted insolently.”

The small eyes that had barely been visible vanished as Baek Jagang burst into loud laughter. His eyes were small, but his mouth was unusually large. So when he laughed heartily, it truly seemed like a great laugh.

Even if the Alliance Leader knew why he had dealt with them, this was how he had to answer. His master was that kind of man.

“Since it has come to this, I intend to absorb the Black Serpent Corps in stages while observing public opinion outside.”

Baek Jagang wore a satisfied expression. He was a man who would do anything if it made the Sado Alliance stronger. And at the root of that reason lay the Demonic Cult.

“Geom Ujin will certainly start a war to unite murim under one rule. We must prepare for that day.”

He was convinced that the current Heavenly Demon would start a war.

“If he starts a war, he will strike us first.”

“Why us, and not the Murim Alliance?”

“Because the Murim Alliance will not involve itself in that fight.”

Baek Jagang predicted the Murim Alliance’s reaction as follows.

“They will absolutely not interfere, hoping that we and the Demonic Cult will fight each other and fall together. Even if they wished to help, do you think they would join hands with us?”

Countless elders of the orthodox factions would rise in opposition to any decision to aid the unorthodox factions, and even if the Murim Alliance Leader wanted it, it would not be an easy matter to force through.

“Even knowing that when the lips are gone, the teeth grow cold, they will never help us. Even if all those teeth are pulled out later, they will mumble with their gums and prattle on about righteousness.”

Hatred was hatred. Baek Jagang’s judgment was cold and clear.

“If by any chance war breaks out between the Demonic Cult and the Murim Alliance, you must aid the Murim Alliance without fail. The Demonic Cult will certainly try to monopolize murim. The same goes for when you become Alliance Leader.”

That was how dangerous Baek Jagang considered the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, and Geom Ujin.

Bi Sain thought the Alliance Leader’s strength lay in the fact that he did not indulge in useless pride. The Alliance Leader never exaggerated the strength of the Sado Alliance. Rather, he proceeded with everything on the premise that they were one step beneath the Demonic Cult and the Murim Alliance. Bi Sain believed that the strength of the present Sado Alliance stemmed from the Alliance Leader’s thorough recognition of reality.

“I will bear it in mind.”

You need not worry when I become Alliance Leader. War will not break out in our generation. The problem is this era now.

Because apart from his recognition of reality, Bi Sain knew that Baek Jagang, too, dreamed of uniting murim under one rule. He had never once spoken such words, but Bi Sain could sense it.

That with those tiny eyes, he wished to look down not merely upon the Sado Alliance, but upon all of murim. That he was enduring and enduring again for that one decisive blow.

“There is one thing I wish to ask.”

The method Bi Sain had chosen in dealing with this matter was a frontal breakthrough.

“Is there a secret organization being raised within the Alliance that I do not know of?”

“Why do you ask that?”

“I discovered that the Black Serpent Corps provided kidnapped children to the Alliance through Yugeommun. If it is not an organization authorized by the Alliance, then I believe someone is raising a private organization.”

If it was a serious matter, then it was serious indeed, but Baek Jagang showed no particular reaction. He merely stared at Bi Sain in silence.

“If that is the case, this matter cannot be overlooked.”

Bi Sain did not avoid the intense killing intent flowing from the Alliance Leader’s tiny eyes. There was no need to fear. He is the Alliance Leader, and I am his successor. He accepted as calmly as he could a gaze he would not have dared meet in the past.

“You have changed recently.”

Bi Sain did not hide his change. There were only two people in the world from whom he had no confidence in hiding his heart. Geom Mugeuk, and the Alliance Leader before him.

“This time, after leaving the Alliance, I met the Demonic Cult’s Young Cult Leader and the Commander of the Demon-Extermination Squad. They, too, were growing with each passing day. If I do not wish to lose, I must make an effort as well.”

Was he pleased by Bi Sain’s confident attitude? A broad smile spread across the Alliance Leader’s large mouth.

Baek Jagang’s gaze turned back toward the window. Looking at the gathering dark clouds, he finally answered the first question.

“I have never permitted such an organization.”

* * *

Swaaaaaaa!

Geom Mugeuk and Pyo Gigwang were hidden in the undergrowth, watching the rain pour down. They had been running frantically to evade the assassins and were taking a brief moment to catch their breath.

“Your skills are better than I thought.”

“Did I not tell you? I said I would protect you.”

He had thought the man’s confidence alone was astonishing, but it seemed it had not been mere confidence after all.

“I am the one who protects the Young Alliance Leader. I do not fall easily to assassins.”

At the word assassins, what Geom Mugeuk had said earlier surfaced anew in Pyo Gigwang’s mind.

—What kind of alliance in the world sends hired assassins after a subordinate it gave a task to? Where, exactly, is there any room for loyalty in this filthy situation?

Because he felt Pyo Gigwang’s heart wavering, Geom Mugeuk did not let this moment slip by.

“Did that person warn you that he would kill you if you failed to keep the secret?”

Pyo Gigwang slowly shook his head. That was what hurt him the most. He had been treated like dog shit rolling on the roadside.

“It may be presumptuous of me to say this, but I can guess why that person chose you, Corps Leader.”

“Why?”

“An ordinary person would have leapt up in rage over this, but you are accepting this attempt to kill and silence you as a matter of course, are you not? That man knew. He knew how a person sunk deep in despair would react in this situation.”

If that was why he had chosen him?

Pyo Gigwang’s face flushed hot. It was unbearably humiliating.

Just then, Geom Mugeuk made a shushing gesture and thrust his sword into the thick grass behind Pyo Gigwang.

With a single short scream, a corpse collapsed forward.

“An entire assassin organization has come. It is probably because they realized you made contact with the Young Alliance Leader. In other words, no matter how many guards the Young Alliance Leader attached to you, they are determined to eliminate you.”

In other words, that was how urgent the man’s situation was. The reason he had not come himself was likely to avoid exposing himself. No matter how thoroughly they crushed this assassin organization, no trace of him would emerge.

“Even with an entire organization mobilized, they have not managed to kill me while you alone are protecting me.”

“That is how it is. Let us go. We need to move.”

The two of them ran along the mountain path again.

More assassins attacked them. Pyo Gigwang did not know even in his dreams, but they were not fleeing. Geom Mugeuk was deliberately finding the assassins and eliminating them.

Trained solely to kill their targets, they knew no fear.

But no amount of courage could overcome a gap in skill as vast as heaven and earth. No ambush worked.

Geom Mugeuk’s methods in dealing with the assassins were so ordinary that, to Pyo Gigwang’s eyes, the assassins’ level seemed rather low instead.

It looked like such a light swing, so why can’t they dodge it? An assassin allows a simple kick like that to land and twist his jaw?

But whenever they attacked him, their terrifying momentum always called forth the same cry.

This time, I’m really dead!

He did not know how many times he had shouted those words in his heart, but they burst forth again.

Thuk!

A sword that had burst out was before Pyo Gigwang’s eyes. Blood dripped from its tip.

The sword did not belong to the assassin. It was Geom Mugeuk’s sword, having pierced through the back of his head.

It looked as though he had hurriedly thrown his sword and killed him, but that single move had displayed flying sword technique superior to any method.

“Are you all right?”

“I am fine.”

“It seems we have dealt with all the assassins now.”

Pyo Gigwang was startled. If told to run, he ran; if told to duck, he ducked; if pulled, he was dragged along; if pushed, he fell over. And before he knew it, the fight was over.

It’s over? Then I survived?

With a face that could not quite believe it, Pyo Gigwang looked at Geom Mugeuk.

Swaaaaaaa!

Geom Mugeuk was washing the blood from his face and body in the falling rain.

Suddenly, that figure felt unfamiliar. He was not the youngest martial artist who had listened to him, enthusiastically chiming in with his words.

The sight of him calmly washing away blood felt like that of a true martial artist who had passed through mountains of blades and forests of swords, experiencing every hardship under heaven. Was that why? Somehow, he looked lonely.

“Let us go.”

“Where?”

Where were they supposed to go now?

But Geom Mugeuk knew exactly where they had to go.

“To that bastard. We should go and demand answers. Ask why he handled things in such a shitty way. If he wanted to finish it, he should have come and finished it himself, so why did he send mere assassins? You should demand to know why he looked down on you so much.”

Geom Mugeuk was saying in Pyo Gigwang’s place the very words Pyo Gigwang wanted to say.

“I believe this matter was not authorized by the Alliance.”

For an instant, Pyo Gigwang flinched, but then he shook his head.

“That cannot be.”

Now that the time had come, Geom Mugeuk offered the decisive words he had been saving.

“If it were something the Leader of the Sado Alliance had authorized, he would never have sent hired assassins. I heard once before that the people the Leader of the Sado Alliance hates most are hired assassins.”

“!”

In that instant, Pyo Gigwang recalled a moment from the past.

When he had been the Black Dragon Corps Leader, he had spoken with his subordinates about this very subject. That the Alliance Leader truly hated assassins, and that in the present Sado Alliance, no one from an assassin background ever rose to prominence. In fact, he had been the one to say it.

Goosebumps rose all over Pyo Gigwang’s body. It was something he had forgotten. After being injured, stepping down from the corps, and living like a ruined man, he had forgotten it all.

If that person were someone carrying out the Alliance Leader’s will, he would not have mobilized assassins, would he?

With that powerful doubt, the conversation he had shared with that man naturally came back to him.

—Is this operation something the Alliance Leader knows about as well?

—Of course. That is precisely why absolute secrecy must be maintained.

At the time, he had naturally thought the Alliance Leader knew. That must be why this person had stepped forward. That was why he had thought even the Young Alliance Leader should not intervene. It was a secret operation that had not even been disclosed to the Young Alliance Leader.

Why did I never doubt it even once?

He knew the answer to the question that surfaced in his heart.

Because all he had wanted was to escape that situation. To escape it and boast to those who had looked down on him, “I’m doing something this important, you fools!”

“If this matter was carried out without the Alliance Leader’s knowledge, then it is treason.”

Pyo Gigwang’s heart sank. If those words were true, it meant that bastard had dragged him into treason. Pyo Gigwang was utterly confused.

“Let us confirm together what the truth is.”

Until yesterday, Pyo Gigwang had believed the truth was something only he knew. No words would have gotten through to him. But now, he was asking this.

“Can we do it?”

“You were prepared for death anyway, were you not?”

Yes, that was right. He did not know how many times today he had closed his eyes thinking he was about to die. He would close them one more time.

Looking at Geom Mugeuk, Pyo Gigwang nodded.

Now the situation had changed.

“From now on, we are not fish. Become a fisherman and cast the net with me. Then let us hear what that fish, who has been acting like a fisherman all this time, has to say for himself.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 350

Chapter 350: I Know a Thing or Two About Being Killed to Silence Me
-------------------------------------------------------------------
Pyo Gigwang realized that the moment had come to reveal the name.

At last, the name of the one behind it came from his mouth.

“The man who came to me was Hwang Seokgyeong.”

“Who is Hwang Seokgyeong?”

“An instructor at Samaenggwan.”

It was someone truly unexpected.

When one entered the Sado Alliance, one would spend some time in a place called Samaenggwan, receiving the basic training required of an alliance member and being assigned to an organization.

There, a proper examination was held, and naturally, the more skilled one was, the more elite the organization one entered. Hwang Seokgyeong was an instructor there.

It was truly unforeseen. Geom Mugeuk had thought the mastermind would be at least someone of unit-leader level, a pavilion master, or perhaps an elder. But an instructor who tested and assigned martial artists?

That made him feel all the more dangerous.

“You believed the word of a mere instructor?”

“He is no mere instructor. His skills are excellent as well, and there is no one who does not know Instructor Hwang. That is why I believed him.”

In truth, it was because he was an instructor at Samaenggwan that Pyo Gigwang had trusted him all the more. Somehow, he had thought an instructor from Samaenggwan suited the task of creating a secret organization. And since he was an instructor at Samaenggwan, Pyo Gigwang had naturally believed that he had received the alliance’s permission.

With a relieved heart, Pyo Gigwang looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“Now that you have learned the name, get yourself out of the net. Go and tell the Young Alliance Leader who the mastermind is. And tell him that I will gladly accept my punishment.”

If his crime of participating in treason was acknowledged, he would have to spend the rest of his life in the prison.

Pyo Gigwang looked up at the sky. The rain that had been pouring down fiercely was letting up.

‘So it’s all over now.’

He felt refreshed, yet for some reason, anger rose within him. A person’s life should be lived by that person himself, and yet he had been toyed with by someone else.

Even so, he could not take revenge himself. He could not beat Instructor Hwang to death with his own hands.

Then Geom Mugeuk said something unexpected.

“He is probably nothing more than a subordinate as well.”

“Nothing more than a subordinate?”

“The Lord of Yugeom Gate said so. That he had received benefits from the alliance. Instructor Hwang may be skilled and famous, but he is not someone with enough authority to secure benefits for Yugeom Gate.”

Now that he heard it, it was true. How did this young martial artist grasp, at just the right moment, the very things he himself had failed to think of?

“We have to find out who is behind him through him.”

He had lived thinking that knowing Instructor Hwang’s existence was some tremendous secret, but that man too was merely a disposable piece moving under someone else’s orders?

If that were the case, once it became known that Instructor Hwang’s identity had been exposed, would assassins go after him as well?

“There is another problem.”

Geom Mugeuk confirmed the point he had been overlooking.

“Do you have any evidence that Instructor Hwang entrusted the task to you, Unit Leader? A secret letter written in his own hand, perhaps, or an order he gave in front of witnesses?”

Pyo Gigwang shook his head. How could there possibly be such a thing?

“Then Instructor Hwang will flatly deny that he ever ordered any such thing.”

It seemed likely. He had even sent assassins to kill him. Having already cast aside all regard for their relationship, what conscience would make him speak honestly?

On the other hand, Pyo Gigwang was a man who had lived like a ruined shell. Would they believe Instructor Hwang? Or would they believe him? The result was obvious without even going through it.

“Then in the end, we can’t catch him?”

“No, we must catch him.”

“How?”

“We have to make him say from his own mouth that he ordered this. And find out who is behind him as well.”

“So how?”

Geom Mugeuk seemed to ponder for a moment.

“If he is dragged before the Young Alliance Leader, he will never open his mouth.”

That would be the case. If he had fostered a private organization without the alliance’s permission, he would be executed by beheading. There was no reason for him to speak.

Geom Mugeuk silently looked at Pyo Gigwang.

“Don’t tell me… you’re saying the two of us should meet him?”

“No.”

A startling statement flowed from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“You must meet him alone, Unit Leader.”

Pyo Gigwang was shocked.

“If you do not meet him alone, he will never confess the truth.”

“He will try to kill me.”

“I will hide nearby and protect you.”

Pyo Gigwang had not expected Geom Mugeuk to make such a proposal. Truly, this young martial artist had never once moved according to his expectations.

“So you mean you will avoid Instructor Hwang’s eyes, hide, and when he tries to kill me, appear and save me?”

“That is correct. You must make him say that he ordered this. It would be even better if he reveals the mastermind too.”

Pyo Gigwang grew worried inwardly. To avoid being detected by Instructor Hwang, Geom Mugeuk would have to stay quite far away. If the man suddenly tried to kill him, would he be able to protect him?

Then, soon after, this thought came to him. Yes, so what if Instructor Hwang kills me? I was a life that should have died today anyway.

Yes, he had gained a chance to confront that bastard. Somehow, he would make him confess and pay for his crimes. He would not go alone.

Pyo Gigwang gazed at Geom Mugeuk for a moment.

When they had first met yesterday, he had never imagined that he would reveal the mastermind to him. He had not known that this man would save his life several times over.

And more than anything else, he was grateful that Geom Mugeuk had listened to his story.

“Very well. Let us do that.”

As if the heavens knew his resolve, the falling rain stopped, and before long, sunlight was shining through.

“Now, let us go.”

As he strode forward, Geom Mugeuk spoke to him.

“The way down is this way.”

Pyo Gigwang changed direction and walked back toward him.

At his awkward expression, Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly.

‘Do not smile so brightly. It makes me afraid to die.’

* * *

Hwang Seokgyeong was walking over from afar.

With a trembling heart, Pyo Gigwang waited for him in front of his residence. This was the first time he had come to see him first like this. He would surely be startled to see him.

Whoosh.

Just as he thought he heard the sound of wind, he was up in a tree. Someone had snatched him up and flown into the air. In that instant, even his mute acupoint had been sealed.

The one who had subdued him and brought him up into the tree was Geom Mugeuk.

He was making a shushing gesture.

‘What on earth is he doing?’

Until they had come here, he had been full of confidence. He had told Pyo Gigwang not to worry, that he would protect him no matter what.

‘But why all of a sudden?’

Soon after, Hwang Seokgyeong flew up to that place.

There was no one in the tree where the two had been. After Hwang Seokgyeong leaped back down, he walked toward his residence.

Geom Mugeuk and Pyo Gigwang were already far away from that place.

Only after they had put sufficient distance between themselves and Hwang Seokgyeong’s residence did Geom Mugeuk release Pyo Gigwang’s mute acupoint.

“What on earth is this?”

Why else? Because it was a face he knew.

Geom Mugeuk had seen Hwang Seokgyeong walking over from afar with the Divine Eye Art. And he had recognized who Hwang Seokgyeong was. He was no mere subordinate.

The Third King among the Twelve Zodiac Kings.

The Fighting King, Cheonya.

In the days when the Twelve Zodiac Kings ruled the martial world, the Fighting King was called the god of battle. He loved fighting and hated losing. Countless experts had lost their lives at his hands.

He was a man who had staked his life on battle. It was said that his martial prowess ranked within the top three among the Twelve Zodiac Kings. Some said he was first, others said third. There was even a rumor that his dream was to defeat Hwa Mugi.

That Fighting King was living in the Sado Alliance under the name Hwang Seokgyeong, as a mere instructor. A conspiracy was being carried out centered around him.

“He is not a fish that can be caught with a net.”

Pyo Gigwang’s attention was no longer on Hwang Seokgyeong.

“You are not one of the Thirteen Young Masters of the Sado Alliance, are you?”

Geom Mugeuk gave a faint smile.

“I have become more curious about who you are than who that man is.”

“If I reveal my identity, you will be killed to silence you. Are you all right with that?”

“Do not worry. I have some expertise in being killed to silence me.”

After exchanging a round of banter, Geom Mugeuk revealed his true identity.

“I am Geom Mugeuk, Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.”

For a moment, Pyo Gigwang froze. Normally, he should have said, “Nonsense, that’s impossible,” and refused to believe it.

“You truly are the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.”

He could simply tell. That this man was the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult. All the special qualities he had shown until now were explained by that single introduction. As was the reason why he himself was still alive.

Then came the shock that followed.

The person who had listened so well to his words was the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult? The person who had saved him was the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult?

“Why is the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult here?”

His voice trembled violently.

“I am handling this matter together with your Young Alliance Leader.”

Even after revealing his identity, Geom Mugeuk still treated him with courtesy.

“What will happen to me now?”

Was he perhaps going to be dragged to the Demonic Cult? Had it not been a joke when he said that anyone who learned his identity would be killed to silence them?

“What do you wish would happen?”

He had only thought of dying, never once about the life that would come afterward.

“Think about it now.”

At that, the first thing that came to Pyo Gigwang’s mind was this.

“I want to live a new life.”

Leaving behind everything that had happened until now, he wanted to live a new life.

“But my sins are too great…”

After all, he had helped those who had rebelled against the alliance.

Geom Mugeuk stared at him, then said something incomprehensible.

“By any chance, are you good at massaging shoulders?”

* * *

“Aaaaaaah!”

After screaming while being carried on Geom Mugeuk’s back, he found that they had arrived somewhere before he knew it.

It was Geom Mugeuk’s Swift Steps, which had surpassed the great completion of the tenth stage and now reached the eleventh. He ran so fast that not only could Pyo Gigwang not make out the surrounding scenery, the feeling of tension was like continuously falling from a cliff.

‘This is a dream.’

That was truly the only thought he had. No matter how much of an expert someone was, they could not run this fast. And while carrying a person on their back, no less.

There was one thing he did not know. Even while running, Geom Mugeuk was releasing internal energy to ensure that the person on his back could remain comfortable. If not, an ordinary person would not have been able to endure this speed.

The place they arrived at was a manor.

The name written on the signboard was Geungnagwon. Not the won for manor, but the won used for wish.

This was the very place he had come to before, when Seo Daeryong had been injured. Here, Geom Mugeuk had become immune to all poisons.

When they entered the courtyard, an old woman walked out from within the building and asked,

“Who have you carried here this time?”

It was Chungui, the owner of this place. Her arm was still wrapped in bandages, and as if her shoulder ached, she was massaging it with her hand.

“Doctor! I missed you.”

Geom Mugeuk ran over and swept her up in his arms.

“What indecent nonsense is this?”

“What do you mean? I’m showing how glad I am.”

He was truly glad to see her after so long.

“Your skills have improved even more while I haven’t seen you.”

Well-versed in martial arts herself, she recognized Geom Mugeuk’s growth at a glance.

“It is all thanks to my dear elder sister.”

At his jest of calling her elder sister, she shook her head with an expression of disbelief, but she was very happy that Geom Mugeuk had come to see her after so long.

Though she did not show it outwardly, her delight was even greater than Geom Mugeuk’s.

“Who is that gloomy sentient being?”

It seemed she could see Pyo Gigwang’s life just by looking at his face.

Geom Mugeuk introduced Pyo Gigwang.

“This is Pyo Gigwang, who once served as leader of the Black Dragon Unit in the Sado Alliance. Now, offer your greetings. This beautiful lady is Chungui.”

Pyo Gigwang knew of Chungui as well.

“If you are Chungui… are you not the Divine Physician who is said to handle insects well? I heard that your medical arts are so outstanding that you cure all kinds of illnesses using insects, and can even bring the dead back to life.”

He had thought she was merely a figure from rumors, so he could only be astonished that Chungui actually existed. But why had Geom Mugeuk brought him here?

“You must have heard other rumors too, no?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Pyo Gigwang glanced at Chungui and could not bring himself to speak.

The one who answered in his stead was Chungui herself.

“That I’m so obsessed with money that they call me a money grubber?”

Geom Mugeuk told him the truth.

“With that money, she supported abandoned children. She sent them food, sent them to schools, and sent them to martial academies. She prepared a way for them to live and sent them out into the world. She is someone who has lived her whole life for orphans.”

“I had no idea you were such an admirable person.”

Pyo Gigwang respectfully cupped his fist and paid his respects.

“But why did you bring me here?”

“You said you were good at massaging shoulders, didn’t you? Elder Chungui’s arms and legs ache all the time, so she needs someone to massage her shoulders.”

It meant that he was to become Chungui’s disciple and live a new life here.

“Try me out for a probationary period, and if I don’t seem suitable, feed me to the worms.”

“You didn’t bring me a young one?”

“Would young people these days ever stick around in a backwater like this? And he is younger than he looks.”

Pyo Gigwang could not bring himself to protest, What’s wrong with my face! After all, he had been told more than once in his life that he looked older than his age.

Chungui sat down on a rock.

“Come here and rub my shoulders.”

Still dazed, Pyo Gigwang went over and began massaging her shoulders. Beneath his hands, he felt bones so small and frail they were almost pitiful.

“Oh, that feels good,” Chungui said, then spoke to Geom Mugeuk.

“You haven’t forgotten me.”

“How could I forget you, Physician?”

She felt a swelling rush of emotion. She had thought it was merely a passing connection.

But Geom Mugeuk had not forgotten and had come to find her. He had even brought her a disciple. These days, being forgotten had somehow felt sorrowful.

“I think I should be going.”

“Eat before you leave.”

“My opponent this time won’t be easy. Perhaps this time, I may be the one carried back on someone’s back.”

Their gazes met in midair. Don’t get hurt. Chungui’s worry was conveyed through her eyes.

“If someone of your caliber says that, then your opponent is already on the road to the afterlife.”

“As expected! You alone recognize my true worth, Divine Physician!”

As the two conversed, Pyo Gigwang let their words pass by him and sank into his own thoughts.

To confess the mastermind behind everything overnight, and then suddenly become Chungui’s disciple. Was it all right for a life to change this abruptly?

Then, all at once, his eyes met Geom Mugeuk’s, who stood before him. With clear, deep eyes, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Whether it is the Demonic Path or the Evil Path, children, at least, should be protected.”

The instant he heard those words, Pyo Gigwang understood. Why Geom Mugeuk had brought him here. It had not been for Chungui’s sake, but for his own.

Chungui was someone who had spent her entire life for orphans.

By contrast, he was someone who had been in charge of sending the children kidnapped by the Black Snake Corps to the Evil Alliance. It was not a crime he had committed with his own hands, but he could not say he was without guilt.

That was why Geom Mugeuk had brought him here. To serve Chungui and repay that sin. To live a second life in this place.

To him, who should have spent the rest of his days in a dark, deep dungeon, Geom Mugeuk had given a new life. If not for him, it was a life that should already have ended.

Regret and gratitude mingled together. Emotions too complex to put into words stormed through his heart. One result born from those emotions fell downward.

Drop.

A tear had fallen. Pyo Gigwang was flustered, for he had never shed tears in his life. Even when he had been told that he was injured and could no longer use martial arts, he had not cried. Even facing death, he had not cried.

And yet now, tears came. Who am I, that he would give me a chance like this? What am I, a fool who was only used by others?

Startled, he wiped away his tears with his sleeve, but Geom Mugeuk, who had been standing before him, had already become a dot at the edge of the sky. He had vanished before Pyo Gigwang could even tell him thank you.

At that final moment, the words Geom Mugeuk had spoken when they first met came to mind.

When our meeting is over, I will disappear like smoke.

‘Thank you, Young Cult Leader.’

The place where the single tear he had shed landed was the nape of Chungui’s neck.

Feeling that heat, Chungui shouted loudly,

“You brat, if you don’t want to become worm feed, rub harder!”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 351
